## 2026-09-18 — SPEC-6-04 (Section 6) — builder: role-aware ModelAdmin least privilege + common/* folded into the measured set

Brings the Django admin surface under spec 6.12's least-privilege enforcement ([6.12.4] confirmation for sensitive actions, [6.12.5] privileged-action log trail via Django's own admin LogEntry, [6.12.7] no blanket staff access). Previously the admin's four model permissions checked Django model perms no staff role carries, so in practice only the superuser could work the admin; now each of the six registered ModelAdmins routes view/add/change/delete through `CAPABILITY_ROLES` (the `admin` role — which the map grants every capability — keeps full function, pinned by test).
- `common/admin.py` — new `RoleAwareModelAdmin` base: `capability_map` (permission kind → capability id; `None` = no role may, superuser bypass only), `action_capabilities` (bulk/export actions gated through the same `get_actions` seam Django uses for `allowed_permissions`, so a gated action vanishes from the dropdown *and* is an invalid choice if POSTed directly), `confirmation_required_actions` (destructive bulk actions interpose an explicit-confirmation interstitial — [6.12.4] — that re-POSTs with `confirm=yes` and the preserved selection before executing), `log_bulk_action` (one LogEntry per affected row — [6.12.5], since queryset bulk actions bypass `save_model` and would otherwise mutate rows unlogged), `has_module_permission` follows the map (the default would hide everything from role users), and Django's own superuser bypass is preserved verbatim as the only user-flag shortcut.
- `products/admin.py` — ProductAdmin: view `products.read`, add/change `products.write`, delete `products.publish` (hard delete ≥ unpublish in sensitivity, so a future publish narrowing narrows delete too); `adjust_stock` gated to `inventory.adjust` (its existing delta/reason input form *is* the explicit confirmation) and logs each adjusted row, `export_csv` to `products.read`.
- `orders/admin.py` — OrderAdmin: view `orders.read`, change `orders.fulfill`, add/delete capability-less (orders originate from checkout; hard delete would bypass the legal status flow — the status/cancel actions are the sanctioned paths); all five bulk actions capability-gated (`fulfill` ×3, `cancel` ×1, `read` for export), `cancel_pending` — the one destructive bulk action, an irreversible status change on a financial record — now requires explicit confirmation, and every mutating bulk action (status changes, cancel) writes a LogEntry naming the actor. CouponAdmin: all four kinds ride `discounts.write` (the map has no discounts.read split; promotions are marketing's domain).
- `ops/admin.py` — SiteSettingsAdmin: view/change ride `settings.manage` (admin only, per the [6.12] identifier table); the pre-existing unconditional singleton add/delete denials are kept and outrank even the superuser bypass.
- `cart/admin.py` — CartAdmin: view `customers.read`, mutations capability-less (carts are customer-owned session state; admin row edits would bypass the cart API's quantity/stock checks). Flagged as the one ModelAdmin the capability map couldn't express unambiguously — resolution: read-only for all staff, superuser bypass preserved.
- `accounts/admin.py` — StoreUserAdmin becomes `RoleAwareModelAdmin, DjangoUserAdmin` (base first, so the capability-driven methods win the MRO): view `customers.read`, add/change/delete `staff.manage` — the User row holds `is_staff` and the role groups, so touching it is privilege management (the [6.12] privilege-escalation guard carried to the admin).
- `templates/admin/action_confirmation.html` — the generic interstitial: lists affected objects, re-POSTs `action`/`confirm=yes`/`_selected_action` (or `select_across`) with the cancel link back to `{% url 'admin:index' %}`; adjust-stock's own deliberate form stays off the mechanism by design.
- `tests/test_admin_rbac.py` — 22 new tests: pinned registry equality (`admin.site._registry` == the six store admins, so a future ModelAdmin forgetting the base fails loudly); capability identifiers all drawn from the roles map; the "every declared action is capability-gated" drift invariant; the full view/add/change/delete × role × admin matrix derived from `CAPABILITY_ROLES` (plus role-less staff denied everywhere, admin-role full function, superuser bypass covering even capability-less kinds minus the SiteSettings singleton denials); per-role `get_actions` availability incl. `delete_selected` needing the delete capability (orders: nobody but superuser — matching `delete: None`); e2e through the real UI: view-only finance reads the order change page but gets 403 on save, marketing gets 403 on the orders changelist, `cancel_pending` renders the interstitial and mutates nothing until confirmed, superuser keeps direct execution, a directly POSTed gated action is inert (302 bounce, row untouched), and both gated bulk mutations (mark_shipped, confirmed cancel) leave a LogEntry naming the acting role user.
- `.coveragerc` — per the combined-audit follow-up, `common/*` folds into the measured set (was omitted): 1182 → 1406 stmts. Exactly one gap was exposed — `common/testing.py:135`, the `auth(token)` bearer-attach branch, only its `None` path was ever exercised — closed by a new test logging in via the API, clearing the credentials, re-attaching the raw token with `auth()` and proving 401 → 200 on an `IsAuthenticated` route.
- Suite: **307 tests, OK (301 pass, 6 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1406 stmts — larger measured set, gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-18 — SPEC-6-05a (Section 6) — builder: staff + roles management surfaces on the admin

Implements [6.12.1] on the User admin: the six staff role Groups are now assignable/unassignable on staff users from the change page, gated by `staff.manage` (admin role) with Django's superuser bypass preserved, and every Group add/remove is audited with its own admin LogEntry naming the acting user and the role (spec 6.12: log privileged actions, protect role changes from unauthorized privilege escalation — line 2261).
- `accounts/admin.py` — StoreUserChangeForm (UserChangeForm + a `staff_roles` ModelMultipleChoiceField) replaces the raw `groups` M2M in the fieldsets: a plain Groups selector would let even an admin grant unmapped, non-role authority, so the surface exposes exactly the six role groups (queryset pinned to `STAFF_ROLES`, seeded with the user's current membership); `get_fieldsets` strips the roles section for anyone without `staff.manage` and for non-staff targets (least privilege — hidden, rebuilt per request, class-level fieldsets never mutated); `clean()` refuses roles on non-staff accounts (a non-staff user holding a role group is an escalation channel — they'd gain API authority while unable to ever reach the admin again); `save_model` re-checks the capability before any role-bearing write (defense in depth for callers that bypass the view flow, which is already 403-gated by `has_change_permission`), and `_apply_staff_roles` diffs only the six role groups (unrelated group memberships survive) logging `Added role "x".` / `Removed role "x".` per change via `log_change`.
- `tests/test_staff_roles_admin.py` — 12 new tests: roles field lists exactly the six roles, seeded with current membership (admin viewer); support-role viewer gets the roles section hidden (least privilege on the read-only page that rides `customers.read`); admin assigns two roles in one save → both groups land and two LogEntries, one per add, each naming the acting admin; role removal logged the same way; a support user POSTing their OWN change form with the admin role gets 403 (self-escalation denial — no group, no capability, no LogEntry); the same attack against another staff user is refused; a customer's change page has no roles field and a tampered POST carrying `staff_roles` is refused by the form guard (nothing written, no role LogEntry); demoting a staff user while selecting a role is rejected (re-render, nothing written); the superuser bypass still assigns roles and logs; the add view still creates role-free accounts (UserCreationForm path, no injectable roles); direct-call pins — `save_model` raises `PermissionDenied` for a staff.manage-less caller carrying role data, and `_apply_staff_roles` leaves non-role groups untouched.
- Suite: **319 tests, OK (313 pass, 6 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1449 stmts, gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-19 — SPEC-6-05b (Section 6) — builder: privileged-action logging for API ops, audit-log route, User-form privilege-escalation guard

Per spec 6.12 [6.12.6] ("Log privileged actions", route `/admin/audit-log`) and the line-2261 guard ("Protect role and permission changes from unauthorized privilege escalation"), on top of SPEC-6-05a (LogEntry as the record source per the SPEC-6-05 split decision; the business-event audit model stays with SPEC-7-01):
- `common/audit.py` (new) — `log_api_action(request, obj, action_flag, change_message)`: the single reusable helper emitting a `django.contrib.admin.models.LogEntry` for API-side privileged writes (via `LogEntry.objects.log_actions`), so the admin chrome and the DRF API feed one audit source. DELETION contract: log before the delete — Django's collector clears the instance pk afterwards (the admin's own `log_deletion` does the same).
- `common/permissions.py` — `capability_required(capability)`: plain-Django twin of `CapabilityPermission` for admin-chrome routes that `permission_classes` cannot reach; same `CAPABILITY_ROLES` authority, Django's superuser bypass preserved (mirroring `RoleAwareModelAdmin`), anonymous → admin-login redirect, authenticated-unprivileged → 403.
- `products/views.py` — the capability-gated DRF writes (the only staff-privileged API mutations today: product create/PUT/PATCH/DELETE under `products.write`) now emit a LogEntry ("Created/Updated/Deleted via API.") naming the acting staff user, each inside one `transaction.atomic()` with the write so the trail can never lag the row; refused or invalid attempts write nothing and log nothing.
- `ops/views.py` + `ops/templates/ops/audit_log.html` (new) + `config/urls.py` — the spec's `/admin/audit-log` route: newest-first paginated LogEntry table (when/actor/action+model/object/details, human `get_change_message` rendering — never raw JSON — links for non-deleted objects), gated by `staff.manage` (admin role; superuser bypass preserved). File placement follows the existing admin-chrome route precedent (`admin/dashboard/`, registered before the admin include).
- `accounts/admin.py` — the flagged SPEC-6-05a hole closed: `get_readonly_fields` makes `is_staff`/`is_superuser`/`user_permissions` read-only for any non-superuser editor (admin role included) on the change page — the flags are excluded from the ModelForm, so a crafted POST is ignored, not validated away. Role assignment (`staff.manage`) is unaffected and the superuser trust anchor keeps full flag authority. Judgment call: `user_permissions` rides the same guard as line 2261's "permission changes" channel (model perms are inert under the roles map, so the restriction costs nothing).
- `tests/test_audit_log.py` (new, 10 tests) — per-flag logging with actor/object identity pins (ADDITION, CHANGE ×2 via PATCH+PUT, DELETION incl. record-outlives-row), denied/invalid attempts log nothing; route matrix: anonymous → admin-login redirect, all five non-admin roles 403, admin role 200 reading both API and admin-chrome entries, superuser bypass + empty state, JSON change messages render human, 55-entry pagination across two pages.
- `tests/test_staff_roles_admin.py` — +4 guard tests (admin-role editor: both flags absent from the form and a crafted dual-flag POST changes nothing while the sanctioned role save still lands; a customer cannot be promoted to staff; a `user_permissions` grant is ignored; superuser contrast still grants). One 05a test re-pointed: `test_demoting_to_non_staff_with_roles_selected_is_refused` now runs as a superuser editor — the demotion vector (editable `is_staff` combined with roles in one save) legitimately exists only where the flag is editable; the pinned guard is unchanged and still asserted there.
- Suite: **333 tests, OK (327 pass, 6 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1495 stmts, up from 1449; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-19 — SPEC-7-01 (Section 7) — builder: append-only business-event audit trail

Per spec 7.1 Audit module ([R-7.20] "Immutable or append-only business activity records") and the section-1 gap [1.22] (no audit trail for orders/payments/auth beyond StockMovement): a cross-domain `AuditEvent` ledger — complementary to SPEC-6-05b's privileged-action LogEntry trail, which is untouched — recording order lifecycle (created, paid), payment intent + verify outcomes (verified plus every failure branch: signature rejected, order not found, already processed, reference mismatch, stock conflict, coupon invalid), and authentication events (registered, login success/failure, email verified, password reset). Per-transition from→to status events stay with section 10 by design.
- `common/models.py` (new) — `AuditEvent`: category (order/payment/auth, derived from the event-type's dotted prefix so an unknown identifier fails loudly instead of writing an unqueryable row), event_type, actor + order FKs (both `SET_NULL` so events outlive the rows they describe, with identifying values preserved in the JSON `detail` payload), created_at. `AuditEvent.record()` is the single sanctioned write path, mirroring StockMovement's actor convention (anonymous/system → NULL); `save()` refuses updates and `delete()` refuses removals — append-only is enforced at the model level and pinned by tests. Placement: `common/` already hosts the audit twin (`common/audit.py`) and RBAC; an orders home would force accounts → orders imports for auth events.
- `common/migrations/0002_auditevent.py` (generated, unedited) — create table with indexes on category/event_type.
- `orders/views.py` — hooks in the three side-effectful order/payment flows, each inside the SAME `transaction.atomic()` as the effect it records (the StockMovement pattern): `create_order` (order.created), `create_payment` (payment.initiated on first intent persistence, wrapped in a new atomic with the order.save; the idempotent reuse path emits nothing), `verify_payment` (signature rejection before the block — a single atomic insert on its own; order-not-found, already-processed, reference-mismatch, stock-conflict, coupon-invalid inside the existing block; payment.verified + order.paid in the success tail).
- `accounts/views.py` — `LoginView.post` override records auth.login (200) and auth.login_failed (400/401 — super().post signals every rejection by raising) with the attempted username in detail and the actor resolved by the same exact-username match `authenticate()` used; 429 throttled refusals are raised before the view runs and write nothing. `register` (auth.registered), `verify_email` (auth.email_verified only when activation actually happens — a re-verify of an active account is a no-op), `reset_password` (auth.password_reset; the one-time token deliberately not stored) each wrap the state change + trail row in one `transaction.atomic()`.
- `tests/test_audit_trail.py` (new, 23 tests) — model contract (category derivation, unknown-prefix rejection, anonymous actor → NULL, update/delete guards, events outlive a deleted user+order via SET_NULL); endpoint-level trail assertions for every event type through the real flows; rollback-together pins: an injected audit-write failure inside checkout and inside verify_payment aborts the order/stock/coupon/cart effects with it (500, no new trail rows, order stays pending, stock intact); 429 refusals write nothing (rate pinned on the throttle class directly, since DRF snapshots `DEFAULT_THROTTLE_RATES` at import so `override_settings` cannot reach it).
- No reader surface (endpoint/admin) in this task — deliberate: SPEC-6-05b's `/admin/audit-log/` route is the LogEntry reader and stays untouched; surfacing AuditEvent can ride a later section with its own RBAC gate decision. No new env keys (no thresholds/keys introduced).
- Suite: **356 tests, OK (350 pass, 6 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1573 stmts, up from 1495; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-19 — SPEC-7-02 (Section 7) — builder: env-driven LOGGING baseline wired to the audit trail and payment failure paths

Per the section-7 observability item plus the logging halves of the section-1 gap [1.22] (no LOGGING config) and section-2 gap [2.12] (no monitoring — only the logging baseline lands here; sentry/metrics/alerts stay deferred to S22 per the ledger): an env-driven dictConfig baseline with request 5xx and payment failures visible at minimum, wired so audit events and every verify_payment failure branch actually emit log records. No AuditEvent semantics change: same insert, same transaction placement, same append-only guards.
- `config/settings.py` — `LOGGING` built by `_build_logging(app_level, file_path)`: console always wired (12-factor stdout; read-only-filesystem-safe, no hardcoded path), a rotating file handler (5 MB × 3) joins only when `LOG_FILE` is set; brace-style formatter (detail payloads render dicts, so %-style `%%` escaping would collide); `disable_existing_loggers=False`. `django.request` pinned at ERROR and the audit channel `common.audit` pinned at INFO — both level-only entries, so each record reaches the root handlers exactly once and stays visible even when `LOG_LEVEL` is raised. `_env_log_level` resolves `LOG_LEVEL` fail-safely (the `_env_int` pattern): unknown/`NOTSET` values fall back to INFO with a warning instead of crashing startup.
- `.env.example` — `LOG_LEVEL=INFO` (default) and optional `LOG_FILE` documented, including the read-only-FS rationale.
- `common/models.py` — `AuditEvent.record` now emits one INFO line (`audit <type> id=<pk> actor=<username> order=<pk> detail=<payload>`) on the dedicated `common.audit` channel after the insert, so the trail is observable in logs without a reader surface; the call writes no rows, so the rollback-together semantics from SPEC-7-01 are untouched.
- `orders/views.py` — module logger plus: a `logger.exception("Payment intent creation failed for order …")` wrap around the Razorpay intent call in `create_payment` (log-and-reraise — the 500 semantics are preserved exactly, only the missing order reference is added), and one logger call per AuditEvent-emitting verify_payment failure branch with the order reference and reason: signature rejected (WARNING), order not found (WARNING), already processed (INFO — benign double-submit), reference mismatch (WARNING), stock conflict (INFO — lost race), coupon invalid (INFO — lost race). No permission, throttle, or response changes.
- `tests/test_logging_baseline.py` (new, 21 tests) — env-driving pins (`LOG_LEVEL` case-insensitive, unknown/`NOTSET` → fallback + warning, missing → default); config structure pins (console always, root level tracks the resolved setting, django.request/`common.audit` pins, brace formatter); file handler only with an explicit path; `AuditEvent.record` INFO emission (stored identity incl. anonymous → `None`); all six verify failure branches + the gateway-failure 500 emit records via `assertLogs`; a no-secrets pin (Razorpay key id/secret absent from the captured output while both introduced surfaces are present).
- Suite: **377 tests, OK (371 pass, 6 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1607 stmts, up from 1573; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-19 — SPEC-7-03 (Section 7) — builder: coupon discount quantize parity ([R-7.7], F-11 flip)

Per the section-7 money rule (exact decimal money, never float) and conventions.md's "always quantize before comparing/serializing": percentage coupon discounts are quantized to the 2-dp money precision in BOTH coupon-affected paths, so preview display, the audit trail and the stored order amount always agree to the paisa. Flips the pinned `expectedFailure` F-11 — expected-failure count drops 6 → 5.
- `common/money.py` (new) — `quantize_money()`: quantize to `0.01` with `ROUND_HALF_EVEN`, the rounding the DB adapter applies when a `DecimalField` value is written, so an in-memory value and the stored value it becomes are the same amount. Placement in `common/` (the shared-kernel app, like audit/permissions/roles): the money rule is cross-app — orders computes discounts today, pricing/reporting consumers would tomorrow; `ops/services.py` already uses the same inline quantize idiom and is deliberately untouched (no drive-by).
- `orders/views.py` — `create_order` (checkout) and `apply_coupon` (public preview) quantize the percentage discount immediately after the `/100` division, before the `maximum_discount` cap comparison, the subtotal clamp and any storage/serialization. Fixed-type discounts needed no change (the value comes straight off a `decimal_places=2` field); `verify_payment`, the cart endpoints and the admin CSV read DB-stored 2-dp values and do no discount math. Quantize is identity at 2-dp grid points, so clean amounts are unchanged; the audit trail now records `'total_amount': '2099.99'` where it previously recorded `'2099.9930'` (same value, money precision).
- `orders/tests.py` — pin flip only: `@unittest.expectedFailure` removed from `test_f11_rounding_parity_between_preview_and_checkout` per its own docstring instruction once the fix landed; the `assertEqual(Decimal(preview.data["discount"]), order.discount_amount)` assertion is byte-identical and now passes for real (30% of 2999.99 = 899.997 raw → 900.00 on both paths). The orphaned `import unittest` (sole use was the removed marker) dropped; `from unittest import mock` stays.
- No model changes, no new env keys — `makemigrations --check` clean.
- Suite: **377 tests, OK (372 pass, 5 `expectedFailure` — F-11 flipped green)**, coverage **100.00%** (1612 stmts, up from 1607; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-19 — SPEC-7-04 (Section 7) — builder: env-driven products page size + stable default ordering ([R-7.6], F-23 + F-12 flip)

Per the section-7 conventions findings: the products listing page size is env-driven (the hardcoded 2 was a dev/test artifact) and the default listing queryset gets a deterministic total order, so pagination is stable across identical requests and the pre-existing UnorderedObjectListWarning anomaly is gone from the run output. Flips the pinned `expectedFailure` F-12 — expected-failure count drops 5 → 4.
- `config/settings.py` — `PRODUCTS_PAGE_SIZE = _env_int('PRODUCTS_PAGE_SIZE', 12)` (the established fail-safe pattern): a storefront-appropriate default replaces the leaked dev value; malformed env values fall back instead of crashing startup.
- `.env.example` — `PRODUCTS_PAGE_SIZE=12` documented with the F-23 rationale.
- `products/views.py` — Paginator takes `settings.PRODUCTS_PAGE_SIZE` (envelope keys unchanged: count/total_pages/current_page/next_page/previous_page/results); when no valid `ordering` param is given the queryset defaults to `order_by('-created_at', '-id')` — newest-first per storefront convention, with the unique `-id` tiebreaker making the sort total, so identical requests always partition the catalogue identically. Explicit whitelist orderings and all permissions/throttles untouched.
- `products/tests.py` — F-12 flip only: `@unittest.expectedFailure` removed from `test_f12_default_listing_order_is_deterministic` per its own docstring instruction; the assertion is byte-identical and now passes for real (identical timestamps force the tiebreaker → ids descending). Docstring updated to describe the landed mechanism (view-level default order_by; the model keeps no Meta.ordering): the pin prose anticipated a `Meta.ordering` fix with an `id` tiebreaker, but the pinned assertion demands DESCENDING ids under equal timestamps, so the landed tiebreaker is `-id`. Size-fixture adjustments (disclosed): the three tests that request page 2 / expect multi-page clamping get an explicit `@override_settings(PRODUCTS_PAGE_SIZE=2)` — assertions unchanged, they keep pinning genuinely multi-page behaviour instead of silently clamping onto page 1 at the new default; stale "hardcoded 2" comments dropped from `_list_all` and the envelope test. One new test `test_page_size_is_env_driven` pins the env knob (default-size envelope math + size-1 override → 3 pages of 1) — without it a regression back to a hardcoded 2 would be invisible to the override-decorated tests.
- Frontend consumers confirmed unaffected (grep only, no client edits): `products/page.tsx` and `sitemap.ts` read `total_pages`/`current_page`/`results` from the API envelope; nothing client-side hardcodes the page size, so the sitemap walk cost collapses automatically (~count/12 vs ~count/2 requests). The now-stale sitemap.ts comment about the size-2 backend is a docs-only nit left for a frontend task (outside this task's declared scope). Admin listing unaffected (ModelAdmin's own paginator).
- No model changes — `makemigrations --check` clean.
- Suite: **378 tests, OK (374 pass, 4 `expectedFailure` — F-12 flipped green)**, coverage **100.00%** (1615 stmts, up from 1612; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-19 — SPEC-15-1 (Section 15) — builder: WCAG 2.2 AA input contrast fix via shared field class

Per spec 15.4 ("Sufficient text contrast", WCAG 2.2 AA target; S15 audit SPEC-15-1) — the two REAL failures fixed and measured with the WCAG relative-luminance formula (per-channel linearization → L = 0.2126R + 0.7152G + 0.0722B, ratio = (L1+0.05)/(L2+0.05)):
- OLD placeholder: `placeholder:text-ink-muted/70` composited #57534e at 70% over the white input background → #898783, **3.5855:1** on surface (< 4.5:1). NEW: full-strength `placeholder:text-ink-muted` → **7.6293:1** on surface, **7.1882:1** worst-case on paper (pass with wide margin).
- OLD border: `border-line` #e6e1d7 → L = 0.755797, **1.3031:1** on surface / 1.2277:1 on paper (< 3:1, WCAG 1.4.11 non-text). NEW: dedicated field-border token `--color-line-strong: #75706a` → **4.9041:1** on surface, **4.6205:1** worst-case on paper.
- `frontend/src/lib/ui.ts` (new) — one `inputClass` export (`border border-line-strong bg-surface px-3 py-2 text-sm placeholder:text-ink-muted focus:outline-none`) replaces the 7 duplicated copies, so future form-control fixes land everywhere at once. Width intentionally excluded from the base class: the 5 full-width forms append `w-full` at the element, CouponForm/FilterBar keep their `min-w-0 flex-1`/`w-56`/`w-24` appends — no conflicting width utilities in one class list. Placement: `src/lib/` is the established shared-module home (money/filters/redirect pattern) with colocated vitest tests; `lib/tokens.ts` is the AUTH-token module, not design tokens.
- `frontend/src/app/globals.css` — `--color-line-strong` added; the decorative `line` token deliberately untouched: grep shows 60+ non-input `border-line`/`divide-line` uses (dividers, cards, footers, badges) plus `var(--color-line)` in `.skeleton`/`.text-ghost`, all exempt from 1.4.11 and visually unchanged. #75706a stays on the palette's warm r>g>b axis between ink-muted (#57534e) and line (#e6e1d7).
- Consumers switched to the shared class — 7, not just the 4 the audit pinned: AddressForm, LoginForm, RegisterForm, EmailOnlyForm, ResetPasswordClient, CouponForm, FilterBar. Grep found the identical failing string (same /70 placeholder + border-line) in the 3 unpinned auth forms, so scoping to 4 would have left the same AA failure live in the tree. No markup or behavior changes beyond className sources/imports.
- `frontend/src/lib/ui.test.ts` (new, 2 tests) — token-level pins (`border-line-strong` + `placeholder:text-ink-muted` present; a guard that no `placeholder:…/NN` opacity modifier ever returns — the exact mechanism that broke 4.5:1). Deliberately not a whole-string snapshot so unrelated layout classes can evolve.
- Frontend evidence: BEFORE (aa01edf, deps `npm ci`) vitest **22/22 (4 files)** + clean build; AFTER vitest **24/24 (5 files, floor moved up)** + clean `next build`; `border-line-strong` confirmed present in the compiled `.next` CSS (utility actually generated). Playwright e2e NOT run locally per task instructions (non-hermetic, CI self-skips; SPEC-3-01 owns it). No backend files touched — backend suite/floor untouched; no new env keys; no model changes.
- Ledger note: section-15.md had parked inputClass centralization under SPEC-2-02; this task's handoff mandates it now, so SPEC-2-02 retains only the broader component-library scope.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-19-1 (Section 19) — builder: in-process notification service ([R-19.0]) with the order.paid proof event

Per spec §19 ("Notifications should be driven by business events rather than manually scattered email calls"): one send path, one template-loading home, and an explicit `dispatch()` that rides the audit-event hooks. In-process substrate only — no queue/outbox table/retries/backoff (SPEC-2-03's separate scope); the full order-email content set (shipped/delivered/cancelled/returns/refunds + admin alerts) stays with SPEC-1-12/SPEC-19-2. `AuditEvent.record` itself untouched — same insert, same transaction placement, same append-only guards (semantics ratified by the SPEC-7-02 audit).
- `common/notifications.py` (new) — `send_email(template_name, context, subject, recipient)`: renders `emails/<template_name>.txt` through the project's TEMPLATES DIRS and sends with `settings.DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL` (sender never hardcoded), raising on failure so request-scoped flows keep their documented SMTP-503 behavior; `dispatch(event_type, context)`: registry lookup plus a log-and-continue wrapper (`logger.exception` on the dedicated `common.notifications` channel riding the existing LOGGING baseline) so a send failure can never break the caller's transaction; events with no registered handler are DEBUG-logged no-ops. Registry is explicit (spec 19.1), never a signal on `AuditEvent`.
- `templates/emails/` (new) — `verification_email` / `username_reminder` / `password_reset` / `order_confirmation` `.txt`. Placement: `TEMPLATES[DIRS]` already points at `backend/templates` (the admin templates' home), so no settings change; the three migrated templates render byte-identical bodies (uid/token passed separately so template autoescape can never rewrite the `&` link separator; files end without a trailing newline to keep the exact pre-service bodies).
- `accounts/views.py` — the scattered `_send_email` helper retired; `_send_verification_email`, `forgot_username` and `request_password_reset` now call `notifications.send_email` with identical subjects, recipients and links; every `try/except → 503` wrapper and uniform anonymous body is untouched (zero behavior change).
- `orders/views.py` — `verify_payment` success: `notifications.dispatch(AuditEvent.EventType.ORDER_PAID, {"order": order})` beside the existing `AuditEvent.record` calls, inside the same `transaction.atomic()` block (rollback-together like record; `dispatch` never raises, mirroring the SMTP-503 account-still-created behavior). One proof event end-to-end; the confirmation email carries order number, total and the frontend URL.
- `accounts/tests.py` — the two SMTP-failure tests' patch target moves from the retired `accounts.views._send_email` to `common.notifications.send_email` (required by the retirement; assertions byte-identical). The `_send_verification_email` patches were unaffected.
- `tests/test_notifications.py` (new, 10 tests) — byte-identity pins for the three migrated emails (body/subject/recipient/sender-from-settings); the one-send-path contract; `dispatch` unit behavior (unknown event no-op, `order.paid` send, missing-order context warning, swallowed-and-logged send failure); the end-to-end proof — verify success delivers the confirmation email, and a failing send leaves the payment 200/confirmed with the audit trail committed and an empty outbox.
- `common/testing.py` deliberately unchanged — the locmem outbox plus `mock.patch("common.notifications.send_email")` are sufficient; no test hook needed. `.env.example` — `FRONTEND_URL` comment extended (it now also feeds order-confirmation emails); no new env keys, no model/migration changes.
- Suite: **388 tests, OK (384 pass, 4 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1639 stmts, up from 1615; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-21-1 (Section 21) — builder: server-side duplicate-checkout dedup guard ([R-21.2.6])

Per spec §21.2 ("Duplicate checkout submissions do not create duplicate orders or charges"): decision taken = MINIMAL SERVER-SIDE GUARD (the handoff's preferred option), not a two-201 contract pin — checkout never clears the cart (cleanup is post-payment), so a double-click / client retry resubmits the byte-identical payload while the first order is still payable, which previously minted a second payable order and with it a real double-charge path; nothing in the code or docs says the guard must not exist, and the ledger reserves only the header-keyed machinery (Idempotency-Key + unique-by-key store, covering cross-session simultaneous duplicates) for SPEC-9-01 — none of that is pre-built here.
- `orders/views.py` — `create_order`'s existing `transaction.atomic()` block now first `select_for_update()`s the cart row (the session-scoped serialization point: two rapid same-session POSTs queue there, so the loser re-runs the lookup after the winner has committed; the cart row is locked only on this path — verify_payment locks Order → Products → Coupon — so no new lock-order cycle, the SPEC-12-02 deadlock advisory is untouched) and consults `_find_duplicate_pending_order`: the user's recent pending order (within `CHECKOUT_DEDUP_WINDOW_SECONDS`, payable gate mirrors verify's already-processed check) carrying the identical fingerprint — sorted (product_id, quantity) lines + coupon + the six shipping fields. Match → replay that order with 200 (INFO log, no second order / item snapshot / order.created trail row); any deliberate difference → fresh 201 exactly as before. verify_payment untouched; no new models/migrations.
- `config/settings.py` + `.env.example` — `CHECKOUT_DEDUP_WINDOW_SECONDS = _env_int('CHECKOUT_DEDUP_WINDOW_SECONDS', 300)`: the guard's only threshold is env-driven per conventions (no magic numbers in code); outside the handoff's ≤3-file list for exactly this reason.
- `orders/tests.py` — `CheckoutDedupTests` (7 tests) pins the new contract: double-click replay (200 + same id, 1 order / 1 item / 1 order.created trail row), the replayed order is the one that gets paid (exactly one gateway order minted for it), a different user with an identical cart is unaffected (per-user keying), deliberate coupon / shipping differences and changed cart lines always create new orders, window expiry allows a genuine reorder, and a settled (no-longer-payable) order never blocks a fresh identical checkout.
- `tests/test_e2e_concurrency.py` — the two-201 assertion updated deliberately in the same commit: cross-user two-201 remains the pinned contract (the dedup guard is keyed per user, so the oversell decision stays at verify_payment's row locks); a comment explains the new contract and points at `CheckoutDedupTests` for the same-user collapse.
- Suite: **395 tests, OK (391 pass, 4 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1665 stmts, up from 1639; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-21-2 (Section 21) — builder: CSRF-enforcement truth + API XSS-output safety pins ([R-21.1.4])

Per spec §21.1.4 (security test layer, CSRF/XSS): the S21 prefetch found CSRF middleware configured (settings.py:66,262) but ZERO CSRF-enforcement tests and no XSS tests anywhere. This task pins today's contract — no enforcement behavior changed (the gap itself is SPEC-17-03's build task); the pins make any future flip deliberate.
- `tests/test_csrf_security.py` (new, 12 tests) — CSRF half: `CsrfStackWiringTests` pins CsrfViewMiddleware installed + `DEFAULT_AUTHENTICATION_CLASSES` JWT-only (the load-bearing line SPEC-17-03 changes; the failure message points at the flip) and `SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE` 'Lax' as the relied-upon mitigation. `SessionCartCsrfTruthTests` pins today's truth per BACKEND_REQUESTS.md's live confirmation: POST/PATCH/DELETE on `/api/cart/*` with only the session cookie and no `X-CSRFToken` succeed (201/200/200), they still succeed under a fully-enforcing client (`enforce_csrf_checks=True` — the exemption is server-side view-level `csrf_exempt`, not a test-client artifact), and no API response ever issues a `csrftoken` cookie. `CsrfMiddlewareDifferentialTests` proves the middleware is live: tokenless POST `/admin/login/` under enforcement → 403 (so the cart pins mean "exempt", not "middleware absent"). `TokenAuthMutationCsrfTruthTests` pins checkout (201) and apply-coupon (200) succeeding without CSRF tokens (the JWT is non-ambient, so checkout's exploitable surface is the session cookie its cart resolution also requires). XSS half: `ApiXssOutputSafetyTests` pins the API boundary — script/meta/img payloads in product name/description and checkout full_name/address come back verbatim-as-stored (list/detail/cart/orders surfaces), `Content-Type: application/json`, payload literal in the raw JSON body — the API emits data, never markup; output encoding is the consumer's job (frontend JSON-LD sink is SPEC-17-06 / S21-4 territory; grep-verified the only server-side `render()` surfaces are staff-gated admin/ops templates, Django-auto-escaped, no `mark_safe` of user content, plus `.txt` emails).
- Docstrings in the new module state exactly what SPEC-17-03's `SessionAuthentication` change will flip (2xx → 403 on session-cookie flows) so updating the pins is a deliberate one-line contract update, not an accident.
- No production code, settings, middleware, or model changes — `makemigrations --check` clean.
- Suite: **407 tests, OK (403 pass, 4 `expectedFailure` flips unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1665 stmts, unchanged — test-only addition; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-8-01 (Section 8) — builder: customer-facing order numbers (ORD-YYYY-NNNNNN) + identifier-exposure strategy ([R-8.4],[R-8.5])

Per spec §8.3 ("Give orders a separate human-readable order number, such as `ORD-2026-001042`"; index on order number; non-sequential identifier exposure), orders were identified only by the sequential auto-pk that URLs/admin/serializers expose directly.
- **Exposure-strategy decision** (documented in the `Order` model docstring and the `OrderSerializer` docstring): the sequential `id` stays the internal key — it remains the URL/admin primary key and no URL changes — while `order_number` is the read-only customer-facing reference, surfaced at checkout (201 body) and on every order read. Guest checkout (SPEC-3-02) will key on `order_number`; the pk never leaves server-side routing.
- `orders/models.py` — `Order.order_number = CharField(max_length=20, unique=True, null=True, blank=True)`: 15 chars for `ORD-YYYY-NNNNNN` + headroom; `unique=True` is simultaneously the customer-reference guarantee, the spec 8.3 index, and the concurrency authority for generation. Nullable by design: checkout (the only production writer) always sets it, non-checkout ORM creations (16 test-fixture sites) keep working, and the migration backfills every pre-existing row, so the column is fully populated after migrating.
- `orders/migrations/0006_order_order_number.py` — AddField + `RunPython` backfill: per-year sequence in `id` order (UTC `created_at` year, the same clock the runtime generator reads), single writer inside the migration transaction so the unique constraint cannot be violated; reverse is a no-op. `makemigrations --check` clean.
- `orders/views.py` — generation inside `create_order`'s existing `transaction.atomic()` block (after the [R-21.2.6] dedup guard, untouched): `_next_order_sequence(year)` reads the year prefix's lexicographic max (zero padding keeps lexicographic == numeric) as a **fast path only**; the unique constraint is the race authority, and a bounded (5-attempt) retry loop with a per-attempt savepoint catches `IntegrityError`, regenerates from committed state, and re-raises loudly on exhaustion (conventions.md:17 — IntegrityError retry, never trust check-then-act). A rolled-back checkout never burns a number; same-session replays never reach the loop (dedup guard returns first).
- `orders/serializers.py` — `order_number` added to `fields` + `read_only_fields`; `orders/admin.py` — `list_display` gains `order_number` beside the pk.
- `orders/test_order_number.py` (new, 9 tests): format pin (`^ORD-\d{4}-\d{6}$` + current-year bucket), per-year sequence + restart-at-000001 rollover, unique-constraint pin, forced collision-retry (deterministic — first candidate collides, retry commits fresh, exactly one retry), exhausted-retries rollback (fails loudly, nothing left behind), dedup interplay (replayed checkout returns the SAME order_number — extends the SPEC-21-1 pin), serializer exposure/read-only + pk-stays-URL-key, admin `list_display`.
- verify_payment/payment semantics, permission surfaces, and URL routing untouched; no new env keys (the format and retry bound are code constants, not config).
- Suite: **416 tests, OK (412 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (1798 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 1665 with the new code; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-8-02a (Section 8) — builder: ProductVariant schema core with globally unique SKU ([R-8.7])

Per spec 8.3 ("Enforce unique SKUs where the business requires global SKU uniqueness", line 2481; index on SKU, line 2553; FKs between core entities, 2483) the product catalogue had nowhere to hang a sellable variant, and the order-item snapshot list (8.3:2499–2515) already names "Variant name/options" and "SKU" as things orders must retain. Half of the SPEC-8-02 split: schema core ONLY — OrderItem snapshot wiring is SPEC-8-02b's, catalogue/admin depth is SPEC-6-08's.
- `products/models.py` — `ProductVariant`: FK → `products` (`on_delete=CASCADE` matching the `StockMovement` child precedent; order history retention is the snapshot's job per 2497, not FK retention), `name` CharField(100) (the variant label the snapshot list reads, 2503), `sku` CharField(64, **unique=True**) — globally unique across the whole table, the constraint and its implied backing unique index in one (the same reading the shipped `Order.order_number` pins; merchant-entered, so conventions.md:17's IntegrityError-retry has no generation path to wrap — the DB constraint is the concurrency authority), `price` DecimalField(10,2, null=True) (exact decimal money per 2489/conventions.md:15; spec 6.2's "variant-specific prices" — NULL means the product price applies, no read-side wiring invented here), `stock` PositiveBigIntegerField(default=0) mirroring `products.stock`'s type, `created_at`/`updated_at` (distinct timestamps per 2523).
- Stock boundary (kept inert by design): `products.stock` remains the ONLY order-time authority — checkout/verify_payment never read `ProductVariant.stock`; a zero-stock variant neither blocks nor consumes a checkout. Reconciliation is SPEC-6-13's; proven by an integration test, not just asserted.
- `products/admin.py` — bare `ProductVariantAdmin(admin.ModelAdmin)` registration only: entity visible, default per-model permission gating (no role holds them yet), deliberately no role matrix/fieldsets/actions (SPEC-6-08's).
- `products/migrations/0007_productvariant.py` — CreateModel; `makemigrations --check` clean.
- `products/test_variant.py` (new, 11 tests): exact field-set pin (drift guard), FK target/on_delete/reverse-accessor + cascade deletion policy, decimal-money + NULL-price metadata, distinct created/updated timestamps, `__str__`, globally-unique SKU proven across two DIFFERENT products via forced-collision IntegrityError (the race authority, deterministic), DB-level unique constraint introspected from the live schema (SQLite labels constraint-origin indexes `index=False` — the physical auto-index exists regardless), stock defaults to zero, the zero-stock-variant paid-checkout boundary pin, and bare-admin registration + reachable changelist.
- No changes to OrderItem, checkout, verify_payment, serializers, cart or URLs; no fixture edits; no new env keys.
- Suite: **427 tests, OK (423 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (1920 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 1798 with the new code; gate 90), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-8-02b (Section 8) — builder: OrderItem snapshot columns sku/variant_name — the historical-order immutability pattern ([R-8.13])

Per spec 8.3 "Historical snapshots" (orders must retain what was actually purchased, 2495–2515: product name 2501, variant name/options 2503, SKU 2505), order lines are now self-describing even after the catalogue changes or the product row is gone. Second half of the SPEC-8-02 split (depends on SPEC-8-02a's ProductVariant).

- **Population-source decision (documented):** no variant-selection input exists at checkout — `CartItem` references products only and variant PICKING rides SPEC-3-21/SPEC-6-08 — and `products` carries no product-level SKU (verified). The honest minimal reading: `sku` snapshots empty and `variant_name` mirrors the product name at checkout (spec's intent that lines are self-describing); a matched variant's SKU/name would fabricate history the customer never chose. Variant matching becomes real with SPEC-3-21's selection input — the columns are already variant-SKU-width (64).
- `orders/models.py` — `OrderItem.sku` (CharField(64), matches `ProductVariant.sku` width) + `OrderItem.variant_name` (CharField(200), product_name width), both `default=''`; docstring pins the frozen-at-checkout contract and the population source.
- `orders/views.py` — line-item construction inside `create_order`'s atomic block (between the 21-1 dedup guard and the 8-01 order-number mint, byte-unchanged) sets `sku=''` and `variant_name=product.name` once; no later save path mutates them.

## 2026-09-20 - SPEC-8-03 (Section 8) - builder: currency column alongside every money column, store-config-driven ([R-8.11], part 1/2)

- `config/settings.py` — `DEFAULT_CURRENCY` via `_env_currency(name, default)`, a fail-safe env resolver in the established `_env_int`/`_env_log_level` idiom: three-letter ISO 4217 shape (case-insensitive), malformed values fall back to `INR` with a warning instead of failing startup. Deliberately env config, NOT a SiteSettings column: a currency is never-changes-at-runtime config (S16 boundary) — runtime-editable currency would reprice historical rows' meaning.
- `.env.example` — `DEFAULT_CURRENCY=INR` documented (affects NEW rows only; existing rows keep their minted currency).
- `orders/models.py` — `Order.currency` + `OrderItem.currency` (`CharField(max_length=3)`, callable `default_currency()` reading `settings.DEFAULT_CURRENCY` at creation — chosen over set-at-creation so every writer, not just checkout, gets the config value; the callable reference keeps migration files deterministic). Order currency denominates `total_amount` + `discount_amount`; item currency rides `price`/`subtotal`. `Coupon.discount_value` stays out of scope: catalogue config, not transaction money.
- `orders/migrations/0008_order_currency.py` — two AddFields + `RunPython` backfill stamping every pre-existing row with the literal `'INR'` (deterministic regardless of the migrating environment's `DEFAULT_CURRENCY`, which the callable default would otherwise bake in); reverse is a documented noop.
- `orders/views.py` — both hardcoded `'INR'` literals in `create_payment` (gateway `order.create` payload + client response) now read `order.currency`; no other line touched (dedup guard, order-number mint, snapshot population byte-unchanged).
- `orders/tests.py` (+9): store-default currency on order/items with Decimal money intact, setting override changes new rows (ORM + checkout), gateway payload charged in the order's currency (mocked Razorpay), recorded currency frozen against later setting changes, `_env_currency` valid/malformed-with-warning/missing branches, and a migration round-trip (`0007` → raw pre-currency rows → `0008`) pinning the deterministic `'INR'` backfill.
- **Part 2/2 remainder (next dispatch)**: `orders/serializers.py` (`currency` in `OrderSerializer` + `OrderItemSerializer` fields/read_only), `orders/admin.py` (`OrderAdmin.list_display` + payment fieldset/readonly), and their exposure tests — spec sentence "customer-facing serializers surface the currency + admin list_display".
- Boundary note: `verify_payment` does NOT assert the gateway amount at HEAD (the dispatch premise was stale — there is no gateway fetch/reconciliation in the verify path); adding server-side amount+currency reconciliation is a separate concern, not smuggled in here. Frontend `money.ts` INR formatting is explicitly a frontend task (formatting consumption, not schema).

## 2026-09-20 - SPEC-8-04 (Section 8) - builder: business-event timestamps on Order — the lifecycle timeline is queryable ([R-8.16])

- `orders/models.py` — six named business-event columns on `Order` (spec 8.3 "Timestamps": distinct timestamps per business event, never overload `updated_at` for one): `paid_at`, `fulfilled_at`, `shipped_at`, `delivered_at`, `cancelled_at`, `refunded_at` — each NULL until its event, written exactly once by the code path performing the event, never mutated once set. `created_at`/`updated_at` already cover Created/Updated; `Published` is a catalogue (Product) event, not an Order lifecycle event, so it is deliberately not an Order column. No index yet: spec 8.3 prescribes indexes from measured query patterns and none of these dates is queried with status today.
- `orders/migrations/0009_order_business_event_timestamps.py` — six `AddField`s (`null=True, blank=True`), no backfill: historical events' times are unknowable, rows stay NULL (documented in the model comment).
- `orders/views.py` — `verify_payment` success path stamps `paid_at` beside the pending→confirmed transition inside the same atomic block (`order.paid_at or timezone.now()` + `update_fields`): rollback-together, and the or-guard pins "written exactly once, never mutated" even beyond the already-processed replay gate. Dedup guard/order-number/snapshot/currency regions byte-unchanged.
- `orders/admin.py` — both cancellation writers stamp `cancelled_at`: the `cancel_pending` bulk action (stamp rides the same `update()` as the status transition; the pending filter makes a re-run a no-op) and the `save_model` change-form path (pending→cancelled is a legal transition there; is-none guard). Exposure: `list_display` gains `paid_at`/`cancelled_at` (the two live writers), all six join `readonly_fields` + the "Timestamps" fieldset — admin edits would falsify the lifecycle record.
- `orders/serializers.py` — all six added to `OrderSerializer` `fields` + `read_only_fields` (the `order_number`/`currency` pattern): the timeline rides every order read (checkout response, dedup replay, list/detail); a client can never claim an event happened.
- `orders/tests.py` (+6, `BusinessEventTimestampTests`): fresh order starts with all six NULL; verify stamps `paid_at` exactly once and a replayed verify never overwrites it; forged signature leaves `paid_at` NULL; admin bulk cancel stamps `cancelled_at` (and only that — cancel-before-payment never implies paid/shipped/refunded) with re-run idempotency; change-form cancel stamps too; serializer + admin expose all six read-only with NULL-over-API until the event. Admin flows driven through the real admin UI (superuser session, in-process, no network).
- Deferred writers (named pattern lands now, writers with their sections): `fulfilled_at`/`shipped_at`/`delivered_at` → fulfilment sections (SPEC-1-05/SPEC-1-08/S10); `refunded_at` → refund flow (SPEC-6-11); `mark_confirmed` deliberately stamps nothing — an unpaid admin confirmation is not a payment event.
- Suite: **452 tests, OK (448 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (1948 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 1938 with the new code), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-9-01 (Section 9) — builder: checkout submit idempotency — Idempotency-Key honored on POST checkout, unique-by-key dedupe inside the atomic creation block ([R-9.3.14], [R-9.3.19])

- `orders/models.py` — `Order.idempotency_key` (CharField 128, nullable): set once by create_order when the client sends the header, NULL for keyless submissions; plus `Order.Meta.constraints` `UniqueConstraint(user, idempotency_key)` `orders_user_idem_key_uidx` — the concurrency authority for keyed replays (NULLs stay distinct, so keyless rows can never collide; per-user scoping means a reused key on another account is an independent submission, never an existence leak). No expiry: the key lives with the order row it deduped, so a retry collapses onto the original outcome forever.
- `orders/views.py` (NEW hunks only; all five protected regions byte-untouched — diff verified 0 deletion lines): header extraction/validation at the top of create_order (blank → absent; >128 chars → 400 "Idempotency-Key is too long" with no side effects); inside the existing atomic block, AFTER the SPEC-21-1 guard: `select_for_update` on the user row (serializes cross-session same-key submissions — the surface the cart-row lock cannot cover; lock order Cart→User, no cycle with verify_payment's Order→Products→Coupon) then the replay probe — a hit returns the original order 200 and never mints a second order_number, regardless of payload drift, dedup-window expiry, or a settled first attempt; after the mint loop: key bound to the fresh order via `save(update_fields=['idempotency_key'])` in the same transaction.
- Composition with SPEC-21-1 (3391d93), unchanged and behind it: the fingerprint guard stays the first line of defence for keyless same-session double-clicks; the key layer catches what it deliberately does not (drifted payloads, expired window, settled order, cross-session retries). Keyless behavior is pinned unchanged by CheckoutDedupTests.
- `orders/migrations/0011_order_idempotency_key_and_more.py` — `AddField` + `AddConstraint`. Forward + reverse + re-apply verified on a scratch DB via `DATABASE_URL`.
- `orders/test_idempotency.py` (new, 14 tests): creation/binding (key bound, absent-header regression pin, blank-as-absent, 128-char boundary accepted, 129-char 400 with zero side effects, failed submit burns no key), replay semantics (drifted-payload replay returns original 200 with no second order/item/trail row, cross-session replay, window-expiry replay, post-settlement replay, same-key-other-user independence), race authority (duplicate (user, key) rejected, NULL-distinctness for keyless rows, unauthenticated keyed checkout 401). Deterministic pins, no thread scheduling, Razorpay untouched.
- Suite: **474 tests, OK (470 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (2098 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 1952 with the new code), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-9-03 (Section 9) — builder: uniform error envelope — one middleware converting DRF field errors + ad-hoc {"error":…} bodies into a single response shape ([R-9.2.19])

- Spec reading: §9.2 closes the API contract (spec line 3002) with "Use appropriate HTTP status codes, validation errors and consistent error responses" — it prescribes CONSISTENCY, not a literal shape, so the single shape is defined here and pinned by tests: `{"error": <message str>, "code": <status-family label>, "details": <field errors / context, {} when none>}`.
- `common/errors.py` (new) — `ErrorEnvelopeMiddleware` + helpers. Middleware-level, NOT a `REST_FRAMEWORK EXCEPTION_HANDLER`: most error bodies here are ad-hoc `Response({"error": ...})` returns that raise no exception, so a handler would never see them; middleware sees every response (DRF responses are still unrendered there, so rewriting `response.data` rewrites the body). Recognition is conservative (error payloads = str `error`/`detail` key or list values, i.e. serializer field errors) so non-error JSON 4xx/5xx (the health probe's 503 checks body) passes through byte-identical; HTML bodies and unparseable JSON pass through too. `code` values are status-family labels (400 validation_error, 401 not_authenticated, 403 permission_denied, 404 not_found, 405 method_not_allowed, 409 conflict, 429 throttled, 5xx server_error) — the cause lives in the message.
- `config/settings.py` — middleware appended to `MIDDLEWARE` (with rationale comment). Frontend `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` — `parseErrorBody` promotes envelope `details` content (arrays → fieldErrors for RegisterForm/ResetPasswordClient, scalars → extras for CouponForm's minimum_order_amount); every legacy flat shape still parses unchanged.
- Deliberate assertion migrations (old shape → new shape, never weakened): `orders/tests.py` coupon-uniformity exact-pin `{"error": "Invalid coupon code"}` → `{"error": "Invalid coupon code", "code": "validation_error", "details": {}}`; `orders/tests.py` ×3 `res.data["products"]` → `res.data["details"]["products"]` (checkout stock-gate context); `products/tests.py` ×4 `res.data["detail"]` → `res.data["error"]` (PermissionDenied body); `products/tests.py` ×4 `assertIn(field, res.data)` → `assertIn(field, res.data["details"])` (serializer field errors); `accounts/tests.py` ×9 `res.data["password"]` / `assertIn("password", res.data)` → details-based access; `tests/test_e2e_auth_recovery.py` ×1 same. All 17 sites assert the same semantic content in the envelope's location.
- `tests/test_error_envelope.py` (new, 8 tests): ad-hoc 404 enveloped; DRF permission-denial enveloped; DRF 401 enveloped; serializer field errors land in `details`; scalar context (coupon `minimum_order_amount`) moves into `details`; health-503 checks body untouched byte-for-byte; non-DRF JSON error body enveloped via the content path; unparseable JSON error body passes through (throwaway urlconf).
- Suite: **487 tests, OK (483 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (2143 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 2102 with the new code), `makemigrations --check` clean; frontend vitest **24 passed**.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-9-04 (Section 9) — builder: customer order-detail endpoint (owner-checked, IDOR-pinned) + order-history pagination ([R-9.2.14], [R-9.2.15])

- Spec reading: §9.2 table rows — `GET /account/orders` = "Customer order history" (lines 2938-2946) and `GET /account/orders/:id` = "Owned order details" (lines 2951-2959). The spec pins NO page size and NO pagination envelope for order history (its only pagination word is "Paginated catalogue", §9.1), so the house page-number envelope (products-listing parity — the shape the frontend's pagination UI is built to) is used with env-driven defaults, documented below.

- `orders/tests.py` (+12, `OrderDetailTests` + `OrderHistoryPaginationTests`): owner detail 200 with serializer body; foreign user's order 404 NOT 200 (IDOR pin); unknown id 404; anonymous 401; v1-mirror serve; envelope key set/count/page fields; newest-first ordering with `?page=2` selection under page size 1; `?page_size` override; cap enforcement; invalid `page_size` ("abc"/"0"/"-5") falls back to default; invalid page falls back to page 1; empty history is a valid first page.
- Deliberate assertion migrations for the new list envelope (old bare array → `results`, semantic content unchanged, never weakened): `orders/tests.py` ×4 sites (currency list reads ×2, owner-scoping ids + coupon/price row, timestamps listing row), `orders/test_order_number.py` ×1, `tests/test_orderitem_snapshot.py` ×3, `tests/test_csrf_security.py` ×1, `tests/test_e2e_purchase_flow.py` ×1, `tests/test_e2e_isolation.py` ×3, `tests/test_e2e_auth_recovery.py` ×1. Frontend: `src/lib/orders-api.ts` gains `fetchOrderHistoryPage` (full envelope + page/page_size params) and `fetchOrders` unwraps `results` so both list consumers keep working (no component changes); `src/lib/api.test.ts` ×1 mocked `/api/orders/` success body updated from a bare array to the envelope shape.

## 2026-09-20 — SPEC-9-06 (Section 9) — builder: REST inventory adjustment — thin endpoint reusing adjust_stock + HasInventoryAdjust, ledger-asserted ([R-9.4.7])

- `cart/urls.py` — `path('coupon/', cart_coupon, name='cart-coupon')`; served on both legacy `/api/cart/` and `/api/v1/store/cart/` mounts automatically (dual-mount resolve pinned).

## 2026-09-20 - SPEC-9-07 (Section 9) - builder: admin orders JSON seam + _bulk_set_status WHERE hardening ([R-9.4.8]-[R-9.4.11] + SPEC-6-04 advisory)

- Spec reading: §9.4 admin-module table rows 8-11 (spec lines 3285-3317) — Orders `GET /admin/orders` (3289), Order detail `GET /admin/orders/:id` (3298), Fulfilment `POST /admin/orders/:id/fulfill` (3307), Cancellation `POST /admin/orders/:id/cancel` (3316); Refunds (3325) stays SPEC-6-11 per depth policy. Admin feature depth (UI, refund flows, item-level ops) explicitly out of scope.
- Routing reading: the ratified family mapping mounts admin JSON at `v1:admin` (SPEC-9-02), and the ledger requirement names `GET /api/admin/orders/` — so these are wired like the dashboard/audit-log pair: direct path entries in `config/urls.py` under BOTH families (legacy `/api/admin/orders/...` + `v1/admin/orders/...`), same view objects, no duplication, no orders/urls.py change (that urlconf is already dual-served under the customer/store families; paths added there would also leak the admin seam onto `/api/orders/admin/...`). The §9.4 JSON precedent (SPEC-9-06 inventory adjust) went through the shared app urlconf into `v1/store/` because products.urls is the only mount for those views; here the dashboard pattern is the closer match and the only one that yields the ledger's path.
- `orders/views.py` (new hunks ONLY — imports + EOF append, 171 insertions / 0 deletions; protected regions 21-1, 8-01, 8-02b, 8-03, 8-04 byte-untouched): `admin_order_list` (unscoped order list, house page-number envelope + `ORDER_HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE`/cap config — no caller, staff included, gets an unbounded page), `admin_order_detail` (uniform 404 for unknown ids), `admin_order_fulfill` (advances one legal step per call: pending→confirmed→shipped→delivered, 409 with the machine's allowed-set in `details` on terminal/illegal states), `admin_order_cancel` (pending-only; 409 with the refund-reconciliation hint mirrors the admin wording; idempotent on cancelled via the machine's self-transition — 200 no-op, no second stamp, no duplicate audit row). Permission classes: `HasOrdersRead` / `HasOrdersFulfill` / `HasOrdersCancel` — defined in common/permissions.py since SPEC-6-03 but previously unmounted; now wired exactly as the factory names anticipated. Writes run `transaction.atomic()` + `select_for_update()` on the order row so the gate-then-update pair cannot double-pass on a stale status; every accepted write lands a privileged-action LogEntry via `common.audit.log_api_action` (the API-side twin of the admin's bulk-action trail, [6.12.6]); cancel stamps `cancelled_at` beside the transition exactly like admin `cancel_pending` (is-none guard keeps a set stamp immutable). Fulfil writes NO business-event stamps — admin `mark_shipped`/`mark_delivered` don't either; the named-stamp writers are their own later task and the JSON seam never records more than the admin surface for the same transition. No throttle scope: the endpoints deny anonymous callers outright (conventions' throttle rule targets public mutating endpoints — SPEC-9-06 same shape).
- `orders/admin.py` — SPEC-6-04 advisory hardening (sanctioned surgical edit, NOT a protected region): `_bulk_set_status`'s UPDATE now re-applies `status__in=allowed_from` in its WHERE clause. The pk snapshot and the UPDATE are two statements; a row whose status changes in between (e.g. concurrently cancelled while pending) matched by pk alone and would be swept to the new status — resurrecting a cancelled financial record. The transition predicate is now the final authority: out-of-set rows can never be written, only counted as skipped.
- `orders/tests.py` (+21): `AdminOrdersApiTests` — permission matrix (anon 403, unprivileged customer 403, finance `orders.read` 200 on list/detail unscoped, finance denied fulfill/cancel with nothing moved and zero LogEntry rows); every error pinned in the uniform envelope (`error`/`code`/`details`, codes `permission_denied`/`not_found`/`conflict`); fulfil happy path + one-step-per-call walk + terminal-state 409s (delivered AND cancelled, the pinned illegal transition) + unknown-id 404 + audit-row assertions (user, CHANGE flag, message); cancel happy path + `cancelled_at` stamp + paid-order 409 with refund hint + delivered 409 + idempotent replay (stamp identical, LogEntry count unchanged) + 404; list envelope shape; v1:admin dual-mount serves. `BulkSetStatusRaceGuardTests` — deterministic race interleave via a queryset subclass that flips a matched pending row to cancelled between the pk snapshot and the UPDATE: out-of-set row stays cancelled (old code resurrected it to confirmed), control row still transitions.
- Suite: **549 tests, OK (545 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (2286 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 2231), `makemigrations --check` clean (no migrations).

## 2026-09-20 - SPEC-10-02 (Section 10) - builder: immutable transition audit trail - OrderStatusEvent written by every legal status writer; bulk path reworked to per-row saves ([R-10.12],[R-10.17],[R-10.18])

- Spec reading (§10.3 transition rules, lines 3491-3509): each transition carries an "Audit record" (R-10.12) whose content names the "Actor or triggering event" (R-10.17 — the event carries both: `actor` + `trigger`, either sufficing, so verify_payment writes actor NULL + trigger `payment_verify`); the trail is append-only (R-10.18 — immutability via a model save guard + view-only admin registration, nothing more, per the design bar). Creation event: written at checkout (NULL→pending) inside the existing atomic block — the spec's lifecycle diagram starts the machine at pending, and a trail that opened mid-life would leave every order's first transition unrecorded. No dimension events: the trail records the LEGACY status transition per R-10.12's from→to wording (the dimensions are derived state, SPEC-10-01a's mapping, not independently-written machine states). No backfill: the table is new, historical transitions are unknowable (same reasoning as the 8-16 timestamp columns).
- `orders/state.py` (+24) — trigger vocabulary single-sourced beside the machine it audits: six `TRIGGER_*` constants + `STATUS_EVENT_TRIGGERS` choices (order_create, admin_change_form, admin_bulk_action, payment_verify, admin_api_fulfil, admin_api_cancel); a new writer cannot invent an unregistered source.
- `orders/models.py` (+80) — `OrderStatusEvent`: order FK (`related_name='status_events'`, CASCADE like OrderItem), `from_status` (NULL exactly for creation events), `to_status`, `actor` (SET_NULL — deleting a user must never cascade into the audit trail), `trigger` (choices from state.py), `created_at` auto_now_add; Meta ordering newest-first with id tiebreaker; `save()` raises TypeError on any pk-set re-save (append-only guard, test-pinned); docstring cross-references SPEC-7-01's LogEntry trail (complementary, not replacement). `orders/migrations/0013_orderstatusevent.py` — CreateModel; forward/reverse/re-apply proven on a scratch DATABASE_URL sqlite DB.
- `orders/admin.py` — (1) `save_model`: event after `super().save_model()` inside an explicit `transaction.atomic()` (changeform view is already atomic; the block keeps rollback-together local), self-transitions write no event; (2) `_bulk_set_status` REWORKED: the single `.update()` becomes per-row saves in one atomic block — same selection predicate, each row's status re-checked at write time under `select_for_update()` (the 9-07 WHERE hardening's final-authority principle, per row), out-of-set rows skipped not swept; per-row saves fire auto_now, fixing the bulk path's stale `updated_at` (empirically: on Django 6.1 even `save(update_fields=...)` skips auto_now unless listed, so `updated_at` rides update_fields explicitly — pinned by test); (3) `cancel_pending` reworked identically — the re-check additionally closes the 9-07-shaped race its old `pk__in`-only UPDATE still had (a row flipped pending→confirmed between snapshot and write would have been swept to cancelled; now skipped); (4) `OrderStatusEventAdmin`: view-only registration — staff read via `orders.read`, add/delete hard-False even for the superuser, every field readonly (change form renders as a view); `_append_status_event` helper shared by the three admin writers.
- `orders/views.py` — **64 insertions / 0 deletions** (every hunk insertion-only; own import lines per the 10-01b pattern; the five byte-frozen protected regions appear as context only). Four writer sites: (1) checkout creation event after the order-number mint loop, inside the outer atomic block; (2) verify_payment — `previous_status` captured just before the frozen 8-04 region, event after the payment_status save, actor None + trigger `payment_verify`; (3) `admin_order_fulfill` — event with `request.user` + trigger `admin_api_fulfil`; (4) `admin_order_cancel` — event with trigger `admin_api_cancel`, unreachable on the idempotent replay branch (no second event).
- `orders/tests.py` (+15 net: `TransitionAuditTrailTests` with 15 tests + race-guard extension): creation event at checkout; verify event (actor NULL, trigger names the source); failed verify writes nothing; rollback-together pinned three ways — verify (audit failure after the event reverts it), change form (event-create failure reverts the whole save), bulk (implied by the atomic block + per-row tests); change-form legal event with actor, illegal/no-op saves write none; bulk per-row events with fresh `updated_at` (`assertGreater` vs the pre-action value) and no event for the skipped row; bulk-cancel events; JSON-seam fulfil walk (pending→confirmed→shipped, staff actor) and cancel with replay appending no second event; append-only save guard raises; admin surface view-only (staff: view yes/mutate no; superuser: add/delete refused); meta pins (trigger choices single-sourced, ordering, verbose name, `__str__`). `BulkSetStatusRaceGuardTests.test_out_of_set_row_is_never_swept_by_the_bulk_update` EXTENDED (enumerated rework): mechanism unchanged (RacingQuerySet.values_list interleave still works against the per-row re-check), added assertions pin the audit layer — the skipped row gets no sweep event, the swept row exactly one; intent preserved (out-of-set rows never swept), still deterministic (no threads/sleeps).
- Suite: **582 tests, OK (578 pass, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (2378 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 2306), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-20 - SPEC-10-05 (Section 10) - builder: side-effect contract per transition - one dispatch point (orders/events.py) firing the shared notifications seam on shipped/delivered/cancelled; hook failure is log-only ([R-10.16])

- Spec reading (§10.3, lines 3489-3509): each transition declares its "Side effects" and "Failure/retry behaviour". Minimal contract per handoff: shipped/delivered/cancelled fire ONE notification hook; the notifications subsystem itself (handlers/templates/outbox) stays SPEC-1-12/S19 — only the seam is wired.
- `orders/events.py` (new, single dispatch point) — `TRANSITION_EVENTS` (shipped→"order.shipped", delivered→"order.delivered", cancelled→"order.cancelled"; dotted names follow the AuditEvent.EventType convention, so SPEC-1-12/S19's future EventType members + handlers bind without touching the hook sites) + `notify_transition(order, from_status, to_status)`. REUSES the existing `common.notifications.dispatch` seam (the R-19.0 dispatch verify_payment already calls for order.paid) — no second send path invented; confirmed is deliberately unhooked (the payment notification already has its owner). Placement documented: writers call it AFTER the transition save + OrderStatusEvent append, INSIDE the same `transaction.atomic()` block — the in-transaction call is safe by construction (dispatch never raises, wrapper swallows anyway; the pre-commit-send caveat is common/notifications.py's documented, accepted substrate cost). Hook cardinality equals the audit trail's: skips and idempotent replays return before the notify site.
- `orders/admin.py` (+16/-0) and `orders/views.py` (+11/-0, both hunks insertion-only; protected regions context-only) — the hook rides all five real writers: `_bulk_set_status` (shipped/delivered; `previous_status` captured beside the existing event's from_status), `cancel_pending`, `save_model` (change form), `admin_order_fulfill`, `admin_order_cancel`. verify_payment untouched.

## 2026-09-20 - SPEC-10-04 (Section 10) - builder: COD branch + failed/retryable payment states in the order machine ([R-10.2],[R-10.4],[R-10.17])

## 2026-09-21 - SPEC-12-01 (Section 12) - builder: StockReservation model + env-driven RESERVATION_TTL for the inventory reservation lifecycle ([R-12.2]/[R-12.3]/[R-12.4]/[R-12.10])

- Spec reading (§12 lines 3579-3633): the data-model task only. §12.1's on-hand/reserved/available-to-sell split maps onto the schema as: on-hand stays `products.stock` (the only authority), reserved = Σ of a product's active reservations' `quantity`, available-to-sell is the derived difference (never stored — the reserved/safety-field accounting depth is SPEC-6-13's), expiry = `expires_at`, owner/reference = the `owner` + `order` FKs. Lifecycle writers are NOT here: SPEC-12-02 (create in create_order's atomic block, convert in verify_payment's locked block) and SPEC-12-03 (reconciler). `orders/views.py` untouched (byte-frozen regions); no admin registration (§12.1 prescribes none).
- `products/models.py` (+104): `StockReservation` — `Status` TextChoices {active, converted, released, expired} (§12.1's recommended-checkout vocabulary: minted active step 3, converted step 5, released step 6, expired step 7); FKs `product` (products, CASCADE), `order` ("orders.Order" — the file's existing cross-app string-FK pattern, cf. StockMovement.created_by, no orders import at model load), `owner` ("auth.User", CASCADE); `quantity` PositiveBigIntegerField (shares the products.stock / StockMovement.stock_after unit domain); `status` CharField(20, choices, default active); `expires_at`; created/updated timestamps. Meta: UniqueConstraint (order, product) `uniq_order_product_reservation` — §12.1 implies it (reserved inventory is a per-checkout hold with an owner/reference; a duplicate row would double-count reserved stock, and a retried checkout must re-target the existing hold, not mint a second); composite Index (status, expires_at) `stockres_status_expires_idx` serving the reconciler's status=active AND expires_at<=now sweep. Classmethod `expiry_from_now()` reads settings.RESERVATION_TTL at call time (ops/services.py:9 pattern — env-driven, never frozen at import).
- `products/migrations/0009_stockreservation.py` (generated, +37): CreateModel with both constraint/index options, dependency on orders 0014 + auth user model. Migration dance on a scratch sqlite DB via DATABASE_URL env var, hermetic: phase 1 full forward `migrate` (0009 applied, exit 0), phase 2 reverse `migrate products 0008` (unapplied, exit 0), phase 3 re-apply `migrate products` (exit 0), plus `showmigrations` confirming 0009 [X]; scratch DB deleted.
- `config/settings.py` (+9): `RESERVATION_TTL = _env_int('RESERVATION_TTL', 900)` — seconds a checkout hold survives before it goes stale and the reconciler (SPEC-12-03) may release it; documented as coordinated with the payment-provider session window (customer must be able to finish paying inside the hold); fail-safe parse per the house pattern.

- `products/tests.py` (+254/-2): `StockReservationModelTests` (11 tests) — field-set pin; status vocabulary/default pin; FK targets + cascade policy + reverse accessors; (order, product) uniqueness pinned twice (forced-collision IntegrityError — savepoint-wrapped so the TestCase transaction survives — and live DB constraint introspection, the ProductVariant.sku/Order.order_number method); cross-order independent holds with Σ active reserved; reconciler sweep exact on both axes (stale active matched; fresh active and converted/released/expired with past expiry not); quantity column-type parity with products.stock; Meta constraint/index pin; TTL read at call time (override 60→120 moves the expiry); `_env_int` fail-safe parse (absent → 900, malformed → 900, set → parsed); `__str__` shape.
