## 2026-09-21 - SPEC-12-02 (Section 12) - builder: checkout reservation lifecycle — create at checkout, convert at verify, release on failed/cancelled paths, deterministic product-lock ordering ([R-12.6]/[R-12.7]/[R-12.8])

- Spec reading (§12 lines 3579-3633): §12.2's "single atomic operation that succeeds only when sufficient stock is available" maps to the CONVERSION/decrement step (the stock-consuming moment), which stays one locked atomic op — sufficiency re-check + decrement + SALE ledger row under product row locks, byte-unchanged as the oversell backstop. Reservation MINTING is deliberately lock-free and advisory (§12.1 step 3 follows step 2's validation): two concurrent last-unit checkouts both mint (soft overbooking, the section-12 verified-facts by-design pay-then-409 residual) and the verify backstop 409s the loser — hard oversell stays IMPOSSIBLE, no check-then-act introduced (R-12.12).
- Declared judgment calls: (1) conversion filters `status=active` ONLY — a hold released by a failed attempt is terminal and is never resurrected into a sale (the 10-04 retry re-checks stock cleanly with no conversion); (2) a LAPSED TTL is NOT a conversion gate — §12.2 never makes TTL an availability authority and §12.1 step 5 converts on successful confirmation; expiry belongs to the SPEC-12-03 reconciler; a captured payment proceeds on the re-checked stock (also pins legacy/no-hold orders verify unchanged); (3) NO StockMovement rows on release/convert — §12.2's text demands none at conversion time; the existing [6.5.17] SALE movement on decrement is kept as-is; release/expiry reconciliation via the ledger is SPEC-12-03's; (4) release sites = the four failed/cancelled checkout paths in scope: 10-04 failed-verify (same transaction as the failed write), stock-conflict 409 (race loser), coupon-conflict 409, admin API cancel. AMENDED (audit cycle 2): the cycle-1 build had declared the admin SURFACE cancel (admin.py `cancel_pending`/change form) out of file scope, leaning on the SPEC-12-03 reconciler for any stale holds; audit cycle 1 returned FIX P1 [R-12.8] — §12.1 step 6 makes "cancelled" a release trigger distinct from "expired", so both admin cancel writers now release too (see the admin.py bullet) and the reconciler stays a pure TTL sweep.
- `orders/views.py` (+112/-2, two declared non-protected line rewrites, everything else insertion-only; all byte-frozen regions — 21-1, 8-01, 8-02b, 8-03, 8-04, SPEC-7-02, R-7.20 — untouched): import line gains `StockReservation` (rewrite #1); `_mint_order_reservations(order, lines, user)` helper above `create_order` — per-line mint inside the caller's atomic block (rollback-together), savepoint-wrapped create so a lost (order, product) race rolls back only the insert, IntegrityError fallback RE-TARGETS the surviving hold (quantity re-armed, fresh TTL, active again) instead of double-creating or crashing — the constraint is the concurrency authority, no check-then-act; `create_order` calls it after the snapshot loop (still inside the atomic block, before the frozen R-7.20 hunk). `verify_payment`: product lock acquisition now `.order_by("id")` (rewrite #2 — closes the section-12 deadlock advisory: one total acquisition order across concurrent verifies, no more rollback-500 loser); conversion insert before the decrement loop (active→converted, idempotent, replay-gated upstream by the already-processed check); releases inserted on the 10-04 failure branch (inside the guarded write), the stock-409 branch and the coupon-409 branch (before their byte-identical returns). `admin_order_cancel`: release inserted inside the atomic block after the audit event (the idempotent replay returns before the site).
- `orders/tests.py` (+389/-2 cumulative): `ReservationLifecycleTests` (12 tests) — per-line active hold with env-driven TTL (override 120s); dedup-replay collapses without touching the hold (re-target-not-double-create at guard level); lost-race re-target via a REAL duplicate mint against the live unique constraint (quantity/TTL/status re-armed, still exactly one row); verify converts + SALE movement pin + replay cannot double-convert; lapsed-TTL conversion; legacy no-hold order verifies; failed-verify releases + retry captures with the hold staying released and stock decremented exactly once; stock-409 releases + restock retry re-checks cleanly; coupon-409 releases; admin cancel releases; AMENDED (audit cycle 2): admin change-form cancel releases the hold + the cancelled self-transition replay never resurrects it; admin bulk cancel_pending releases the hold + a re-run never resurrects it (fixtures check out BEFORE admin login — login() rotates the session key the cart is bound to).
- `orders/admin.py` (+21, audit cycle 2 — BUG-001 P1 fix; views.py untouched): `StockReservation` import + the views.py release idiom (order-scoped `filter(status=ACTIVE).update(status=RELEASED)` — a no-op on already-released holds, so double-cancel cannot resurrect) inserted into BOTH cancel writers inside their EXISTING `transaction.atomic()` blocks, after the transition+audit row and before the side-effect hook: change-form `save_model`'s cancelled-transition branch and `cancel_pending`'s per-row loop — the admin surfaces now match `admin_order_cancel` exactly. `_bulk_set_status` registers no "cancelled" action — no third site.
- `tests/test_e2e_concurrency.py` (+19/-1 cumulative): the oversell race test now also pins the race through the reservation lens — winner's hold converted, loser's released by the 409 backstop, zero active holds survive (import line gains `StockReservation`); audit cycle 2 wraps the 91-col assert continuation to the Black 88-col limit (no behavior change).
- Race/lock semantics: creation lock-free (cart/user row locks already serialize same-session/keyed submissions; the (order, product) constraint backstops the rest); conversion + releases run under the existing Order→Products→Coupon lock order inside verify's atomic block and are single `QuerySet.update()` statements filtered on active; product locks are now acquired in ascending-id order, killing the cross-cart deadlock advisory; no new lock-order cycle (create_order still locks only Cart/User).
- Suite: **638 tests, OK (632 pass + 12 new + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (2511 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 2490), `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change — no migration). Audit cycle 2: pre-flight AND post-fix full suites both exit 0.

## 2026-09-21 - SPEC-12-03 (Section 12) - builder: expire_reservations management command - scheduled stale-reservation reconciliation via the StockMovement ledger ([R-12.9])

- Spec reading (§12 lines 3579-3633, §12.1 step 7): the reconciler's vocabulary is EXPIRED - the 12-01 Status transition map assigns step 7 to EXPIRED while RELEASED is step 6's in-flow vocabulary (owned by the 12-02 checkout/admin writers), so the sweep flips `status=active AND expires_at past -> expired` (`expires_at__lte`: the hold's TTL instant arrived; tests backdate by minutes so the lte/lt boundary is a declared reading, not a pinned behavior). A lapsed TTL remains a non-gate for verify's conversion (12-02's declared call): a swept checkout's late verify still lands on the re-checked stock - sweep and verify never resurrect each other's rows (conversion filters active; the sweep's UPDATE re-filters active+expiry).
- Ledger reading ([6.5.17] idiom consistency): each affected product lands ONE StockMovement row per sweep with delta=0 BY DESIGN - a hold never left on-hand stock (it gated available-to-sell only; the SALE decrement is verify_payment's conversion), so delta=+qty would corrupt the ledger arithmetic (stock_after = before + delta); the row records reserved units returning to the pool: reason=CORRECTION, stock_after gated on the locked on-hand, order refs in the note, created_by=None (system actor, the SALE idiom). No new Reason choice - choices edits generate an AlterField migration and this task ships none.
- Idempotency/locking: snapshot read (status+expires_at, served by `stockres_status_expires_idx`) -> per product in ascending pk: `select_for_update()` the product row (stock_after gates on the real on-hand; lock order identical to verify_payment's -> no lock-order inversion), then an atomic conditional UPDATE re-filtering status AND expires_at at flip time - a hold re-armed by a checkout retry (fresh TTL, the _mint_order_reservations IntegrityError path) or released by a concurrent writer no longer matches: never double-released, never resurrected; flipped==0 writes no phantom movement. Whole batch in one `transaction.atomic()`.
- `products/management/__init__.py` + `commands/__init__.py` (new, empty): the tree had no management commands yet (no in-repo precedent); BaseCommand style, verbosity-respected stdout, no args, no return value (call_command treats a non-None handle() result as output text).
- `products/management/commands/expire_reservations.py` (+113 new): the sweep + module docstring carrying the coordination prose.
- `tests/test_expire_reservations.py` (+208 new, 6 tests): zero-stale no-op (live hold survives, ledger untouched, verbosity-0 prints nothing); expired hold -> EXPIRED + one delta-0 CORRECTION row (stock_after=current on-hand, order ref in note, created_by None, on-hand unchanged); live + CONVERTED/RELEASED terminal holds untouched (no resurrection); re-run idempotent (exactly one ledger row across two sweeps); one row per product referencing every order + per-product stock_after pin; mid-sweep re-arm race (manager shim simulates the concurrent re-target between snapshot and flip -> raced hold survives unreleased with no phantom row while the clean product's release proceeds).
- `.env.example` (+12): SPEC-12-03 cadence note under RESERVATION_TTL - schedule `python manage.py expire_reservations` well inside the TTL (cron/celery-beat; scheduler wiring owned by S19/S22, none built here); coordination rules: TTL > payment-provider checkout-session window, sweep cadence < TTL; the sweep reclaims the reservation book only - payment truth (webhook/timeout reconciliation, SPEC-1-06/SPEC-1-18) is never touched. No new settings key (cadence is scheduler-side, not app config). orders/views.py and orders/admin.py untouched (protected regions).
- Suite: **644 tests, OK (640 pass = 634 prior + 6 new, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (2543 stmts, 0 miss - grew from 2511), `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change - no migration).

## 2026-09-21 — SPEC-17-01 (Section 17) — builder: JWT lifecycle — SIMPLE_JWT config, token-blacklisting logout, session invalidation on password reset [R-17.5/R-17.8/R-17.10]

Per section-17.md SPEC-17-01 row and BACKEND_REQUESTS.md [P1] "SIMPLE_JWT configuration" (the ledger notes this task matches that contract — verified: the suggested contract's 15min+/7d/ROTATE/BLACKLIST shape is exactly what shipped).
- `config/settings.py` — `rest_framework_simplejwt.token_blacklist` added to INSTALLED_APPS (app ships its own migrations 0001–0013; none for our apps). New `SIMPLE_JWT` block: `ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME` = env `JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME_SECONDS` (default 900s = 15 min), `REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME` = env `JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME_SECONDS` (default 604800s = 7 days), both integer seconds via the `_env_int` fail-safe pattern; `ROTATE_REFRESH_TOKENS = True` + `BLACKLIST_AFTER_ROTATION = True` — every refresh mints a fresh refresh token and the presented one is blacklisted, so a leaked refresh token cannot be replayed. requirements.txt untouched (simplejwt 5.5.1 already pinned, line 5).
- `accounts/views.py` — new `LogoutView` (DRF APIView, `permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]`, 'auth' throttle scope): POST with body `{"refresh": "<token>"}` blacklists the presented refresh token (200); missing/non-string/empty `refresh` → 400 field error; invalid/expired token → 400; anonymous → 401 (nothing to revoke, no token-validity oracle). New `_blacklist_user_refresh_tokens(user)` hook [R-17.10 "Session invalidation after critical account changes"]: blacklists every outstanding refresh token for the user, idempotent via `get_or_create`, called inside `reset_password`'s existing `transaction.atomic()` block — old sessions die exactly when the credential change lands. No email-change endpoint exists in the accounts surface today (verified against views.py/urls.py), so the hook is wired at the only critical-change site; it is user-generic and ready for any future change endpoint (declared in the builder report).
- `accounts/urls.py` — `logout/` route (serves both `/api/accounts/logout/` and the v1 alias `/api/v1/account/logout/` via the shared urlconf include).
- `.env.example` (+12) — the two JWT lifetime keys with defaults and the coordinate-the-pair note (access = stolen-bearer usability bound; refresh = sign-in duration bound).
- `accounts/tests.py` (+11 tests: config pin ×3, logout ×4 incl. anonymous-401 permission test, rotation/invalidation ×1, reset-hook ×3 incl. bystander-isolation) and `tests/test_settings_security.py` (+4: env-override lifetimes, default lifetimes, malformed-value fallback, rotation/blacklist flags + app presence — subprocess, clean environment). Throttle wiring guard extended: `logout` joins the pinned scope map.

Per the section-17.md SPEC-17-02 row and spec §17.1 lines 4267–4269 ("use secure, HttpOnly cookies where appropriate ... Do not store long-lived authentication tokens in browser local storage by default"). Cookie contract: `Set-Cookie: refresh_token=<jwt>; Path=/api/; HttpOnly; Secure (when not DEBUG); SameSite=Lax; Max-Age=<REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME>`. SameSite reading declared: **Lax** — the cookie's only consumers are POST endpoints (refresh/logout), which Lax never serves cross-site, so the cookie itself is CSRF-immune without Strict's navigation stranding; it matches the same-site frontend/API deployment constraint BACKEND_REQUESTS.md [P1] already documents for the session cookie, and explicit CSRF enforcement for cookie-authenticated mutations stays SPEC-17-03's follow-up. All three knobs env-driven (`JWT_REFRESH_COOKIE_NAME/PATH/SAMESITE`).
- `config/settings.py` (+16) — the three cookie settings with documented defaults (Path `/api/` covers both mounts of accounts/urls.py: legacy `/api/accounts/` and v1 `/api/v1/account/`; Secure follows DEBUG, fail-closed per V-02).
- `accounts/views.py` (+122/-13) — `_set_refresh_cookie(response)`: moves the minted refresh token out of the JSON body (the JS-readable leak surface) into the HttpOnly cookie, max-age synced to the token lifetime so the browser never presents a cookie older than the token it holds; `_clear_refresh_cookie(response)`: expires it in place with matching Name+Path. `LoginView.post` wires the helper on success (audit trail unchanged); `RefreshView` moved here from urls.py: body token still accepted for non-browser API clients, otherwise the cookie token is merged into the parsed JSON body in place (DRF 3.18 exposes no `request.data` setter; immutable QueryDict bodies excluded — the browser client is JSON-only); on success the ROTATED token re-enters the cookie, never the body; `handle_exception` override sweeps the cookie on a 401 authentication verdict only (429 throttle refusals are not a verdict on the token — the cookie must survive shared-budget refusals). `LogoutView.post`: cookie is the token source for browser clients (body fallback kept for API clients), blacklist + cookie clear on 200; a garbage/dead cookie token gets the same 400 field error (envelope shape unchanged) but the cookie is swept so a dead token cannot pin the browser.
- `accounts/urls.py` (-14) — `RefreshView` now imported from views (cookie logic lives with the views); routes unchanged.
- `.env.example` (+13) — the three cookie knobs, the mount-coverage note for PATH, the Secure-follows-DEBUG rule, and the tighten-to-Strict caveat.
- `frontend/src/lib/tokens.ts` — `REFRESH_STORAGE_KEY`/`aurel.refresh` localStorage persistence deleted; `setTokens(access)` is memory-only; `getStoredRefreshToken` removed; `doRefresh` posts an empty body with `credentials: 'include'` (the cookie is the credential) and clears the session state on a server rejection (transient network failures keep it). New `aurel.session` localStorage key is a NON-SECRET boolean hint (never a token) gating the boot-time refresh probe so anonymous page loads don't burn the 'auth' throttle budget.
- `frontend/src/lib/auth.tsx` — login consumes `{access}` only (no body refresh); bootstrap probes refresh when the hint says a cookie may exist; logout now calls `POST /api/accounts/logout/` (auth'd, best-effort) so the backend blacklists the cookie token and clears the cookie — the browser can no longer present the token itself. `api.ts` untouched: the refresh-call credentials path was already `include` and the X-CSRFToken slot stays 17-03's scope.
- `frontend/src/lib/api.test.ts` (+8/-8) — the three silent-refresh tests re-pinned from localStorage seeding to memory token + hint, plus cookie-contract pins (credentials include, empty body, no token persisted); `tokens.test.ts` (new, 7 tests) pins the storage contract: no token value ever written to localStorage (only the "1" hint), memory-only access token, hint lifecycle (set on session, cleared on server-rejected refresh, kept on network failure), single-flight, empty-body refresh shape.
- `accounts/tests.py` (+9 tests / 1 re-pinned): cookie flags on login (HttpOnly/Secure-DEBUG-False/SameSite/Path/max-age-synced, body `refresh` dropped) incl. the DEBUG=True Secure-off flip; refresh-via-cookie rotation (rotated token re-set in cookie, body access-only, original blacklisted, session continuity, dead-cookie sweep); refresh without cookie or body → 400; logout-with-cookie blacklist + clear; garbage-cookie logout reject + sweep. `RefreshRotationTests` re-pinned to the cookie carrier (17-01 semantics preserved: body-presented token still rotates + blacklists); `test_login_rate_limit_engages` re-pinned from body-refresh to cookie. Settings-defaults pin class added.
- Suite: **668 tests, OK (664 pass = 656 prior + 8 net new accounts tests, 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged — ceiling held)**, coverage **100.00%** (2592 stmts, 0 miss — grew from 2567), `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change). Frontend: vitest **31 tests passed (6 files)**, `next build` exit 0.

## 2026-09-21 - SPEC-17-03 (Section 17) - builder: CSRF enforced on session-cookie cart/checkout mutations; SPA X-CSRFToken slot activated ([R-17.18])

- Spec reading: section 17.3 risk table, CSRF row (lines 4318-4322) "Appropriate CSRF protection for cookie-based state-changing requests" + 17.1 line 4267 ("Carefully configure SameSite, HTTPS and CSRF protection based on the authentication architecture"); the exploitable surface is BACKEND_REQUESTS.md [P1] lines 43-48 (live-confirmed: POST/DELETE on /api/cart/ with only the session cookie and no token returned 201/200).
- Enforcement mechanism chosen: `common/authentication.py` (new) - `SessionCartCSRFAuthentication(SessionAuthentication)` in DEFAULT_AUTHENTICATION_CLASSES AFTER `JWTAuthentication`. Why a subclass rather than stock SessionAuthentication: DRF 3.18.1's stock class returns None for anonymous users ("Unauthenticated, CSRF validation not required"), which would have left the GUEST session cart - the exact live-confirmed exploit - unprotected; the subclass extends `enforce_csrf` to every request that presents the session cookie (named user or anonymous guest) while returning None in all paths (a pure gate: JWT stays the only credential in the chain, so no permission surface changes and bearer requests short-circuit the chain before the gate - an Authorization header is not an ambient credential, nothing to forge). Requests with no session cookie (fresh visitor's first mutation, non-browser API clients, fresh login) are ungated by design.
- CSRF-cookie issuance reading: `cart/views.py` `cart_detail` GET decorated `@ensure_csrf_cookie` - GET /api/cart/ is the SPA's boot call (Header's cart badge react-query runs it on every page load), so the browser holds the csrftoken cookie before the first gated mutation and api.ts replays it as X-CSRFToken (double-submit). `config/urls.py` untouched.
- Cookie-flag reading: `SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE` now explicit, env-driven, default 'Lax' - aligned with 17-02's JWT_REFRESH_COOKIE_SAMESITE reading (cross-site POSTs cannot attach the cookie; the server-side gate is the enforcement, SameSite is the same-site belt). CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS already env-driven (pre-existing) covers HTTPS Referer/Origin checking for the deployed storefront origin.
- Frontend: the api.ts X-CSRFToken slot (lines 29-39, 118-121) was discovered ALREADY WIRED (reads the csrftoken cookie, sends the header on POST/PATCH/PUT/DELETE) - 17-03's frontend work was therefore: exported `csrfTokenFromCookie` from api.ts (stale "backend never issues one" docstring rewritten), and `tokens.ts` `doRefresh` now attaches the same header - closing a real 17-02 interplay gap: a browser that built a guest cart sends its sessionid cookie on the refresh POST too, so without the header the gate would 403 the refresh and spuriously log the user out.
- `tests/test_csrf_security.py` (SPEC-21-2 security-truth layer) FLIPPED per its own instruction ("when SPEC-17-03 lands ... updating this module is the deliberate contract update, not an accident"; same pattern as the V-02 flip): CSRF half now pins the enforced truth (tokenless enforcing client -> 403, valid pair -> 201, cookie issuance on the cart GET, suppression-flag differential), wiring pin asserts [JWTAuthentication, SessionCartCSRFAuthentication], JWT-bearer money-path pins re-documented as designed behavior (unchanged assertions); XSS half untouched.
- `cart/test_csrf.py` (new, 11 tests): Django's hermetic CSRF recipe (enforce_csrf_checks=True + token pair acquired through the API); guest enforcement (add no-token 403 with NO CartItem written / valid token 201 / mismatched token 403 / PATCH+DELETE enforced); orders-family apply-coupon covered by the global chain WITHOUT touching orders/views.py; fresh-visitor first mutation ungated; Django-session-authenticated user enforced (stock half); JWT-bypass pin (login -> session cart -> checkout 201 with no CSRF header); 17-02 interplay pin (login/refresh/logout all 200 with session cookie present + header); fresh login without any cookie ungated.
- `tests/test_settings_security.py` (+2, subprocess pattern): SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE defaults to 'Lax' and honors the env override.
- `.env.example` (+11): SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE block - the belt-vs-gate reading, the GET /api/cart/ issuance contract, JWT-never-gated note, CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS deployment note.
- Suite: **681 tests, OK (677 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (2707 stmts, 0 miss - grew from 2592), `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change). Frontend: vitest **36 tests passed (6 files, +5 CSRF pins)**, `next build` exit 0.

## 2026-09-21 - SPEC-17-05 (Section 17) - builder: mandatory TOTP MFA for privileged roles at the admin door + staff API login ([R-17.9])

- Spec reading: 17.1 line 4263 "Staff MFA, preferably mandatory for privileged roles" - a single line naming no mechanism, no artefact and no package (no QR image anywhere in the section), so the factor is built on a stdlib RFC 6238 TOTP implementation (hmac/hashlib/base64/secrets, ~90 lines, pinned against the public RFC 6238 SHA-1 appendix-B vectors) plus an otpauth:// enrollment URI STRING (not a QR image). No dependency was added. Privileged = `staff.manage` capability holders (`admin` role per CAPABILITY_ROLES) plus Django superusers - the trust anchor every admin surface preserves a bypass for must not escape the factor that anchors it (`common/permissions.py`: `is_privileged` + `IsPrivilegedRole`).
- `common/totp.py` (new): RFC 4226 HOTP (SHA-1, dynamic truncation) under the RFC 6238 30s step, 160-bit base32 secret generation, `otpauth://` URI (issuer:account label, literal colon delimiter), and `verify_code` with a +-1 step drift window and the RFC 6238 section 5.2 replay guard (a counter at or below the stored watermark never verifies again). Step/digits/window are code constants on purpose: a deployment-tunable drift between server and enrollment URI would silently break every staff login.
- `accounts/models.py`: `TOTPDevice` (OneToOne user, base32 secret, enabled flag, confirmed_at, last_used_counter replay watermark) + the shared user-facing MFA messages. Enforcement gate = enabled AND confirmed; setup mints an unconfirmed disabled row. Migration `accounts/0001_initial`. Secret exposure discipline: returned exactly once by setup, never by status/confirm/disable, never in `__str__`, never logged.
- `accounts/serializers.py` + `accounts/views.py`: `LoginView` now runs `MFATokenObtainPairSerializer` - privileged users must present a valid `totp` code in the login body (BACKEND_REQUESTS.md defines no MFA contract; the simpler single-request field was chosen and declared); missing code -> 400 "Enter your authentication code.", wrong/expired/replayed -> 400, unenrolled privileged -> 400 with the enrollment path named; every rejection lands in the existing LoginView audit path as AUTH_LOGIN_FAILED and no tokens are ever minted without the factor. Non-privileged logins (customers, the five non-admin staff roles) are byte-identical to before, extra `totp` field ignored.
- Enrollment API (all 'auth'-throttled, mounted in both route families via the shared urlconf): `GET /api/accounts/mfa/status/` (privileged-only, `{enabled}` only), `POST /mfa/setup/` (secret + otpauth URI shown once; re-enrollment while a device is active re-proves the factor), `POST /mfa/confirm/` (valid code flips enabled), `POST /mfa/disable/` (valid code strips the factor). Setup/confirm run a DUAL trust path, declared: the privileged JWT session (steady state) OR a credential bootstrap (username+password) available exactly while no device is active - the rollout path that keeps blocked-at-login enforcement reachable, because a privileged account with no device can never obtain a JWT (industry-standard first-factor-enrollment window: once a device is active, credentials alone are refused with 403; an attacker with only a password is exactly where the pre-MFA world was, and strictly worse off against every enrolled account).
- Admin surface: `config/admin.py` (new) swaps the default admin site via `MFAAdminConfig.default_site` -> `MFAAdminSite` whose login form is `MFAAdminAuthenticationForm` (`accounts/admin.py`): privileged users need a valid TOTP code, unenrolled privileged accounts are refused with the enrollment path named, `login_form` resolves lazily to avoid re-entering accounts.admin during app-registry bootstrap. `templates/admin/login.html` overrides the stock template solely to render the code field (stock template hardcodes username/password rows only); kept byte-faithful otherwise. `config/settings.py`: `django.contrib.admin` -> `config.admin.MFAAdminConfig` (same app label, same registration flow, all existing `admin.site._registry` pins hold).
- Rollout semantics (declared): mandatory means mandatory - an unenrolled privileged account is blocked at BOTH surfaces until enrolled, with the enrollment path named in the error; recovery is self-service through the credential bootstrap (setup -> confirm with the fresh secret's code). Disable under a mandatory policy blocks the next password-only login until re-enrollment (pinned by test).
- `common/testing.py`: `api_login` lazily ensures the fixture device (RFC vector secret, recognizably a test constant) and attaches a fresh code, rewinding the replay watermark one step so the suite's staff logins survive the replay guard across the 30s window; `make_staff` unchanged.
- `accounts/tests.py`: URLConf throttle-wiring guard's expected route->scope map extended with the four MFA routes ('auth').
- `tests/test_mfa.py` (new, 52 tests): RFC 6238 vectors (8- and 6-digit) + every verify/replay branch; privileged predicate matrix; enrollment happy/malicious paths on both trust paths; secret-shown-exactly-once pin; disable flow; API-login enforcement pins (missing/invalid/expired/replayed codes, watermark persistence, unenrolled block, audit trail, customer + non-admin-staff + superuser differentials); admin-login enforcement pins (field rendered, code required/invalid/unenrolled, same-code replay across clients, roleless-staff unaffected, superuser enforced); admin-site wiring pin.
- Frontend (minimal, backend-enforced contract): `LoginForm.tsx` + `lib/auth.tsx` gain one labeled optional "Authentication code" input (inputmode=numeric, autocomplete=one-time-code, WCAG-labelled) sent only when filled; a 400 `totp` field error surfaces its message (names the enrollment path). No e2e change (e2e exercises a customer login, unaffected).
- Suite: **733 tests, OK (729 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (2929 stmts, 0 miss - grew from 2707), `makemigrations --check` clean (accounts/0001_initial only). Frontend: vitest **36 tests passed**, `next build` exit 0.

## 2026-09-22 - SPEC-17-07 (Section 17) - builder: env-gated transport/cookie hardening flags [R-17.11]

- Spec reading: 17.1 "Carefully configure SameSite, HTTPS and CSRF protection based on the authentication architecture" - the settings carried ZERO SECURE_*/HSTS/cookie-secure flags (implicit Django defaults), so the hardening was unreachable from deployment config. Coordinates with the S22 loop, which owns the production TLS topology: this task makes the hardening REACHABLE, never on where it would break plain-HTTP local development.
- config/settings.py: new `_env_bool` helper (truthy spellings 1/true/yes/on, everything else falls back to the documented default - the same fail-safe pattern as `_env_int`, never crashing startup). Added: SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS (default 0 = header unset; enabling HSTS on a partly-HTTP host locks users out for the declared window), SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS/SECURE_HSTS_PRELOAD (default false), SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT (default false), SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER assembled from SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER_NAME/_VALUE - unconfigured unless BOTH parts are set, because a half-trusted scheme header is worse than none (a forgeable pair lets clients lie about their scheme). SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE/CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE default to `not DEBUG`, mirroring the JWT refresh cookie's secure=not-DEBUG contract in accounts/views.py so the three auth cookies can never disagree about transport security (the cart's csrftoken cookie and CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE flip together, keeping SPA form posts alive over HTTPS).
- tests/test_settings_security.py: TransportHardeningTests (8 tests) on the existing subprocess recipe - safe-off defaults, env-on flips, proxy header pair/half-configured, cookie-secure follows DEBUG for local dev, malformed HSTS seconds and malformed bool fall back to defaults. The truthy branch of `_env_bool` is covered in-process via the pure helper with a temporarily-set env var.
- .env.example: SPEC-17-07 block documenting every flag, the ordering constraints (preload effectively irreversible; subdomains only once every subdomain is HTTPS), the proxy-header trust warning, and the cookie-secure default rationale.
- Suite: **740 tests, OK (736 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (2943 stmts, 0 miss - grew from 2929), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-22 - SPEC-17-08 (Section 17) - builder: product image upload size cap [R-17.21]

- Spec reading: 17.3 application-security table row "File upload abuse -> Type/size validation, safe storage, scanning where appropriate". The type half was already covered (ImageField's Pillow check, products/models.py); the size half was absent - no cap existed on any surface.
- Entry surfaces found (both enforced): (1) the staff REST API - POST/PUT/PATCH /api/products/ accept `image` through ProductSerializer (DRF ModelSerializer field); (2) the admin - products.products add/change pages (django ModelForm via ProductAdmin's Image fieldset). There is no other upload path (no user-generated images, no other model carries an ImageField).
- products/models.py: `validate_image_size` model-field validator, so ONE enforcement point reaches both surfaces automatically (DRF copies model-field validators onto serializer fields; ModelForm validates them natively). Size is read via getattr(handle, "size") falling back to seek(0, SEEK_END)/tell - upload wrappers differ (BytesIO under the 2.5 MB in-memory threshold has no .size; TemporaryFile past it) and a naive .size read 500s the boundary case. Rejects with ValidationError -> 400 "Image exceeds the maximum upload size of N MB." for the API (uniform error envelope, `image` in details) and a form error on the admin page; rejected uploads write nothing to storage. Cap = MAX_UPLOAD_MB * 1024 * 1024, env-driven via the fail-safe `_env_int` pattern (default 5), settings.MAX_UPLOAD_MB.
- config/settings.py: MAX_UPLOAD_MB (5); .env.example documents it, the both-surfaces enforcement, and the web-server body-limit interplay (proxy/DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_MEMORY_SIZE fires first with its own 413).
- products/migrations/0010_alter_products_image: field-level validators are part of the model definition, so the alteration is generated and applied (no schema/data change, safe no-op at the database level).
- products/tests.py: ImageUploadSizeCapTests (6 tests) - oversized API rejection (400, image in details, no row created), at-cap pass (payload trimmed by the TINY_PNG header so the file body lands exactly on the limit), no-image passthrough, empty-value validator passthrough (covers the guard branch), env override tightening to 1 MB (message reflects the new cap), admin form rejection (re-render with the error, image column stays empty).
- Suite: **746 tests, OK (742 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (2959 stmts, 0 miss - grew from 2943), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-22 - SPEC-17-06 (Section 17) - builder: JSON-LD script-context escaping + baseline security headers [R-17.17]

- Spec reading: 17.3 table row "XSS -> Output encoding, safe rich-text handling, CSP" (R-17.17). Two halves shipped together: the sink (staff-controlled product.name/description embedded into two <script type="application/ld+json"> blocks via raw JSON.stringify on the product page) and the missing CSP/headers baseline.
- frontend/src/lib/json-ld.ts (new): `toJsonLdScriptContent` - JSON.stringify then escapes `<` `>` `&` U+2028 U+2029 as their \uXXXX forms. `</script>` (and `<!--` openers) in a product name can no longer terminate the script element early; the escapes are JSON-transparent (JSON.parse decodes them back to the identical string), so the LD+JSON payload is semantically unchanged for crawlers. `&` is included because HTML-entity decoding inside script-adjacent contexts can otherwise re-introduce `<`.
- frontend/src/app/products/[slug]/page.tsx: both JSON.stringify call sites (product + breadcrumb JSON-LD) route through the helper. frontend/src/app/page.tsx: the homepage Organization JSON-LD also routes through it (corrected in audit cycle 2 — raw JSON.stringify here was missed in cycle 1, so the ld+json sinks closed by this task are TWO: products/[slug] product + breadcrumb blocks, AND the homepage Organization block; grep re-verified those are all of them).
- frontend/src/lib/json-ld.test.ts (new, 5 tests): literal `</script><script>alert("xss")</script>` product name serializes with no `</script>`/`<script` in the output while JSON.parse recovers the exact payload; `a<b>c&d` escapes to the exact expected string; U+2028/U+2029 cases; nested-object/list traversal; innocent payloads byte-identical to plain JSON.stringify. Audit cycle 2 adds a homepage-payload escape pin (6 tests total), mirroring page.tsx's Organization payload shape.
- frontend/next.config.ts: headers() applying to every route - CSP baseline `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; font-src 'self'; img-src 'self' <API origin> data:; connect-src 'self' <API origin>; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests`. Choice declared: 'unsafe-inline' stays in script-src because Next's App Router injects inline bootstrap scripts that cannot be nonced without forking the document render; fonts are self-hosted via next/font (no fonts.googleapis.com connect/style needed); API origin (env-driven NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL) is added to img-src/connect-src because product media and all fetches target it. Plus X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin, X-Frame-Options: DENY (legacy-browser belt to frame-ancestors 'none'), Permissions-Policy camera/microphone/geolocation denied.
- Verification: vitest 41 passed (36 -> 41), `npm run build` green with the headers config active.

## 2026-09-23 - SPEC-19-2 (Section 19) - builder: admin alert dispatch reusing existing detection [R-19.15/20/21/27 alerting halves]

- Spec reading: §19.2 admin notifications; the ledger row scopes 19-2 to the ALERTING halves of R-19.15 (inventory attention), R-19.20 (repeated payment failures), R-19.21 (background/process failures surfacing as integration outages), R-19.27 (security-sensitive account changes) — the notification halves of R-19.3..14 stay with SPEC-1-12/SPEC-3-14 per the owner attributions below the table.
- backend/ops/alerts.py (new): five alert types with one dispatch function each — low_stock, out_of_stock, payment_failure_spike, security_alert, integration_outage. ALL sends ride the SPEC-19-1 single send path (`common.notifications.send_email`; zero new send_mail calls — the R-19.0 conformance point). Recipients = `ALERT_RECIPIENTS` env (comma-separated, parsed/deduped; EMPTY disables admin alerts entirely — the earlier staff@… fallback idea rejected as a hardcoded address, conventions.md). Dedupe = per-alert-type cooldown in the default cache: `ALERT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` (default 300s) — the /health/ probe and dashboard load are pollable triggers, so without the window a degraded store would email-bomb the staff inbox; suppressed sends are INFO-logged, not silent. Send failure = log-only (`logger.exception` on the dedicated `ops.alerts` channel), identical to dispatch's contract: an alert can never break the flow that tripped it.
- Declared spike rule ([R-19.20], spec names the alert class but no number): ≥ `PAYMENT_FAILURE_SPIKE_COUNT` (default 3) failed-payment attempts within trailing `PAYMENT_FAILURE_SPIKE_WINDOW_SECONDS` (default 300) fire one spike alert, then the cooldown applies (the condition stays true until the burst ages out). Detection reads the AuditEvent trail (`payment.signature_rejected/order_not_found/reference_mismatch/stock_conflict/coupon_invalid`) — the verify-failure branches already record exactly these beside their warnings, so orders/views.py stays untouched (frozen; the audit hook IS the existing seam).
- Trigger wirings (insertion at existing detection sites only): ops/services.py `check_stock_alerts()` (low/out-of-stock sets from the same LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD definition the dashboard shows; called from the dashboard view and exported for pollers), ops/views.py `health` (degraded → integration_outage alert, best-effort detail rendering — the error-envelope seam substitutes arbitrary 503 bodies, so the alert can never 500 the probe; the payload also carries the `stock_alert_checked` key (ops/services.py:141), always False in get_health's own output because the stock-breach dispatch is deliberately outside the probe — a raise inside it must never flip /health/ into a 503 — and fires beside the dashboard's `check_stock_alerts()` call and for pollers), accounts/views.py `reset_password` (security_alert beside the AUTH_PASSWORD_RESET audit hook, username-only identity line per the R-17.32 allowlist), ops/views.py `dashboard` (payment_failure_spike via ops/alerts.py `check_payment_failure_spike` — audit c1 BUG-1's production call site, wired beside `check_stock_alerts()` mirroring the stock-alert poll symmetry; the detector (ops/alerts.py) reads the AuditEvent trail (`payment.signature_rejected/order_not_found/reference_mismatch/stock_conflict/coupon_invalid`) and skips the query entirely when recipients are unconfigured, so an unconfigured dashboard load stays cheap).
- templates/emails/alert_*.txt (new, 5): plain-text staff alert bodies. config/settings.py + .env.example: ALERT_RECIPIENTS / ALERT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS / PAYMENT_FAILURE_SPIKE_COUNT / PAYMENT_FAILURE_SPIKE_WINDOW_SECONDS documented.
- Tests: backend/tests/test_alerts.py (new, 21) — per-type fire + outbox assertions (locmem), recipients parsing/disabled-alerts, per-type cooldown pin + expiry + suppression logging, spike threshold/window-edge (out-of-window failures don't count), no-recipients negative paths (incl. spike query skipped), end-to-end password-reset → security alert, degraded /health/ → outage alert, send-failure log-only contract.
- Suite: **773 tests, OK (769 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3048 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-23 - SPEC-19-4 (Section 19) - builder: back-in-stock opt-in + restock trigger [R-19.11]

- Spec reading: §19.1 table "Back-in-stock event -> Optional opt-in notification" — the customer opt-in model, the REST opt-in/opt-out surface, and the trigger hook on stock edits; the trigger reuses existing detection (adjust_stock, the SPEC-6-02 sanctioned inventory mutation used by both the admin action and the REST inventory endpoint — zero edits to frozen orders/views.py).
- products/models.py — `RestockNotification` (new model): user FK + product FK (CASCADE), `active` flag, `notified_at` spend stamp, created/updated; `UniqueConstraint(user, product)` (`uniq_user_product_restock_optin`) as the concurrency authority (no check-then-act); composite index `(product, active, notified_at)` = the trigger's exact lookup. Lifecycle semantics: opt-in arms (active=True, notified_at NULL); the restock trigger emails once and stamps notified_at (spent); a sell-out crossing (stock positive -> 0, in adjust_stock) RE-ARMS spent rows (clears notified_at) so the next 0 -> positive restock re-mails — one email per stock cycle; explicit opt-out (active=False) never mails; re-opt-in re-arms.
- Trigger hook: `adjust_stock` fires `notify_back_in_stock` on the 0 -> positive crossing (old-stock condition checked first so the query only runs on real restocks) and `rearm_restock_notifications` on the positive -> 0 crossing — both inside adjust_stock's existing atomic block (rollback-together, the StockMovement pattern; a forced post-send failure rolls back stock, movement AND the spent stamp — pinned by test). Sends ride the SPEC-19-1 single send path (`common.notifications.send_email`, template `back_in_stock.txt` — no new send path); per-recipient send failures are logged on the dedicated `products.restock` channel and skip only that row's stamp (retried on the next trigger); the block never raises (notification outage cannot fail the inventory write, the alert/dispatch log-only contract).
- products/restock_views.py (new) — POST/DELETE `/api/products/<slug>/restock-notifications/`: `IsAuthenticated`, ownership = `request.user` filter (no body user field), `restock` throttle scope (`THROTTLE_RESTOCK_RATE`, default 5/min — recovery-shaped budget: each opt-in is intent to receive outbound email). Opt-in refused 400 while in stock (nothing to notify); accepted idempotently via get_or_create + re-arm on re-opt-in (201 created / 200 re-armed); DELETE deactivates with a uniform 200 whether or not the row exists (no existence leak). Wired in products/urls.py ahead of the slug catchall (mounted at /api/products/, /api/v1/store/products/, /api/... aliases — no new urlconf).
- Migration: products/migrations/0011_restocknotification.py (Create model RestockNotification + constraint + index). Dance: forward 0000->0011 on scratch sqlite, unapply 0011 (reverse drops the table), re-apply — all clean; scratch DATABASE_URL used, real db untouched.
- Tests: products/test_restock.py (new, 20) — opt-in/out lifecycle incl. auth (401), in-stock refusal, idempotent re-arm, uniform 404/DELETE, ownership scoping, unique-constraint IntegrityError pin, str; trigger: fires per armed opt-in on 0->positive (admin adjust_stock + REST inventory/adjustments paths), spent rows silent without sell-out, re-arm after sellout, non-crossing top-ups and sale decrements never fire, send-failure log-only + non-blocking for remaining recipients, rollback-takes-the-stamp, duplicate-send pin.
- Suite: **793 tests, OK (789 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3287 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-30 - BUG-1 (recovery) - builder: hermetic settings-import test harness (dotenv env leak) [P2]

- Seam: `config/settings.py` runs `load_dotenv()` at import (settings.py:21), so an untracked, git-ignored `backend/.env` is already merged into the test process's `os.environ` by the time `tests/test_settings_security.py:run_settings_import` builds its "clean" subprocess environment — the neutral `cwd` only stops the *child* from finding the file, it does not undo the parent's leak. A local `SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=false` / `CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE=false` therefore reached the child and `TransportHardeningTests.test_defaults_are_safe_off_for_development` observed `SESSIONSEC False` instead of the documented `not DEBUG` default (True) — a real security assertion failing on a clean checkout.
- Fix (test-harness only, no production change): the env scrub now also excludes a module-level `_LEAKED_ENV_NAMES` frozenset — `SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE`, `CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE`, `SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS`, `SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS`, `SECURE_HSTS_PRELOAD`, `SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT`, `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER_NAME`, `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER_VALUE`, `MAX_UPLOAD_MB`, `LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD`, `DASHBOARD_SALES_WINDOW_DAYS`, `DB_LOCAL_URL`, plus `DATABASE_URL` (the pre-existing exclusion, folded in) and the three other defaults this module pins in subprocesses (`JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME_SECONDS`, `JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME_SECONDS`, `SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE` — the last is set in the very `.env` that exposed the bug). Explicit `env_overrides` are still applied after the scrub, so every override/negotiation test still drives the child deliberately.
- Tests: the security assertion is untouched and passes; added `TransportHardeningTests.test_dotenv_leaked_flags_cannot_defeat_the_hardened_defaults`, which injects the leaking names into `os.environ` in-process and pins that the child still reports the hardened defaults. Verified it is a real pin: with the scrub bypassed both it and the original test fail (2 failures), with the scrub both pass. Suite: **798 tests, OK (794 pass + 4 pre-existing `expectedFailure` unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3292 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-11 (Section 20) - builder: SAFE inline admin stock editing on the changelist [R-20.35]

- Requirement: in the product changelist stock is inline-editable like price, but the SPEC-6-02 [6.5.17] invariant is unchanged and non-negotiable - "no silent inventory edits, every mutation path lands a StockMovement ledger row". Inline editing is the UX affordance; the ledger write is the contract. The existing get_readonly_fields (change form) and the deliberate adjust-stock action (SPEC-20-16) are untouched and still green.
- backend/products/admin.py: list_editable = ("price", "stock") + new ChangelistStockForm (the changelist formset's form, so the grid validates the cell with the model's own PositiveBigIntegerField rules). save_model is the single write path: the formset's own UPDATE is narrowed with update_fields to the inline columns the editor actually changed, ALWAYS minus stock, and the count is applied as a **delta** through products.adjust_stock (reason correction, note changelist inline stock edit) - which re-reads the row under select_for_update (conventions.md:16) and writes the StockMovement row with created_by = the acting staff user. No second, ledger-free writer exists: a raw absolute write would both break the invariant and clobber a concurrent payment-time sale.
- Capability gate: changelist_view withdraws the cell for anyone without inventory.adjust - the SAME capability the adjust-stock action rides, read off ction_capabilities via a new inline_stock_capability property so the two surfaces cannot drift. The narrowed list_editable lives on a copy.copy of the registered ModelAdmin (a process-wide singleton) so one request's role cannot leak into the next one's grid. Effective rule: the cell needs products.write (Django's own change permission) AND inventory.adjust - in the current roles map that is the dmin role and the superuser bypass; a hand-crafted orm-0-stock from catalogue (write, no adjust) is inert and writes no movement, and inventory (adjust, no write) is rejected 403 by Django before the formset runs.
- Tests: backend/tests/test_admin_inline_stock.py (new, 17) - inline cell renders for a capable role; form/list_editable drift pin; capability derivation pin; ledger pin (exactly one movement, delta 
ew - old not the absolute count, stock_after == persisted stock, reason/note/created_by); upward and downward edits; price+stock in one save; two rows in one save each landing their own row; below-zero and non-numeric cells rejected with zero movements; untouched cell writes nothing; the two gate paths (no cell + inert forged POST; 403 without change permission); singleton-leak pin; and two adjust-stock-action no-regression pins (ledger row with created_by, below-zero refusal).
- Two pins in backend/products/tests.py rewritten because they asserted the SUPERSEDED contract (that stock is not in list_editable and that a POSTed orm-0-stock moves nothing) - both now assert the SPEC-20-11 contract: the cell renders beside price, and a price-only inline save leaves stock and the ledger untouched. The change-form pins (	est_change_page_stock_is_display_only_add_page_keeps_input, 	est_change_form_save_ignores_tampered_stock) are unchanged and still green.
- Pins verified as real by mutation: replacing the delta route with a direct obj.stock = requested; obj.save() fails 6 of the new tests; removing the capability narrowing fails both gate tests.
- Suite: **815 tests, OK (811 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3323 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean.

- Requirement: an opening stock set when a product is created must PERSIST, on both entry surfaces.
