## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-13 (Section 20) - builder: MFA enrollment reach (admin link + enrollment page)
- User-visible bug: mandatory MFA ([R-17.9]) refuses an unenrolled privileged login at both doors, but nothing in any UI reached the enrollment endpoints — such an account was locked out of `/admin/` and the API with no way back in. Enrollment authorization is untouched (`_authorize_enrollment`, `auth` throttle, privileged-only, anonymous bootstrap credentials refused): this adds the missing REACH, never a new way in.
- `templates/admin/login.html` + `config/admin.py` (justified addition, no enforcement logic touched): the login page always links the enrollment surface, and says so loudly inside the error note when the R-17.9 block is the reason the attempt failed (`MFAAdminSite.login` flags `mfa_enrollment_blocked` off the form's non-field error, and `each_context` supplies `mfa_enroll_url`). `config/settings.py` + `.env.example`: `MFA_ENROLL_URL` (env-driven, defaults to `$FRONTEND_URL/staff/mfa/enroll`).
- frontend: new `src/app/staff/mfa/enroll/page.tsx` + `src/components/auth/MfaEnrollForm.tsx` drive the credential bootstrap the backend already allowed (setup -> secret + `otpauth_uri` -> confirm -> "continue to staff sign in"); new `src/app/staff/login/page.tsx` is the privileged door the flow completes at, and `LoginForm` grew a `variant` prop so the code field is named plainly there instead of behind the unknowable "(staff with MFA only)" hint. Pure request-shape/validation logic lives in `src/lib/mfa.ts` (no secret is ever stored or logged; the password stays in component state between the two steps, which the bootstrap window requires).
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_mfa.py` (+7, new `MFAEnrollmentReachTests`) — the login page always offers the enrollment URL and it is configuration (`override_settings`), the R-17.9 block shows the notice while a plain password failure does not, an authenticated visit redirects (no form to flag), the page never renders a secret, and the full reach end to end (password-proven setup -> confirm -> a login the factor now admits) with no JWT at any point. `frontend/src/lib/mfa.test.ts` (new, 9): both request shapes, the code normalisation/plausibility rules, and that no credential or secret is ever persisted.
- Suite: **828 tests, OK (824 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3338 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean; frontend `npm run test` 49 passed / `npm run build` green.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-9 (Section 20) - builder: scannable MFA enrollment QR from the provisioning URI

- Requirement: setup returned the `otpauth://` URI as a string, so enrolling meant transcribing a 32-character base32 secret by hand. Approach chosen: render the QR **server-side** and ship it inside the existing setup response — the enrollment surface is reached from the Django admin login page as well as from the storefront, and an inline base64 SVG needs no JS, no extra frontend dependency and no new endpoint (a QR endpoint would have had to re-disclose the secret or mint a token for it). The secret therefore still leaves the server exactly once, in one response, exactly as before.
- `common/qr.py` (new): the one place a QR encoder is allowed — `common/totp.py` stays stdlib-only, because the factor itself must not drag a dependency into the login path. New pinned dependency `segno==1.6.1` in `requirements.txt`: pure Python, no runtime dependencies, emits SVG without an image library, so enrollment does not depend on a rasterizer. Scale, the 4-module quiet zone and `m` error correction are code constants rather than env config, for the same reason the TOTP step/digits are not deployment-tunable.
- `accounts/views.py`: `MFASetupView` adds `qr_data_uri` beside `secret` and `otpauth_uri`, generated from that same response's provisioning URI. The picture is presentation only — every enforcement decision reads the secret and the URI — and nothing logs or returns it again.
- frontend: `MfaEnrollForm` renders the QR as an `<img src>` (the CSP already admits `data:` images) with the setup key and setup link ALWAYS beside it as the manual fallback, because plenty of authenticator flows cannot scan; `src/lib/mfa.ts`'s `qrImageSource()` accepts only the `data:image/svg+xml;base64,` prefix, so a foreign scheme can never reach an `<img src>` and an absent artifact degrades to typing rather than to a dead step.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_mfa.py` (new `MfaQrTests` + `MFAEnrollmentQrTests`, 6 tests) — the artifact is an inline SVG whose geometry is the QR matrix sized for THIS payload at the declared scale and quiet zone; it is byte-identical to an independent render of the provisioning URI the same response returns (so the view cannot have encoded some other string); it changes when one character of the secret changes (two users can never scan each other's code); it carries no plaintext secret in the markup; it rotates with a re-minted secret; and, like the secret, it is shown exactly once (absent from status, disable and the admin login page). `frontend/src/lib/mfa.test.ts` (+3): the artifact is rendered, and every foreign or missing scheme falls back to the manual key.
- Suite: **834 tests, OK (830 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3351 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean; frontend `npm run test` 52 passed / `npm run build` green.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-10 (Section 20) - builder: split customer vs privileged login so the storefront hides the TOTP field

- Bug: ONE `LoginView` served both populations and `MFATokenObtainPairSerializer` declared `totp` unconditionally, so every customer was shown an authentication-code input (hedged with an unknowable "(staff with MFA only)" hint) that no server check ever read. Split by DOOR, chosen over a request signal because a flag in the body would keep the field in every customer's validation surface, and over a header for the same reason (a client-controlled switch decides whether the factor applies is exactly the sort of thing an attacker would flip).
- `accounts/serializers.py`: new `StorefrontTokenObtainPairSerializer` declares the parent's explicit `username`/`password` and NOTHING else, so `totp` is not a field in either direction — the storefront form cannot render it and a body that includes it is ignored. It **refuses** a privileged account (`PermissionDenied`, 403, naming the staff door): a door with no factor to demand must not become the way around [R-17.9]. The refusal reveals nothing the caller has not already proven with a correct password.
- `accounts/views.py` + `accounts/urls.py`: `StorefrontLoginView(LoginView)` on `POST /api/accounts/storefront/login/` (also under `/api/v1/account/`). It inherits LoginView wholesale — same `auth` throttle, same audit events, same HttpOnly refresh cookie [R-17.12] — and differs only in `serializer_class`. `login/` keeps `MFATokenObtainPairSerializer`, so the privileged branch (is_privileged -> device -> verify -> counter consume) is byte-for-byte untouched; `PermissionDenied` was added to LoginView's audited-rejection tuple so the new refusal lands as AUTH_LOGIN_FAILED like every other.
- frontend: `useAuth().login(surface, ...)` picks endpoint and body from `lib/mfa.ts` (`loginEndpoint`/`buildLoginBody`) — the customer body can never grow a `totp` key even if a caller passes one. `LoginForm` renders the authentication-code input on the `staff` variant ONLY; the storefront variant has no MFA field at all, no more "(staff with MFA only)" hint, and customer-only links (register / resend / forgot) plus a "Staff sign in" link that gives a staff member who lands here the route to the door that does enforce the factor.
- Tests: ONE deliberate pin rewrite in `backend/tests/test_mfa.py` — `test_customer_totp_field_ignored` asserted the SUPERSEDED contract ("the storefront login SERVES a totp field and ignores its value") and is now `test_customer_door_has_no_totp_field` (the customer serializer's fields are exactly `password`/`username`). Its API-level behaviour is re-pinned on the customer door in the new `StorefrontLoginSplitTests` (8 tests): a posted `totp` is not a factor and mints no device, the refresh cookie stays out of the body, the wrong-password 401 stays uniform, a privileged account is refused and AUDITED, an unenrolled privileged one is refused too, a support-role staffer is untouched, and the staff door still refuses without a code, refuses a wrong one, admits a valid one and cannot spend the same code twice. New pins verified as real by mutation: deleting the refusal turns 2 of them red. No other test in this file was touched, weakened, skipped or marked expectedFailure; `accounts/tests.py`'s throttle-wiring map gained the new route.
- Suite: **843 tests, OK (839 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3362 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean; frontend `npm run test` 55 passed / `npm run build` green.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-8 (Section 20) - builder: per-device "trust this device" MFA with an env-driven 30-day TTL

- Requirement: R-17.9 demanded a fresh TOTP code on EVERY privileged login, which the user directive rejects as unacceptable. Approach chosen: the grant lives on the EXISTING `TOTPDevice` row (no new model) as `trusted_until`, and it only ever suppresses the challenge for the browser that asked for it. A cookie-only grant would have degraded MFA to password-only from anywhere for 30 days, which is the opposite of keeping enforcement strict.
- `accounts/models.py` + `accounts/migrations/0002_totpdevice_trusted_until.py`: one nullable `trusted_until` DateTimeField. NULL = never trusted = challenged every login, so the migration is purely additive and safe in the strict direction. `accounts/mfa_trust.py` (new): the only place that decides whether a login may skip the challenge -- `trust_deadline`/`grant_trust` (explicit `update_fields` write, secret and replay watermark untouched), `bind_trust_cookie` (HttpOnly, `SameSite=Strict`, max_age = the grant's REMAINING life) and `is_trusted_device` (requires BOTH an unexpired server-side grant and an unexpired, untampered `django.core.signing` marker naming this user AND this device row, under its own salt).
- `accounts/views.py` + `accounts/urls.py`: `MFATrustDeviceView` on `POST /api/accounts/mfa/trust/` (also under `/api/v1/account/`), gated `IsPrivilegedRole` + `'auth'` throttle like its siblings, and deliberately requiring a FRESH code through the same replay-guarded `_consume_code` -- so a stolen session cannot convert itself into thirty days of challenge-free logins, and granting trust spends a one-time code exactly once. `MFASetupView` (rotation) and `MFADisableView` now clear `trusted_until`: a rotated secret or a stripped factor is a different device state than the one that was trusted.
- `accounts/serializers.py`: the privileged branch gains ONE guard (`if not mfa_trust.is_trusted_device(...)`) around code; everything inside it -- require a code, `verify_code`, consume `last_used_counter` -- is byte-for-byte the pre-SPEC-20-8 path. Non-privileged logins and `StorefrontTokenObtainPairSerializer` are untouched (verified by diff). The admin login form keeps challenging every time: `accounts/admin.py` is not modified.
- `config/settings.py` + `.env.example`: `MFA_TRUST_DAYS` (env-driven int, default **30**, `0` is the documented kill switch that puts every privileged login back behind a code), `MFA_TRUST_COOKIE_NAME` (default `mfa_trusted_device`), `MFA_TRUST_COOKIE_SAMESITE` (default `Strict`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_mfa.py` (+20, new `MfaTrustDeviceTests`) -- a trusted device logs in with no code; another browser, another user's marker, another device row, a forged marker and a marker signed before the TTL are all still challenged; a valid-code login and a fresh enrollment grant nothing; the opt-in needs privilege + a session + a fresh code + an enrolled factor; the TTL is env-driven (`override_settings` 7 days, and 0 = immediate lapse), an expired grant returns the challenge and does NOT resurrect a code already spent; disable and re-enrollment both drop the trust; customers are untouched. `backend/tests/test_settings_security.py` (+2, `MfaTrustConfigTests`) pins the env knob itself in a clean subprocess: default 30, override 0/7, malformed falls back. Mutation-verified: dropping the expiry check, the (user, device) pair match, or the disable/re-enroll reset each turns pins red. `backend/accounts/tests.py`'s throttle-wiring map gained `mfa-trust` (exact-equality guard over every account route).
- Suite: **865 tests, OK (861 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3412 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean. Frontend untouched (the staff-side checkbox affordance is the follow-up task; the API is the opt-in surface this task ships).

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-8b (Section 20) - builder: admin login honors trust-this-device

- Closes the gap SPEC-20-8 left open (see its note "the admin login form keeps challenging every time"): `accounts/admin.py`'s `MFAAdminAuthenticationForm.clean` now consults the SAME skip decision as the staff serializer -- one guard, `mfa_trust.is_trusted_device(getattr(self, "request", None), user, device)`, placed AFTER the enrollment check (a disabled factor still demands enrollment) and BEFORE the code is demanded. The request arrives for free: `AdminSite.login` -> `LoginView` passes it to the form, so `self.request.COOKIES` is where the signed marker lives.
- `accounts/mfa_trust.py` is the single source of truth and stays the only implementation: `is_trusted_device` needed only a cookie jar, so a plain `HttpRequest` works exactly like the DRF request (no signature change, no second rule to drift) -- the one edit is a fail-safe `getattr(request, "COOKIES", None) or {}`, so a form built without a request is challenged instead of 500ing. Everything below the guard is the byte-identical pre-SPEC-20-8 challenge: require a code, `verify_code`, consume `last_used_counter`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_mfa.py` (+14, new `AdminMfaTrustDeviceTests`): a trusted unexpired device reaches `/admin/` with no fresh code and moves no watermark (a posted code is not a factor); another browser, a lapsed grant, a forged marker, another user's marker and `MFA_TRUST_DAYS=0` are all still challenged; an invalid code is still `MFA_CODE_INVALID` with a marker present, trust cannot resurrect a code the opt-in spent, disabling MFA still demands enrollment, and non-privileged staff / customers are exactly as before. Mutation-verified: removing the guard turns the 4 skip pins red while the 10 fail-safe pins stay green.
- Suite: **879 tests, OK (875 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3416 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check "No changes detected". Frontend untouched.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-1 (Section 20) - builder: the confirmation interstitial states amount, currency, affected items and resulting state

- Requirement: R-20.20 — a financially significant confirmation must show WHAT is being done, not just which rows. The delivered interstitial (`common/admin.py` + `templates/admin/action_confirmation.html`) rendered description + count + `str(obj)` only, so cancelling three orders to watch one of them disappear read as "3 affected".
- `common/admin.py`: the render path is factored into ONE `render_confirmation(...)` seam that every confirmation goes through; it carries a `details` payload supplied per action (`confirmation_details` returns `{}` by default, so any admin that declares nothing renders byte-identically to before) plus a `warning` sentence (default = the shipped prompt). `render_action_confirmation` is now a thin caller — the bulk-action gate (`get_actions` / `_confirm_first`), the superuser direct-execution bypass and the object-list render are unchanged.
- `templates/admin/action_confirmation.html`: renders the payload as a summary line plus one `<dl>` per selected row (Amount / Currency / Resulting state) and the row's affected item lines. The delivered description, `Affected (N)` list, hidden selection and `Yes, proceed` button all still render.
- `orders/admin.py`: `OrderAdmin.confirmation_details` builds that payload for `cancel_pending` only. Money stays `Decimal` end to end and the roll-up is quantized before rendering (conventions.md:15); currencies are listed from the rows, never assumed. Rows the action will NOT touch are spelled out too (`confirmed → unchanged (paid, never cancelled here)`) — skipping paid orders is part of what the operator is confirming.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_admin_rbac.py` (+2): `test_cancel_confirmation_names_amount_currency_items_and_state` pins the real values through the rendered page (summary roll-up, per-row Amount `Decimal` / Currency / Resulting state, the order item lines, and that nothing committed) and that the delivered `Affected (2)` render is intact; `test_confirmation_details_are_empty_without_a_declared_payload` pins the empty default for a non-declared action and for an admin that declares no payload at all. The pre-existing `test_cancel_pending_requires_explicit_confirmation`, the superuser-bypass pin and the audit pins pass unchanged.
- Suite: **881 tests, OK (877 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3435 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean (no model change).

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-2 (Section 20) - builder: explicit confirmation step on staff-role changes

- Requirement: R-20.18 — a staff-role add/remove commits directly in `save_model` with a per-direction audit and a `staff.manage` guard, but with no confirmation. The guard and the audit were already right; what was missing was the deliberate step. This reuses the delivered interstitial (same template, same confirm contract) instead of inventing a second confirmation mechanism.
- `common/admin.py`: `render_confirmation` gains `re_post` (flatten the interrupted POST into hidden `(name, value)` pairs so the confirmed leg re-runs the ordinary, fully validated save — the step adds a click, never a second, looser write path) and a `cancel_url` so a non-bulk step returns to its own page instead of the admin index. The CSRF token and the `confirm` marker are dropped from the flattened payload: the template owns both, so an interrupted POST can neither forge a token nor pin its own outcome. `request.FILES` is deliberately not flattened (no file field rides this seam).
- `accounts/admin.py`: `StoreUserAdmin.changeform_view` (the seam `get_urls` wraps, entered before the form is validated — the only place that can stop the save before `_apply_staff_roles` runs) renders the interstitial when a POST would change the six role groups. Guards come first and each returns "no confirmation needed": the `confirm=yes` commit leg; a caller without `staff.manage` (they must be refused by the view's own change gate, never shown a page for a privilege change they may not make); a non-staff target (roles are not part of a customer's change surface — that form keeps its own guards). The check is a DIFF, never a presence test: an emptied multi-select submits no `staff_roles` key at all, which is exactly a removal. Only the six role groups are resolved (non-numeric/foreign pks are not part of the diff, so the form's own queryset validation stays the authority on the commit leg). `save_model`, `_apply_staff_roles` and the `staff.manage` guard are untouched; the prompt is deliberately not the bulk action's "cannot be undone" wording, because a role change can be undone.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_staff_roles_admin.py` (+7 in a new `StaffRoleConfirmationTests`, and 5 pre-existing flow pins that keep every original assertion and gain the confirm leg): the interstitial renders with the pinned payload (1 add / 1 remove, real role names, `Affected` object list, the confirm marker, a Cancel back to the change page), the submitted fields travel through as `pending_fields` (CSRF and marker excluded) and nothing is committed; confirming commits with one audit entry per direction under the acting user ([6.12.5] intact); a direct POST is never committed unconfirmed; an emptied selector is confirmed as a removal; a save that changes no role is saved with no extra click; a caller without `staff.manage` gets the 403 rather than a confirmation page; a customer's change form keeps its own guards.
- Deliberate consequence to note: the superuser gets the confirm step too (SPEC-20-2 does not extend the grandfathered SPEC-6-04 bulk-action bypass) — one behaviour for every editor, so the page the operator reads cannot differ by role. Authorization is unchanged either way.
- Suite: **888 tests, OK (884 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3469 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean (no model change).

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-5 (Section 20) - builder: optional reason/note capture on cancel_pending

- Requirement: R-20.28 — reason+note capture existed on the inventory path only (`AdjustStockForm`); `cancel_pending` took no reason and had no form. This adds it on the shared interstitial, gated to that one action.
- `common/admin.py`: `ConfirmationNoteForm` — one optional free-text field (`confirmation_note`, 500 chars), the same shape as `AdjustStockForm`'s note. Orders carry no reason *vocabulary* (no ledger enum like `StockMovement.Reason`), so free text is the whole capture; minting an enum would mean a model change + migration for an audit string, which the spec does not ask for. `confirmation_reason_actions` is a declared set, and `confirmation_reason_form` returns `None` for anything outside it — so the textarea appears where an admin declared it and nowhere else. `confirmation_note` re-validates the submitted value through the same form: the mutation is never blocked by an optional field, and an over-long crafted note is dropped rather than truncated into the audit trail (the same outcome as supplying none).
- `orders/admin.py`: `OrderAdmin.confirmation_reason_actions = {"cancel_pending"}` and `_cancel_change_message` merges the reason into the audit message (`"Bulk action: order cancelled. Reason: <note>"`). With no reason typed the message is byte-for-byte the one shipped since SPEC-6-04 — the capture is additive and never rewrites the audit contract. The superuser's grandfathered direct execution is untouched (it never reaches the interstitial, so there is no note to read, and `confirmation_note` returns `""`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_admin_rbac.py` (+3): the textarea is offered and optional on the cancel interstitial and a typed reason lands in `LogEntry.change_message` under the acting user; with no reason the shipped message is unchanged and the cancel still happens; and the gate itself — a trimmed reason comes back for `cancel_pending`, another action reads `""` from the very same POST and gets no form at all, an over-long note is dropped, and `confirmation_reason_actions` is a subset of `confirmation_required_actions` on every registered admin. `backend/tests/test_staff_roles_admin.py` gains the cross-seam pin: the staff-role interstitial offers NO textarea, so the new declaration cannot leak onto another surface.
- Suite: **891 tests, OK (887 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3494 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean (no model change).

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-3 (Section 20) - builder: per-request correlation IDs across header, logs and audit trail

- Requirement: R-20.26 - zero correlation infrastructure (no `X-Request-ID`, no request-id column, no middleware module). Staff actions, their log lines and their audit rows could only be tied together by timestamp.
- `common/middleware.py` (new): `RequestIDMiddleware` reads an inbound `X-Request-ID` when it is safe, else mints a 32-hex id, sets `request.request_id`, stores it in a `ContextVar` and echoes it back on the response. Registered FIRST in `MIDDLEWARE` so responses produced above the view (security redirect, CORS preflight, admin login bounce) carry it too, and `__call__` converts an exception that reaches the outermost middleware with `response_for_exception` - the same call the handler would make, so `got_request_exception` still fires and the SPEC-7-02 500 log line is preserved while the header survives the unhandled-exception path. `sanitize_request_id` treats the inbound value as untrusted input: `\A[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,64}\Z`, so an over-long, spaced or header-injecting value is dropped in favour of a fresh id rather than echoed into a header, every log line and the DB.
- `common/middleware.py`: `RequestIDFormatter` stamps `request_id` on every record (referenced from `LOGGING` via `"()"`, not added to each call site's format string - a per-call-site format change is exactly how correlation stops covering new lines). `NO_REQUEST_ID` renders the field for records emitted outside a request. The `ContextVar` is reset in a `finally`, so one request's id can never label the next one on a reused worker thread.
- `common/models.py` + `common/migrations/0003_auditevent_request_id.py`: `AuditEvent.request_id` (blank, indexed, NOT NULL - a management command has no request to correlate with) is stamped by `record()` from the ambient id, so the trail joins to the request's logs and to the id on the response. The audit log mirror carries `request_id=` too.
- `config/settings.py`: `MIDDLEWARE` + the `plain` formatter (`{levelname} {asctime} {name} request_id={request_id} {message}`, brace style) + `CORS_EXPOSE_HEADERS`, without which a browser could never read the id it is asked to quote (pinned to the middleware's constant so the literal cannot drift).
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_correlation_ids.py` (new, 19 tests): an inbound id is honoured and echoed, a request without one gets a generated 32-hex id, two requests get different ids, a 404 carries one too; the unhandled-exception 500 STILL returns the inbound id (via the real SPEC-7-02 gateway-failure re-raise) and the escape-the-whole-chain branch converts and stamps; an over-long/CRLF/space/script value is never echoed while a value exactly at the 64-char cap is; the id is stored on the `AuditEvent`, appears in the audit log line and is stamped by the formatter inside and outside a request; the formatter is wired, brace-style pins intact, middleware first, CORS exposure; and the id does not leak from one request into the next.
- Suite: **910 tests, OK (906 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3538 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean after committing the migration.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-4 (Section 20) - builder: structured before/after values and a source discriminator on audit records

- Requirement: R-20.27/R-20.29 - guarded mutations recorded prose only ("Updated via API.", "Added role X.") and nothing anywhere said whether a change came from the admin or the API. Order transitions (`OrderStatusEvent.from_status/.to_status`) and `StockMovement.delta/.stock_after` already carried real values; the catalogue, role and promotion surfaces did not.
- `common/audit.py`: the trail now has ONE sanitizing path and ONE structured writer. `EXCLUDED_AUDIT_FIELDS` (credential-bearing field names, matched normalized-substring so `totp_secret`/`apiKey`/`razorpay_key_secret` are caught and `razorpay_order_id` is not), `clean_audit_value` (JSON-safe, 200-char cap, Decimals as exact text - never a float) and `clean_audit_payload` (drops excluded keys at every nesting depth). `AuditEvent.record` applies it, so no call site can write a raw detail - the single gate the security contract depends on. `field_changes`/`model_field_changes` build the real `(field, before, after)` triples (a field that did not move is not recorded; a creation records `before=None`), and `log_mutation` stores them with the actor, the target identity (label/pk/repr, so the row outlives a deletion) and the surface. `source` is a REQUIRED argument: an audit row that cannot say where a change came from is worse than no row.
- `common/models.py` + `common/migrations/0004_auditevent_source_alter_auditevent_category_and_more.py`: `AuditEvent.source` (admin/api/system, indexed) is the discriminator - a real field, so "admin work vs API work" is a filter, not a text search. `system` is the honest default for a business-event writer that attributes itself to no staff surface. New categories `catalogue`/`staff` and event types `catalogue.created/updated/deleted` + `staff.roles_updated`.
- `products/views.py`: the create/update/delete API writes now record the values actually written with `source=api`. `_save_product_update` takes the pre-write snapshot (after `save()` the old values are gone) and serves both PUT and PATCH, so the two cannot drift.
- `accounts/admin.py`: `_apply_staff_roles` adds one structured `staff.roles_updated` row with the real before/after role sets and `source=admin`; the per-direction `log_change` rows are byte-identical, so the shipped SPEC-6-04 audit contract is untouched. A save that changes no role writes nothing.
- `orders/admin.py`: `CouponAdmin.save_model` records the promotion toggle's real before/after with `source=admin`. Django's own changelist LogEntry names the column ("Changed Active.") but not the values; the snapshot is read from the DATABASE because `save_form` has already applied the new values by the time `save_model` runs.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_audit_structured.py` (new, 24 tests): the exclusion covers this project's real material (read from `TOTPDevice.secret`/`User.password` + the gateway key setting, not typed out) while `razorpay_order_id`/`razorpay_payment_id` survive; values stay JSON-safe/bounded/money-exact; a change set drops an excluded field ENTIRELY rather than redacting it; `record()` strips a credential field from the detail AND the log mirror; the API create/patch/put/delete rows pin real values (a read-only `stock` on update is absent, not fabricated); the role change pins `[support] -> [marketing]` after the SPEC-20-2 confirm leg; the coupon toggle pins `True -> False`; and admin/api rows are separately queryable with nothing claiming the admin surface unnamed.
- No existing test was modified: every shipped pin (test_audit_log.py's "via API" messages, test_admin_rbac.py's cancel/ship logs, SPEC-20-5's reason-merge, SPEC-7-01's payment details) still passes unchanged - the structured data is additive, on a model that can carry a field LogEntry cannot.
- Suite: **935 tests, OK (931 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged - ceiling held at 4)**, coverage **100.00%** (3616 stmts, 0 miss), makemigrations --check clean after committing the migration.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-1b (Section 20) - builder: the cancel interstitial stops calling an already-cancelled order "paid"

- Requirement: R-20.20 nit raised by the SPEC-20-1 audit - `orders/admin.py` emitted "(paid, never cancelled here)" for EVERY non-pending row, so selecting an already-cancelled order rendered the false "cancelled -> unchanged (paid, never cancelled here)". The resulting state was always right; only the reason clause lied.
- `orders/admin.py`: `_cancel_confirmation_row` derives the reason - "already cancelled" for a cancelled row, the refund-reconciliation hint for a paid one. Both reasons still say the row is skipped, so the operator's decision is unchanged.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_admin_rbac.py` (+1, additive): an already-cancelled row renders "cancelled -> unchanged (already cancelled)" and never mentions "paid"; the existing pending/confirmed pins are untouched.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-6 (Section 20) - builder: saved filters for the orders + products changelists

- Requirement: R-20.11, spec 20.1 "Saved filters/views where useful" — a soft ("where useful") data-table capability that Django ships no mechanism for. BUILT, not grandfathered: orders and products are exactly the two changelists a support/finance operator re-enters all day, and the surface stays small because a saved filter is only a shortcut to a URL, never a second path to the rows.
- `common/models.py` + `common/migrations/0005_savedfilter.py`: new `SavedFilter` (user, ContentType, name, JSON params, created_at), unique per (user, model, name) in the database. A saved view is a private preference — scoped to one account, deleted with it, holding no domain data beyond the search term its owner typed.
- `common/saved_filters.py` (new): `SavedFilterMixin` folds `?_saved_filter=<pk>` into the same `request.GET` the changelist already reads, then lets Django filter/validate/order as usual, so a saved filter can only narrow a listing. The pk lookup is scoped to the requesting user IN the query, so a foreign pk is the same answer as a missing one; both write endpoints gate on the target ModelAdmin's own `has_view_permission` (spec 6.12 least privilege), so a role without `orders.read` cannot save, apply or even reach one. A stored spec is validated with `get_changelist_instance` at save time — Django's ChangeList is the authority on which parameters a changelist accepts, so a replayed spec can never become a 400. Ordering (`o`) is deliberately excluded: it is a list of `list_display` column indexes and would re-sort a different column after any insertion.
- `products/admin.py` / `orders/admin.py`: `SavedFilterMixin` first in the bases, so the merge also runs on ProductAdmin's view-only branch (the role without `inventory.adjust`); the delivered grids, inline-stock cell, actions and confirmation gates are untouched.
- `templates/admin/saved_filter_bar.html` + per-model `change_list.html` overrides (products, orders): the bar is additive chrome below the grid (`{{ block.super }}`), with a labelled save form, an apply link and a per-row delete button.
- `config/urls.py`: two POST-only endpoints before the admin include (save / delete); applying needs no route of its own.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_admin_saved_filters.py` (new, 31 tests) — the bar is on both changelists and the inline stock cell still renders; a save stores exactly the selection on screen; a rejected lookup, a multi-valued key and ordering are dropped rather than stored; nothing-to-save / blank / over-long name / same-name race are honest refusals, not 500s; applying narrows the listing; a foreign, unknown or malformed pk is ignored without leaking existence; a role without the capability is refused by the changelist itself; delete is owner-only (404 otherwise) and still works for a user who lost the capability; a hand-edited non-string param is skipped, not crashed on.
- No existing test was modified. Suite: **966 tests, OK (962 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3763 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-5-10 (Section 5) - builder: unified global admin search across products, orders, customers

- Requirement: spec 5.1 draws the admin's ONE search box as "Search orders, products, customers…"; the ledger row was the cross-entity global search, owner S20. BUILT, dependency-free (no third-party admin app), reusing what the admin already trusts.
- `common/admin_search.py` (new): `global_search` searches products, orders and customers (`auth.User`, labelled "Customers" per the spec's vocabulary) in one request. A model's results come from its own registered ModelAdmin — `get_search_results` over `get_queryset`, i.e. the same declared `search_fields`, the same `lookup_allowed` vocabulary and the same root queryset as its changelist — so the global search cannot reach a field the changelist would not have searched. Capped at 10 rows per model with a "see all" link that hands the term back to that model's changelist (which re-applies its own gate).
- Capability gating is per model and non-negotiable: a group appears only if that ModelAdmin's own `has_view_permission` allows the caller, and the check precedes the query, so a role without `orders.read` gets no order rows, no order heading (the model is not named at all, so its existence is not confirmed) and issues no order query. A caller who may view none of the three gets a 403, not an empty page that would read as "nothing exists".
- `common/permissions.py`: new `capability_required_any` — the disjunction twin of the existing `capability_required`, same contract (anonymous -> admin login, unprivileged staff -> 403, superuser bypass preserved). Needed because a route spanning three models has no single capability behind it, and the alternative (a blanket `is_staff`) is exactly what SPEC-17-10 closed on the dashboard route.
- `templates/admin/global_search.html` (new) + `templates/admin/base_site.html` (new, Django's own plus one additive block): the search box lives in the admin's `nav-global` chrome, so it is on EVERY staff page (index, changelists, dashboard, audit log) and absent from the login page; the per-model search box above a changelist grid is untouched.
- `config/urls.py`: one route, `/admin/search/`, before the admin include.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_admin_global_search.py` (new, 18 tests) — one term reaches all three models and every row links to its own change page; marketing (products-only) sees no order/customer group and issues no order query (asserted over the captured SQL, not just the rendered page); finance sees orders + customers and no product link; a role-less staff account is 403; anonymous -> admin login; the superuser bypass still sees everything; no matches and no term are clean 200 pages; results are capped with a way on; the term is bounded; `SEARCH_CAPABILITIES` is pinned to the registered ModelAdmins' own `capability_map["view"]` and every target to a registered changelist; the chrome box is present for staff and absent on the login page; the per-model `searchbar` is still rendered.
- No existing test was modified. Suite: **984 tests, OK (980 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3816 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-20-6b (Section 20) - builder: admin-area capability routes require staff membership

- Requirement: R-20.11 follow-up from the SPEC-5-10 audit - the admin-chrome decorators `capability_required` / `capability_required_any` gated `/admin/search/`, `/admin/dashboard/` and `/admin/audit-log/` on the capability alone, so a NON-staff account carrying a role group reached them (200 + `Orders (n)` + live change-form links on the search) while every Django admin view sent the same caller to the login. Only DB tampering can build such an account (the product refuses roles for non-staff users), but the area no longer depends on that invariant.
- `common/permissions.py`: both decorators now require `user.is_staff` in addition to the capability, mirroring `staff_member_required`. Anonymous -> admin login, role-less staff -> 403, the capability layer and the superuser bypass are all unchanged (the superuser bypass stays first, so it cannot be narrowed by the new gate). Chrome-asymmetry note: the non-staff half is gone (no chrome box, no page); the role-less-STAFF box -> 403 remains, because that is the intended least-privilege capability layer (SPEC-17-10), not the staff gate, so `base_site.html` is deliberately untouched.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_rbac_foundation.py` (+5, new `AdminChromeCapabilityDecoratorTests`) pins the shared decorator contract for both decorators — staff+capability admitted, non-staff+capability denied (with the capability precondition asserted), staff-without-capability still denied, anonymous still redirected, superuser bypass intact; `backend/tests/test_admin_global_search.py` (+3, new `GlobalSearchStaffGateTests`) pins the route: a staff `support` holder still searches, a non-staff `support` holder gets 403 with no order heading and no change link, and the route now agrees with the changelist it links to. No existing test was modified. Suite: **992 tests, OK (988 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3816 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check` clean.

## 2026-09-30 — SPEC-21-5 (Section 21) — builder: checkout-refresh safety, server-side pin

- Requirement: R-21.2.6 follow-up — a browser refresh mid-checkout must not corrupt the session. The BROWSER leg belongs to SPEC-3-01 (Playwright e2e is non-hermetic and its CI job self-skips), so this task pins only the SERVER-SIDE invariant that leg depends on.
- backend/tests/test_checkout_refresh.py (new, 10 tests) — TEST-ONLY, no production code touched: re-issuing checkout against a still-payable order collapses onto it (one order, one item snapshot, one creation audit row, repeated refreshes never accumulate); the collapsed id is the only charge target (one gateway intent across original + refreshed tab); the cart survives the refresh with its lines, quantities and applied coupon intact (checked on the row AND on the SPA's boot read); a settled refresh starts a fresh order and the new pending order re-arms the gate for the next refresh; a deliberately changed basket (coupon applied mid-flow) is a new purchase, not a refresh; two buyers refreshing never collapse into one another.
- The collapse mechanism itself is deliberately NOT re-specified: it stays owned by SPEC-21-1 (orders.views._find_duplicate_pending_order, the pending-order fingerprint guard) and SPEC-9-01 (the Idempotency-Key layer behind it). No defect surfaced in orders/views.py, so it is untouched; no existing pin was modified, weakened or deleted.
- Suite: **1002 tests, OK (998 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3816 stmts, 0 miss).

## 2026-09-30 - SPEC-21-3 (Section 21) - builder: hermetic restore drill + incident-recovery runbook

- Requirement: R-21.2.12 - no DB-restore or incident-recovery drill existed, so "can we get the data back" was an untested assumption. BUILT as a runnable drill plus the manual-reconciliation contract the code already enforces.
- ops/management/commands/restore_drill.py (new, with ops/management/ + ops/management/commands/ package markers): manage.py restore_drill [--dry-run] [--simulate-failure] [--database ALIAS] snapshots the target with dumpdata, builds an EMPTY throwaway sqlite database from the migrations, loads the dump into it, and verifies the restore by comparing per-model row counts - the failure that matters after a restore is a snapshot that loads but is missing rows, so row counts are the measure and the failing models are NAMED in the output. Exit code is the signal (0 = round-tripped, non-zero = refused or failed), so CI can gate on it.
- Safety is structural, not advisory: it REFUSES any target that is neither in-memory, nor a file under the OS temp directory, nor a database other than the configured one - before a single query, and with --dry-run too (a dry run is a report, not an exemption). The source is only ever READ (no flush/DROP/migrate). Every write lands in a temp dir the command creates and deletes, including on the failure path. The restored database is reached through a scratch alias registered on the connection handler's thread-local store only, so it cannot outlive the command.
- backend/docs/runbook-incident-recovery.md (new): detection (/health/, the four alert types, the audit log) -> triage (read-only, three-way split into data loss / money-without-state / code defect) -> recovery (restore procedure, then the manual-reconciliation procedure) -> verification -> escalation. The reconciliation section documents the contract ALREADY pinned in orders/state.py:30, orders/admin.py (save_model abort + bulk cancel_pending) and orders/views.py admin_order_cancel 409: cancelling a paid order is impossible in code because there is no refund flow (V-03), so the money moves at the gateway and the store records the outcome through the audited admin surface - never raw SQL, never a deleted trail row.
- Tests: backend/tests/test_restore_drill.py (new, 19 tests) pins the hermetic safety through the REAL path (an alias configured to a production-shaped database path that is never created; no safety function is patched out): the refusal and its message, dry-run writing nothing and refusing production too, an unusable database producing a sentence instead of a traceback, the decision rule itself, scratch connection unregistered and temp dir gone after BOTH a passing and a failing run, the source database byte-identical before and after, and --simulate-failure exiting non-zero with the short-stocked model named.
- backend/docs/test-gaps.md: the SPEC-21-4 counts were re-measured in this commit too (the suite grew by 19 here), so the file is not left knowingly stale: **1021 tests = 1017 pass + 4 expectedFailure**, coverage **100.00%** (3909 stmts, 0 miss), tests/ package 516.
- Suite: **1021 tests, OK (1017 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, makemigrations --check clean (the drill adds no model). Verified end-to-end from the CLI: --dry-run exits 0 against the dev sqlite file and exits 1 with the refusal text when DATABASE_URL points outside the temp directory.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-2-10b (Section 22) - builder: orchestration for the deployable unit

- `docker-compose.yml` (new, repo root): the SPEC-2-10a image wired to a `postgres:17-alpine` service (named volume, `pg_isready` healthcheck) that the app waits for with `condition: service_healthy` - a release's migration can never race a database that is still booting. Configuration goes through the contract `backend/config/settings.py` already reads, never a parallel one: `DATABASE_URL` is assembled from the SAME `POSTGRES_*` variables the db service reads, so the password has exactly one home (the git-ignored repo-root `.env`) and cannot drift out of sync with the database it names; a `DATABASE_URL` in `.env` overrides it wholesale for a managed database (and `sslmode` is explicit - `disable` for this in-network container, the operator's value for anything remote). The backend healthcheck probes the REAL `/health/` (`ops.views.health`, which answers 503 while the database or the media mount is degraded), so it fails on a genuinely unhealthy app instead of merely reporting that the process is up. No credential is written into the file: compose refuses to start without `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, and `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY` still arrives through `env_file` so the existing fail-closed settings guard remains the single authority.
- `Procfile` (new, repo root): one `web:` line running the SAME gunicorn command as the image CMD (`config.wsgi:application --bind 0.0.0.0:${PORT:-8000} --workers 3 --access-logfile - --error-logfile -`), from `backend/` because the Django project is not at the repo root. Deliberately NO `release:` line: release-time migrations live in exactly one place (SPEC-22-07), and a second migrate path is a second thing to keep correct.
- Verified: `docker compose config` exits 0 for three env shapes (default in-cluster URL, a `.env` `DATABASE_URL` override, and `POSTGRES_USER`/`POSTGRES_DB` overrides reaching both services) and fails closed with a named message when `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` is unset; the temporary `.env` used for those checks was removed again and is git-ignored.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-07 (Section 22) - builder: release-time migrate + migration-review checkpoint

- `scripts/release.sh` (new, repo root): the release sequence, and the ONLY release-time `manage.py migrate --noinput` in the repository - build/pull -> database up (waits on its healthcheck) -> migration-review checkpoint -> migrate -> collectstatic -> recreate the app and wait for `/health/`. The checkpoint is R-22.11 and it is fail-closed: `makemigrations --check --dry-run` runs before anything is applied, and when a model change has no committed migration the release stops and says to commit the generated file - a release may never generate, let alone silently apply, a schema change nobody reviewed. Every management command runs through `docker compose run` on the released image, so what migrates is exactly what ships; the script is `set -euo pipefail`, refuses up front when the repo-root `.env` is missing, never prints the environment, and is idempotent end to end (a re-run is a no-op at every step).
- `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (new): triggered by the COMPLETION of the existing `backend-tests` workflow and additionally refusing unless `secret-scan.yml` also succeeded on that exact commit (polled, because the two run in parallel) - it depends on the existing gates instead of duplicating them with a second test run that could disagree with the first. `master` is absent from `RELEASE_BRANCHES`, the single place a deploy can originate, because master is released by promotion (a merge), never by a push. Credentials come only from Actions secrets (`DEPLOY_HOST`, `DEPLOY_USER`, `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY`, optional pinned `DEPLOY_KNOWN_HOSTS`), land in a 0600 temp file removed by a trap, and the deploy host checks out the gated commit before running `./scripts/release.sh`.
- `.github/workflows/backend-tests.yml`: `paths` widened (push AND pull_request) to the deployment files - `docker-compose.yml`, `Procfile`, `scripts/release.sh`, `deploy.yml`. deploy.yml is triggered by THIS workflow's completion, so a commit touching only the deployment wiring would otherwise produce no backend-tests run and could never be released. The suite, the pre-merge drift gate and the coverage gate are untouched.
- `backend/tests/test_deployment_contract.py` (new, 27 tests): pins the contract the Python suite cannot otherwise see - the compose healthcheck probes the real `/health/` and fails on a non-200, `POSTGRES_*` overrides reach both services, the Procfile gunicorn argv equals the Dockerfile CMD argv, EXACTLY ONE release-time migrate exists across the deployment surface while the pre-merge drift gate stays intact, the release sequence is ordered (checkpoint before migrate, migrate before the restart), no credential-shaped assignment in the new files is a literal, and no `branches:` list in the deploy workflow contains master. Mutation-verified: pointing the healthcheck at another path and adding a `release:` migrate line to the Procfile turn the relevant pins red, then green again after revert. The files are parsed as TEXT on purpose - PyYAML is not a declared dependency and must not enter the production image for a test.
- Suite: **1060 tests, OK (1056 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3927 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean, `bash -n scripts/release.sh` clean, black clean.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-03 (Section 22) - builder: staging env + per-environment isolation + fail-closed DATABASE_URL

- `backend/config/settings.py`: closes R-22.3's correctness blocker - a missing, malformed or unsupported `DATABASE_URL` no longer falls back to the sqlite dev database outside development, it refuses the boot by name (the message never echoes the URL, which carries a password). The parser keeps its fail-safe shape (`_parse_database_url` returns the entry plus whether the URL was usable, and why not); the new `_databases_from_url(url, debug)` is where the boot decision lives, so development keeps the sqlite convenience and every non-debug environment gets the refusal. An EXPLICIT `sqlite:///` URL is still honoured anywhere - a single-box staging install is deliberate, not a silent substitution.
- `backend/config/settings.py`: `DJANGO_ENV` (local|ci|staging|production) names the deployment, is REQUIRED outside development and refuses an unknown value instead of guessing; `ALLOWED_HOSTS` and `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` go through `_env_hosts`, which refuses the localhost development defaults outside development instead of leaving a deployed environment silently unconfigured. Isolation is encoded, not documented-only: a deployment must say which environment it is, and each one names its own database, secret key, hosts and origins.
- `backend/.env.example`: `DJANGO_ENV`, the non-debug host/origin requirement, and the corrected DATABASE_URL fallback wording (falls back in development, refuses outside it).
- `backend/tests/test_settings_security.py`: 13 new tests (`NonDebugFailClosedTests`) - one key short of the production shape refuses (missing/malformed/unparseable DATABASE_URL, unsupported scheme, undeclared or misspelled DJANGO_ENV, absent ALLOWED_HOSTS, absent CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS), staging is refused exactly like production, an explicit sqlite URL still boots, `DJANGO_DEBUG=true` + missing DATABASE_URL still boots on sqlite with the dev host defaults, all four declared environments boot, and the pure resolvers are pinned in-process. Existing non-debug boots now share one `_NON_DEBUG_ENV` constant instead of repeating a partial env, the three import tests that pinned the silent fallback were retargeted to development boots, and `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` joined `_LEAKED_ENV_NAMES` (it sits outside the `DJANGO_` family, so an untracked local `.env` was leaking into the child and could mask a refusal). Mutation-verified: disabling the new `if not resolved and not debug:` guard turns 6 of the 13 red.
- Suite: **1078 tests, OK (1074 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3962 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean, `manage.py check` clean. No model, no dependency.
- Follow-up (SPEC-2-10a owns the file) - **this line was wrong and the gate fix cycle corrected it in code, not in prose**: it claimed that giving the build-time `RUN` a `DATABASE_URL` was the whole remedy for the now-dead image build. The guards are conjunctive, so `DATABASE_URL` alone still exited 1 on `DJANGO_ENV` (and then on `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`). `backend/Dockerfile` now declares all five keys in the build layer, and `docker-compose.yml` carries the two the runtime service was missing; see the fix-cycle entry at the end of this file.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-2-04/SPEC-22-03 (gate fix cycle) - builder: the image builds again and the container boots

- `backend/Dockerfile`: the build-time `collectstatic` layer is a `DJANGO_DEBUG=false` boot like any other, so SPEC-22-03's guards applied to it and `docker build` exited 1 (`ImproperlyConfigured: DJANGO_ENV must name the deployment environment`) - the promoted image (SPEC-2-10a) could not be built at all. The `RUN` now declares every key the guards require, all build-layer-only and none of them real: `DJANGO_ENV=ci` (a build is not a deployment), the existing non-secret placeholder `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY`, `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS=localhost`, `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:3000`, and a throwaway `DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/build.sqlite3` - a file nothing creates or reads, because `collectstatic` opens no connection but the guard cannot know that. **No `config/settings.py` change: the production guard is untouched and still fail-closed.**
- `docker-compose.yml`: the runtime env contract listed the secret key, hosts, `DJANGO_DEBUG` and `DATABASE_URL` but never `DJANGO_ENV` or `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`, so with exactly that env the gunicorn `CMD`, `release.sh` step 3 (`makemigrations --check`) and `/health/` all refused to import - a crash-loop no release could get past. Both are now pass-throughs of the repo-root `.env`: `DJANGO_ENV` defaults to `production` (set `DJANGO_ENV=local` for local work; this file is the production orchestrator) and `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` is required (`${CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS:?set CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS in the repo-root .env}`), so compose names the missing key up front instead of letting the container die on the settings import - no literal localhost origin is baked in, which is exactly the unconfigured deployment the guard exists to refuse. Neither is a secret, and the `.env` contract at the top of the file now documents both keys. Verified with `docker compose config`: exit 1 naming the missing origin when it is unset, exit 0 with `DJANGO_ENV: production` + the supplied origins when it is set.
- `backend/tests/test_deployment_contract.py` (+9: `BuildLayerSatisfiesTheNonDebugGuardsTests`, `ComposeRuntimeEnvContractTests`): both fixes are proved by booting the app, not by prose. The build env is PARSED out of the committed `RUN` line and used verbatim for `manage.py check` and a real `collectstatic` (only the output directory is redirected to a temp dir, since `/app/staticfiles` exists inside the image); the compose contract's shape migrates, then loads `config.wsgi` - the exact module the image `CMD` hands gunicorn - and answers `/health/` 200 in-process, with no server and no network. Dropping any one of the five guard keys must still be refused by name, which doubles as the proof the production guard was not weakened. Mutation-verified: against the pre-fix files the new pins go red with the auditor's exact messages (`ImproperlyConfigured: DJANGO_ENV must name the deployment environment ...`, `'CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS' not found in {...}`).
- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md`: the "Known gap" section claimed a build-time `DATABASE_URL` was the whole remedy for the image build. Refuted (the guards are conjunctive: `DATABASE_URL` alone still exits 1 on `DJANGO_ENV`) and replaced by where each required key is actually set - the build layer and the container - and by the pins that hold both there. Suite: **1087 tests, OK (1083 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (3962 stmts, 0 miss - no production code changed), `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean, `bash -n scripts/release.sh` clean.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-02 (Section 22) - builder: backups (R-22.9) + code/release rollback (R-22.23)

- `backend/ops/management/commands/backup_db.py` (new) + `scripts/backup.sh` (new) close R-22.9: `manage.py backup_db` runs `pg_dump` (stdout straight to the file, connection identity in the child's `PG*` environment and never in argv, `--no-password` so a wrong password fails fast) against the configured `DATABASE_URL`, or the stdlib sqlite online-backup API for local/dev; it writes a `.partial` file it moves into place only on success, prunes to the newest `BACKUP_RETENTION` dumps (default 7, by filename, its stamped scheme plus the engine's own file signature) and refuses a missing `BACKUP_DIR` by name. No output carries the connection identity - not the URL, the password, the host, the port, the user or the database name, and not libpq's `Connection parameters:` dump or the address it RESOLVED either; messages name the alias, the engine and the file. A failed dump exits non-zero keeping no dump, and the source is only ever read (sqlite opened `mode=ro`). The cadence is a daily cron entry on the deploy host running the committed script, which bind-mounts the host backup directory into the one-shot container; the runbook also carries the no-image-change route (the postgres image's own `pg_dump`, then `backup_db --prune-only`) for the image that ships no PostgreSQL client.
- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md`: the backup story (mechanism per engine, env keys, retention semantics, cron + systemd schedule, the `pg_dump` prerequisite, and what is NOT covered - off-host copy, the media volume, host loss) and a new release-rollback section closing R-22.23 - pin the known-good sha, redeploy it through `release.sh`, then choose honestly between forward-fix, `migrate <app> <migration>` (only where the reverse has been read and is lossless) and restore-from-snapshot for a data-corrupting release, stating plainly that nothing here reverses a migration automatically. `docs/runbook-incident-recovery.md` now points its S22 deferral at this story and its §3a restore step at `pg_restore`.
- Tests + suite: `backend/tests/test_backup_db.py` (new, 49 tests) - a failed dump exits non-zero with no dump kept, retention keeps the newest N and deletes only files that carry the stamped scheme AND the engine's signature, and a probe URL carrying a password (in the environment AND the connection config) appears in no output including a redacted `pg_dump` error; the sqlite path is a REAL backup of a real file, rows read back and the source byte-identical; the schedule artifact is pinned as text (fail-closed, bind-mounted, no literal credential, cadence documented). Mutation-verified: reversing the retention sort, dropping the redaction, swallowing the dump exit code and removing the bind-mount each turn the relevant pins red. Suite: **1136 tests, OK (1132 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (4136 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean (no model), `bash -n scripts/backup.sh` clean, black clean.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-02 fix cycle 1 (gate) - builder: the backup cadence actually runs

Audit of `1c6f78f` returned 5 bugs, two of which falsified this section's own claims. All five are fixed in code, and the two claims above are now true rather than withdrawn.

- **P1 `scripts/backup.sh` - the whole cadence was dead.** The script referenced `$BACKUP_RETENTION` unguarded under `set -euo pipefail`, and both documented invocations (its own header line and the runbook cron entry) set `BACKUP_DIR` alone - so every scheduled run aborted with `BACKUP_RETENTION: unbound variable` (exit 1) *before* a dump was taken, and every test that pinned this script was a text assertion that never executed it. The optional knob is now defaulted (`BACKUP_RETENTION=${BACKUP_RETENTION:-7}`) next to the `BACKUP_DIR` check, and - the same knob, silently ignored before - it is now passed into the one-shot container with `-e BACKUP_RETENTION=`, because `docker compose run` does not forward the host environment and the log line claimed a retention the command never saw.
- **P2 `backup_db.py` - redaction was incomplete.** `pg_dump`'s own stderr quotes the whole connection (`connection to server at "db.prod.internal" (10.42.0.7), port 5432 failed ... for user "perfume_admin"`, `Connection parameters: host=... dbname=...`), and only `DATABASE_URL` and the password were masked, so host, resolved IP, port, user and database name reached the cron mail verbatim. `_secrets` now covers the whole connection identity (PASSWORD, USER, HOST, PORT, NAME) and a new `scrub()` additionally masks what no setting can match: libpq's `Connection parameters:` line and bare IPv4/IPv6 literals (clock times are not addresses, and survive).
- **P2 `backup_db.py` - `prune()` deleted an operator's hand-taken backup.** The glob was `perfume-*.dump` (prefix + suffix), so a foreign `perfume-20190101T000000Z.dump` was deleted by retention while the docstring promised the opposite. Pruning now requires BOTH this command's stamped name shape (`STAMP_GLOBS`, spelled out rather than wildcarded, so `perfume-latest.dump` cannot match) AND the engine's own file signature (`PGDMP` / the sqlite header, read as the first bytes only). The signature half is what keeps the documented `--prune-only` route working - a dump the postgres image's own `pg_dump` wrote is identical by name - and anything the rules cannot vouch for is left for a human.
- **P3 `.github/workflows/backend-tests.yml`**: `scripts/backup.sh` added to the `paths` filter of both triggers, next to `scripts/release.sh` - a commit that changes only the cadence now runs the gate.
- **P3 secret-scan noise**: the redaction fixture password is no longer `probe-xxxx` (an x-padding placeholder reads as a redaction marker to the scanner); it is `probe-fixture-only`, still assembled into the probe URL so no literal `postgres://user:password@host` string exists in the repository. No `.gitleaks.toml` allowlist entry was needed.
- Tests: `backend/tests/test_backup_db.py` 49 -> 62 tests. `BackupScriptExecutionTests` is new and **runs** the committed script in a throwaway deploy-host layout (compose file, `.env`, backup directory) with `docker` stubbed on PATH and only `BACKUP_DIR` in the environment - the pin that was missing. `test_a_failed_dumps_error_text_cannot_leak_the_connection_identity`, `test_scrub_masks_the_connection_shape_third_party_text_adds` and `test_scrub_masks_an_ipv6_address_but_not_a_clock_time` cover the redaction gap; `test_prune_never_deletes_a_hand_taken_dump_that_borrowed_the_scheme`, `test_prune_never_deletes_a_file_that_only_borrowed_the_name`, `test_a_stamp_that_is_not_a_date_is_not_our_scheme`, `test_a_directory_wearing_a_dumps_name_is_never_unlinked` and `test_ownership_is_decided_by_the_engine_signature_not_the_name` cover the retention gap; `test_the_backup_script_gates_the_backend_suite_on_both_triggers` pins the workflow paths. Mutation-verified (each reverted, each red): removing the retention default -> `AssertionError: 1 != 0 : ./scripts/backup.sh: line 115: BACKUP_RETENTION: unbound variable`; restoring the prefix glob -> 2 retention failures; neutering the signature check -> 3 retention failures. ## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-04 (Section 22) - builder: env-gated error tracking + /health/ uptime registration (R-22.13, R-22.12)

- `backend/config/settings.py`: error tracking, optional end to end. `SENTRY_DSN` gates everything: the `sentry_sdk` import sits INSIDE the gate (`_init_error_tracking`), so with no DSN nothing is imported, nothing is initialised and nothing leaves the process - local development, CI and this suite are byte-for-byte as before. `init()` receives the DSN, `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT` (defaulting to `DJANGO_ENV`, so a staging boot cannot file its errors as production), `SENTRY_SAMPLE_RATE` (1.0) and `SENTRY_TRACES_SAMPLE_RATE` (0.0 - nothing instruments transactions), with `send_default_pii=False`. The two integrations make the EXISTING logging config the reporting path rather than adding one: `django.request` is pinned at ERROR by `_build_logging` (SPEC-7-02), so a `LoggingIntegration(event_level=ERROR)` is what turns every request 5xx already logged into an event; `DjangoIntegration` captures the exception with request context. A DSN on a host whose image predates the SDK pin degrades to a named warning, never a refused boot. `_env_float` joins the other fail-safe resolvers (bad value -> documented default + warning).
- `backend/requirements.txt`: `sentry-sdk==2.71.0`, the official SDK, pinned like everything else and imported only behind the DSN gate.
- `backend/Dockerfile`: a container `HEALTHCHECK` that probes the image's OWN `/health/` (`config/urls.py` -> `ops.views.health`, which answers 503 while the database or the media mount is unusable). 200 exits 0; the degraded 503 raises in `urlopen` and any other non-200 fails the comparison, so a broken storefront is `unhealthy` rather than merely "running". It probes `$PORT` - the variable the CMD binds - and identifies itself as a real request does (`Host` from the first `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS` entry, `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`), so the hardening SPEC-22-08 turns on cannot refuse it.
- `backend/.env.example`: the four `SENTRY_*` keys, all optional and all commented except the sample rate.
- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md`: "Transport hardening and uptime" - the hardening table, the error-tracking contract (what is reported, the PII-off default, why the DSN stays environment-only) and the uptime story (image HEALTHCHECK + compose healthcheck + how to register an external probe against `https://<host>/health/`, treating any non-200 as down, with no vendor token or account id in the repository).
- `backend/tests/test_error_tracking.py` (new, 18 tests): inert without a DSN (the SDK is absent from `sys.modules` after a real settings import in a clean subprocess, with a sentinel module on `sys.path` so an import moved to module scope would be caught; init is never reached); live with a DSN against a STUB module - DSN/environment/sample rates recorded, PII off, DjangoIntegration + error-level LoggingIntegration present, and the `django.request` channel it reads still pinned at ERROR; the sample-rate resolvers; and the Dockerfile probe EXECUTED against a stubbed `urllib.request` (200 -> exit 0, 503 -> non-zero, unreachable -> non-zero, `$PORT` honoured on the wire, headers as asserted above). Mutation-verified: making the probe accept any status, and deleting the `if not dsn` gate, each turn the relevant pins red. Suite: **1167 tests, OK (1163 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (4180 stmts, 0 miss), `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected". No model.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-06 (Section 22) - builder: WAF ruleset + secret rotation procedure (R-22.16 WAF half, R-22.8 rotation half)

- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md`: two operator-facing sections, both truthful about what they do not cover.
  - **WAF ruleset for the front door.** The layer division first, because the failure this prevents is an operator tightening the proxy to the app's numbers: DRF's `ScopedRateThrottle` budgets the sensitive public endpoints **per identity** (one account hammering login is distinguishable from one IP behind a shared NAT - a proxy cannot), so the proxy limits are **deliberately looser** (`limit_req_zone` ~60 req/min/IP with a small burst, `limit_conn`) and exist only to stop volumetric floods. Also: method allow-listing, path hygiene, the OWASP CRS in **detection-only first** (this app's legitimate traffic - product copy, addresses, order notes - false-positives on SQLi/XSS heuristics, and enabling blocking before reading the false positives rejects real checkouts) with the webhook/admin-field/`/health/` exclusions, no caching of `/health/` at the edge (a cached 200 hides a degraded store from both the healthcheck and the uptime monitor), `client_max_body_size` matched to `MAX_UPLOAD_MB`, and an explicit "a WAF is not authorization" - every access decision stays in Django. A managed WAF is a legitimate substitute; its account/zone/token are deployment facts that stay in the secret store.
  - **Secret rotation.** Which secrets and how often (`DJANGO_SECRET_KEY`, `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, SMTP, `SENTRY_DSN`, the deploy SSH key, vendor tokens), the **mint-then-retire order** per credential so each step is reversible until the last, the `SECRET_KEY` caveat stated honestly - it signs the JWTs and the guest-cart sessions, so rotating it is a **mass logout** plus mass token invalidation, one environment at a time - and a verification list whose honest test is that the **previous** credential no longer authenticates. It also states what the existing scans do not do: `gitleaks.toml` / `.gitguardian.yml` / `secret-scan.yml` find a leak after the fact, and nothing here rotates anything. A secret that was ever committed is treated as compromised: rotate first, clean history second.
- `backend/tests/test_deployment_contract.py` (`FrontDoorRunbookTests`, 7 tests): the runbook is only useful if the properties hold, so they are pinned - the WAF section names `ScopedRateThrottle`/`limit_req_zone`/`THROTTLE_RECOVERY_RATE` and the per-IP reason not to tighten; the rotation table names every secret the deployment actually holds and states the order and the verification; the `SECRET_KEY` caveat states the downtime cost; and neither section may carry a credential-shaped value or a vendor token. Mutation-verified: reworded `per-IP` and `mass logout` turn the relevant pins red. Suite unchanged in count - see the combined verification under SPEC-22-04 above.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-08 (Section 22) - builder: the shipped TLS hardening is actually ON in the deploy artifact (R-22.6, V-06)

- `docker-compose.yml`: SPEC-17-07 shipped `SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT` / HSTS / the trusted proxy header as env-gated flags with development-safe defaults, and nothing in the deploy path set any of them - so "HTTPS enabled" was true of no real deployment (V-06). All six keys are now in the backend service's `environment` with production defaults (`SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT=true`, `SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS=31536000`, `SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS`/`_PRELOAD` `false`, `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER_NAME=HTTP_X_FORWARDED_PROTO` / `_VALUE=https`), each a `${KEY:-default}` pass-through so a plain-HTTP local `docker compose up` overrides two of them in the repo-root `.env` and nothing else. **The settings defaults are unchanged** - the hardening is enabled at the deploy layer only, so local development and the test suite are untouched.
- One correctness detail the flag names hide: `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER_NAME` must be the WSGI **environ** name (`HTTP_X_FORWARDED_PROTO`), which is what Django reads out of `request.META`. The bare wire header `X-Forwarded-Proto` would never match, so every proxied request would look like plain HTTP and be redirected to HTTPS forever - a redirect loop, which is worse than no hardening at all. `includeSubDomains` and `preload` stay off by default deliberately: the first breaks every plain-HTTP subdomain, the second is effectively irreversible.
- `docker-compose.yml` healthcheck: the probe now presents the first `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS` entry as `Host` and the trusted forwarded scheme, read from the app's own env. Without that, the hardening this same commit turns on would 301 the probe to an `https://` the container does not serve and 400 it as an unlisted host - i.e. a hardened deployment would report itself unhealthy forever.
- `backend/.env.example` + `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md`: the production values, the WSGI-environ-name rule with the nginx `proxy_set_header` line that satisfies it, and the two keys a plain-HTTP local run must override (with the localhost-lockout failure mode spelled out).
- `backend/tests/test_deployment_contract.py` (`TlsHardeningDeployContractTests`, 11 tests): the defaults are read out of the committed compose file (never restated here) and used to boot the app - `REDIRECT True`, the real HSTS window, `('HTTP_X_FORWARDED_PROTO', 'https')`, subdomains/preload off - while `DJANGO_DEBUG=true` with no hardening keys is pinned to the development-safe defaults, so this cannot be "fixed" later by moving a default. The last test proves the interaction the hardening creates: with the compose values applied, a request shaped like the healthcheck gets `PROBE 200` and a naive one gets `NAIVE 301`. Mutation-verified: dropping `SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT` and using the bare header name turn 6 of the 11 red. Suite unchanged in count for this commit's second half - see the combined verification under SPEC-22-04 above. No model, no new dependency, `docker compose config` exit 0.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-04 + SPEC-22-06 + SPEC-22-08 - builder FIX CYCLE 1 (audit of batch 5: 0edcb12, 4b5697a, 40d7f47)

- **[P1] `backend/config/settings.py`: a malformed `SENTRY_DSN` crashed the whole storefront.** `sentry_sdk.init()` sat OUTSIDE the `try`/`except ImportError`, so the `sentry_sdk.parsers.BadDsn` that the SDK's own DSN parser raises for a bad value was raised at **settings-import time** - `SENTRY_DSN=not-a-dsn` -> `BadDsn: Unsupported scheme ''`, `SENTRY_DSN=https://` -> `BadDsn: Missing hostname`, exit 1. Via compose `env_file` that is a permanent crash-loop for every worker, and it fails `release.sh`'s migrate checkpoint; it also broke the docstring's own promise that a bad DSN cannot take the site down. `init()` now sits INSIDE the guarded block and a second `except Exception` degrades any SDK raise to a named warning + `ERROR_TRACKING_ENABLED = False`. `except Exception` is deliberate: whatever the SDK raises while parsing a configuration an operator typed is, by that fact, not worth a boot. The warning quotes the exception type and message and names the remedy, and deliberately does NOT echo the DSN (it carries the project's public key). The `if not dsn` gate and the SDK import staying inside it are untouched - the no-DSN sentinel property still holds.
- **`backend/tests/test_error_tracking.py` 18 -> 22 tests** (`MalformedDsnBootTests`, new). The gap the audit named: every "with a DSN" test installed a stub whose `init()` only records, and the missing-SDK test covers `ImportError`, so nothing could ever see what the real SDK does with a bad DSN. The new pins run against the **real** SDK - in-process (`BadDsn` -> warning + False) and as a whole clean-subprocess settings import (exit 0, `ENABLED False`, the DSN still read as configured) for both malformed shapes - plus the counterpart, so "catch everything and do nothing" is not a passing answer: a valid-shaped DSN still yields `ENABLED True` with a real client while `socket.connect`/`create_connection`/`getaddrinfo` are blocked in the child, i.e. the boot still dials nothing. `test_the_pinned_sdk_is_really_installed` asserts `sentry_sdk.VERSION` equals the `requirements.txt` pin **read out of that file**, so a venv that never installed the SDK cannot make the real-SDK pins pass vacuously and bumping the pin cannot turn the assertion into a lie. Mutation-verified: moving `init()` back outside the guard turns the two malformed-DSN pins red with `BadDsn` and exit 1, while the valid-DSN pin stays green. No network in any of them.
- **[P2] `sentry-sdk==2.71.0` really installed** (`pip install sentry-sdk==2.71.0` into the venv the suite runs on; `sentry_sdk.VERSION` -> `2.71.0`). The pin in `requirements.txt` was correct and CI installs from it, but the local venv did not have the package, so the suite had only ever validated a hand-written fake and BUG-1's enabling condition was never exercised against the real SDK.
- **[P3] `backend/docs/changes.md`**: two test counts corrected - `FrontDoorRunbookTests` 8 -> **7** and `TlsHardeningDeployContractTests` 9 -> **11** (both were already those counts in the commits above; the "6 of the 9" mutation sentence follows the real denominator now).
- **[P3] `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md`**: the rotation step for the deploy SSH key claimed GitHub Actions uses `core.ssh_command`/`ssh-key`. It does not - there is no ssh-agent action and no `core.ssh_command` anywhere in this repo. `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` writes `secrets.DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` to a `mktemp` file under `umask 077` (with `DEPLOY_KNOWN_HOSTS` beside it, both removed by an `EXIT` trap) and calls `ssh -i "$key" -o IdentitiesOnly=yes`. The runbook now describes that, which is also what an operator rotating the key actually has to do.
- Suite: **1189 tests, OK (1185 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%**, exit 0, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", `black --check` clean on the new test module and on the settings lines this commit adds (`settings.py` is not Black-formatted as a file at HEAD - it carries single-quoted string literals throughout, and line-for-line comparison against a Black-formatted copy shows **zero** differences inside the block this commit changes; the pre-existing drift is untouched here and left as a separate task), `docker compose config` exit 0 with the full production env and exit 1 naming `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` when it is absent. No model, no new dependency, no env key added.



## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-09 (Section 22) - builder: the controlled process for real customer data in non-prod (R-22.4)

- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md` (new section "Non-production data: the controlled process", between the environment contract and the transport-hardening section): the gap this closes is that the staging contract promised a "sanitised dump or seeded fresh" with no procedure behind it, while `manage.py restore_drill` refuses unsafe *restore* targets - a guard that says nothing about somebody making a second copy of a production dump onto a laptop. The section is three layers.
