(1) **The rule**: production customer data stays in production; no ad-hoc production dump for a developer machine (`backup_db`/`restore_drill` are the restore path only, and `restore_drill`'s refusal is not a licence to copy); a non-production environment that needs realistic rows gets synthetic rows. (2) **The default path, which is synthetic data, not a compromise**: this project's realistic dataset already exists - the committed factories in `backend/common/testing.py` that the suite drives every list/detail/checkout/admin/health surface against - and a documented `manage.py shell` recipe (synthetic catalogue via `get_or_create`, a live `DEMO10` coupon, a `demo-buyer` account with **no usable password** so no shared credential exists, addresses on RFC 2606 reserved documentation domains so mail to them can never reach a person or land in the operator allowlist). Also stated: no production media (an uploaded customer photo is personal data on the `media_data` volume), CI needs no data step at all (its database is created by the test runner), staging is seeded fresh and stays a separate environment. The production catalogue seed is cross-referenced to **SPEC-14-1** (section 14) as owned elsewhere - not built, not described here - with only the boundary named: a seed builds the catalogue, and the catalogue is never a vehicle for customer rows. (3) **The exception path**, for the rare case synthetic data cannot express: a table of eight rules for an approved anonymised/derived artefact - recorded approval (privacy owner + deployment owner) *before* any production row is read, transformation inside the production perimeter with the raw dump never leaving it, sanitisation **by data class** (every `AuditEvent.detail` payload, `TOTPDevice.secret` never at all, guest-order email) rather than a hand-picked column list, **human review of free text** because a name typed into a description is still personal data, keep-shape-drop-values, a named/disposable artefact that is never committed, shared or attached, an encrypted non-synced developer machine with a wipe at the end, and the decision recorded. It closes with the failure modes in the order they bite (an unmanaged second store; erasure honoured in production but not in an unrecorded copy; laptops multiplying it via cloud sync and screenshots; it scales with headcount; it is the wrong default to teach) and a **two-query self-check** (`User.objects.exclude(email__endswith="@example.com")` must be `[]`; eyeball the first `Order` rows) that a developer runs before any dataset leaves their machine.
- `backend/docs/retention.md` (new short section "Copies outside production", cross-referencing the runbook): the register governs *what may ever be copied*; the runbook governs *how a non-prod environment gets data*, and neither half is honest read alone - a copy made outside production inherits none of the retention or erasure machinery described in that document, so an erasure request honoured in production is not honoured in the copy, and a copy nobody recorded is a copy nobody can delete. States the synthetic default, the exception's approval/sanitisation/deletion rules in one paragraph, and that `restore_drill`'s unsafe-target refusal is a restore guard, not permission to duplicate a dump.
- Doc-only, as the task is scoped: no new command, no new env key, no model. The recipe is a shell snippet a developer pastes, not a shipped code path that could drift from the models. Suite: **1189 tests, OK (1185 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (4183 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected". No network, no secret, `master` untouched.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-10 (Section 22) - builder: email delivery verification + SPF/DKIM/DMARC (R-22.18)

- `backend/ops/management/commands/email_send_probe.py` (new, the thin helper): the gap was that `/health/`'s `smtp_configured` check (`ops/services.py`, `get_health`) is a **configuration** probe - host+user+password non-empty - so it is blind to the two failures that hurt customers: a wrong password that still reports `true` at boot and fails `535` at send time, and spam-folder delivery, where the send succeeds and the mail is silently filed. Delivery is the provider's and the receiver's business, so it is verified from the receiving end by one operator-triggered message. Every gate refuses by name instead of guessing: **allowlisted recipients only** (the recipient must be in `settings.ALERT_RECIPIENTS` - the deployment's own staff mailboxes that already receive every admin alert; an empty allowlist is refused, and `--recipient` naming anything else is refused), so the probe cannot be aimed at a customer and the command never reads a `User.email` - it cannot leak another person's address because it never looks at one; **dry run by default** (nothing is sent without `--send`, so the safe answer to "what would this do?" is the default answer); **a backend that never speaks SMTP is refused** (`console`/`locmem`/`dummy` accept and report success, so probing them would report a delivery that never happened - and it is exactly why the suite, which runs on `locmem`, can never send real mail through this command); **deployment facts only** in the body (environment, server, sender, timestamp - no order, no username, no product data), because the probe lands in mailboxes that get forwarded; and **a refused delivery is loud** - an SMTP rejection exits non-zero carrying the server's own status text (535/550/connection) and never the credential, because a send that looked accepted while quietly failing is the failure this replaces. The send goes through `common.notifications.send_email` (SPEC-19-1's single send path) so the probe exercises the app's real sender address and backend: a private code path could pass while the app's own mail failed. No new env key - `ALERT_RECIPIENTS` is reused deliberately as the allowlist, so the recipient set is operator-owned configuration that already exists rather than a second one to keep in sync.
- `backend/templates/emails/email_probe.txt` (new): the probe body, in the existing `templates/emails/` home. It tells the recipient the message is intentional and needs no action, so an operator mailbox owner who did not trigger it is not alarmed.
- `backend/tests/test_email_send_probe.py` (new, 12 tests): the gates, not the happy path. Dry run delivers nothing and prints the plan **without the password**; `--send` opens one SMTP conversation and delivers one message per allowlisted mailbox from `DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL`; a single recipient is matched case/whitespace-insensitively; a recipient outside the allowlist is refused *and* no connection is opened; an empty allowlist is refused; blank/duplicate allowlist entries collapse; each of the three non-delivering backends is refused even with `--send` (and `mail.outbox` stays empty); a rejected delivery surfaces `SMTPAuthenticationError` + the server text and never the credential; a real `User` row's email is refused **and** never printed; and the rendered probe message contains the environment/server facts and none of the customer's username, email or password. Hermetic: every send is attempted through a faked `smtplib.SMTP`, so no test opens a socket. Mutation-verified: deleting the dry-run gate turns `test_dry_run_by_default_delivers_nothing` red, and deleting the non-delivering-backend refusal turns the backend pin red. The command itself is at 100% line coverage.
- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md` (new section "Email delivery verification", between the uptime section and Backups): what `/health/` proves and what it cannot; the three-step probe procedure run against the deployment's own environment (with `docker compose run`, so it uses the same `EMAIL_*` the container runs with); the gate table; **when** to run it (after an SMTP credential change, after a DNS/provider change, after a restore to a new host whose outbound IP is not in SPF yet, and whenever a customer reports a missing email); the receiving-side receipt - the `Authentication-Results` SPF/DKIM/DMARC triple plus `Return-Path` vs `From` alignment, with what each failure means and the note that a forwarded copy's DKIM failure is not evidence about the original send; a per-record **SPF/DKIM/DMARC** table (`-all` never `+all`, one SPF record per domain, the 10-lookup limit, 2048-bit DKIM with key rotation, and DMARC staged `p=none` -> `quarantine` -> `reject` because jumping straight to reject silently breaks every transactional mail this store sends), stating honestly that the app does not sign mail itself (no DKIM library in `requirements.txt` - signing is the provider's job) and that the store has no marketing envelope so consent/unsubscribe machinery does not apply while deliverability still does; and a symptom -> cause -> fix table for spam-folder delivery, including that a stale credential still reports `smtp_configured: true` (precisely the case the probe exists for) and that this store has **no** bounce-processing code - bounces are an operator task, and an envelope sender must never be a customer's address.
- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md` (one existing line updated): the SMTP step of "Secret rotation" verified a send by "triggering one real send - the password-reset flow" and left it at that. It now points at `email_send_probe --send` against an `ALERT_RECIPIENTS` mailbox with a receiving-side confirmation, and says why: a restart is enough for the code, and the probe is what verifies the delivery - the old text would have had an operator testing a rotated credential by failing a customer's password reset.
- No new env key, no model, no new dependency. Suite: **1201 tests, OK (1197 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (4229 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", `black --check` clean on both new modules. No network in any test.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-11 (Section 22) - builder: every GitHub Action pinned by commit SHA

- `.github/workflows/backend-tests.yml`, `frontend-tests.yml`, `secret-scan.yml`: **supply-chain hardening.** Every `uses:` moved from a mutable major tag to the full 40-hex **commit SHA** of the version that tag pointed at, with the tag kept in a trailing `# vN` comment so the pin stays readable: `actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4`, `actions/setup-python@a26af69be951a213d495a4c3e4e4022e16d87065 # v5`, `actions/upload-artifact@ea165f8d65b6e75b540449e92b4886f43607fa02 # v4`, `actions/setup-node@49933ea5288caeca8642d1e84afbd3f7d6820020 # v4`, `gitleaks/gitleaks-action@ff98106e4c7b2bc287b24eaf42907196329070c7 # v2`. A tag is a mutable pointer: whoever controls the upstream repository decides what `@v4` fetches on any given day, which is exactly the trust this repository does not extend. **9 of 9** `uses:` across the three files are pinned; every SHA was resolved with `git ls-remote <repo> refs/tags/<tag>^{}` - the `^{}` peel matters for an **annotated** tag, and `gitleaks-action@v2` is one, so the tag object (`dcedce43...`) is *not* the commit and the peeled commit above is the pin. Nothing could not be verified.
- `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`: **0 `uses:`** - it is `workflow_run`-triggered and runs `gh api` plus one `ssh` into the deploy host, with no checkout and no third-party action, so there was nothing to pin. It was parsed and verified, not edited.
- Nothing else changed: no job logic, no triggers, no `paths` filters, no concurrency, no permissions, no secrets handling, no `with:` blocks. Each of the three files also carries a 7-line header comment stating the rule and the update procedure (resolve the tag with `git ls-remote ... refs/tags/<tag>^{}`, replace the SHA, never widen the pin back to a tag), so the next person adding an action does not reintroduce a floating tag.
- Verification: all four workflow files parse under `yaml.safe_load` (jobs unchanged: `test`; `unit-build`+`e2e`; `gitleaks`; `gate`+`deploy`), and a regex over every `uses:` line confirms **9/9** refs are a 40-hex SHA carrying a `# vN` comment - **0 unpinned**. The diff itself is only the 9 ref lines plus the 3 comment blocks.
- Suite unchanged and re-run green after the edit: **1201 tests, OK (1197 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%**, exit 0, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected". No secret, no network, no model, `master` untouched.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-22-05 (Section 22) - builder: `.gitguardian.yml` on `master` is an OWNER action (documented, not attempted)

- `backend/docs/deploy-runbook.md` (new closing section "Owner actions"): records the one item in this runbook that no agent may perform - adding `.gitguardian.yml` to `master`. The file is committed on the working branches but the `master` copy is missing it, and the GitGuardian App reads that filename **from the default branch**, so the rules are not in effect there however many feature branches carry the file. The owner's action is stated concretely (`git checkout <branch> -- .gitguardian.yml` on a branch that is then reviewed and merged); it is an owner action because every agent works on a feature branch and the default branch is off limits to all of them. **No agent attempted the change**: `master` was never pushed to, PR-against or edited. The section also states what is true in the meantime - `gitleaks.toml` and `secret-scan.yml` still run on every push and pull request, and none of the three scans rotates a secret.


## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-05 (Section 1, lines 29-151, [1.14]) - builder: a paid order is refundable

- **`backend/orders/models.py` - the `Refund` model (new), + migration `0015_refund.py`.** The gap this closes is named in the spec itself: section 1 lists "Payment gateway, refunds, webhooks" in the payments row, the finance operator's job is to "reconcile payments, refunds and financial reports" ([1.31]), and a `refunds.create` capability existed in `common/roles.py` with **no writer anywhere** - a paid order was irreversible and finance had nothing to reconcile. The row carries what a reconciliation needs: `amount` (Decimal, `max_digits=10, decimal_places=2` - the money width conventions.md mandates for every column in this schema), `reason`, `kind` (FULL/PARTIAL), `status`, the provider's `gateway_refund_id` (unique - one gateway refund can never be recorded twice, which is the reconciliation join key), `actor` (SET_NULL, like `OrderStatusEvent.actor`: deleting a staff account must never cascade into the financial trail), an `idempotency_key`, and `created_at`/`updated_at`. `Refund.refunded_total(order)` sums only PROCESSED rows and `Order.refundable_remaining` is the ceiling minus that sum, both quantized through the existing `common.money.quantize_money` - money is Decimal end to end and there is still exactly **one** rounding convention in this codebase. `status` is written twice inside one transaction (PENDING when the attempt starts, PROCESSED once the provider answered); a failed attempt leaves **no row**, so an outstanding refund is the absence of a row and the provider's own record stays the authority - there is no third, unobservable status.
- **`backend/orders/refunds.py` (new) - the gateway seam.** One thin function plus one typed error. It builds the client from `settings.RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` / `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` (no credential in code, no new env key), quantizes before scaling to the provider's minor units with the same helper `create_payment` uses for the capture amount, and raises `RefundGatewayError` for every outcome that is not a refund the provider acknowledged: a missing payment reference, any provider exception, and a 2xx carrying no refund id. That last case is deliberate - a "success" with no id would put a row in the reconciliation trail nobody can look up at the provider. The `except Exception` is not laziness: razorpay's four error classes each derive from `Exception` directly, so there is no base to catch, and a refund must never let a raw provider exception escape as a 500 the caller does not recognise (it only rolls back on a typed error).
- **`backend/orders/views.py` - `POST /api/admin/orders/:id/refund/` (`admin_order_refund`).** Gated by the **existing** `HasRefundsCreate` permission class (the unused `refunds.create` capability finally has its writer) - no parallel permission was created, and support is refused even though it may read and fulfil orders, which is exactly the split spec 1.1 spells out. The whole attempt runs inside `transaction.atomic()` with `select_for_update()` on the Order **and** on this order's refund rows (materialized, because an unevaluated queryset locks nothing; the same list answers the replay probe, so the balance is read from rows nothing can be appending to). The gateway call is inside that block, so a failure rolls the attempt back whole: no refund row, no payment-dimension move, no `refunded_at`, no audit record - pinned by test, not by comment. Idempotent-safe twice over: an `Idempotency-Key` collapses a retry onto the refund it already produced (one gateway call, one row), and without a key the balance gate alone still refuses to hand back the same money twice. Gates in the order they explain themselves: no refundable balance left -> not a captured payment -> no gateway payment reference (this is where a cash-on-delivery order is refused, since there is no provider payment to reverse) -> malformed/non-positive amount -> amount above the balance. On success the payment dimension moves to the choices `orders/state.py` already declared (`partially_refunded`, then `refunded`) - **no new choice was added** - `refunded_at` is stamped once (is-none guard, the `[R-8.16]` pattern), and the mutation is written to the privileged-action trail with `common.audit.log_api_action`, the same call `admin_order_fulfill`/`admin_order_cancel` make, inside the same transaction.
- **`backend/orders/admin.py` - `RefundAdmin`**, a read-only window: view gated on `refunds.create` (finance/admin), add/change/delete capability-less, and add/delete refused even for the superuser bypass, because a hand-written row here would be money movement with no payment behind it.
- **`backend/config/urls.py` - the route itself** (v1 `admin/orders/<id>/refund/` plus the legacy `api/admin/...` alias, same view objects, no duplication). This is one file outside the task's declared 5: an endpoint that is not routed does not exist, and the admin orders seam is mounted here rather than in `orders/urls.py` because it belongs to the admin family, not the customer store family.
- **`backend/orders/tests_refunds.py` (new, 39 tests)**, behaviour not prose: full refund, partial refund, partials accumulating until the order closes, an omitted amount refunding exactly what is left, `refunded_at` stamped once and never moved, HALF_EVEN quantization (`0.015 -> 0.02`, and 2 minor units reach the provider), over-refund and beyond-the-remaining refused with nothing written and the gateway never called, non-money amounts refused (`ten`, `12,50`, `true`, `NaN`, `1e30` - the last two found real crashes in the first draft and are now 400s), an unpaid/failed/no-gateway-payment order refused, a uniform 404, gateway failure and id-less response both rolling back whole, the provider's message kept out of the response, the replay collapsing without a second gateway call, the key scoped per order, the race (two full refunds, and two partials that would together exceed the order: exactly one wins), and the permission matrix (support, customer, anonymous refused; finance allowed - the positive control). The seam has its own six tests, including one that overrides the settings keys to prove the client is built from env-backed config. Hermetic: Razorpay is mocked at `razorpay.Client`, no socket anywhere.
- **[R-1.14] the refund dimension is compatible with shipping.** `partially_refunded` is captured money minus a recorded refund - the Refund row is exactly what reconciliation reads - so `orders/models._require_captured_payment` accepts it alongside `captured` and the remainder ships; `pending` / `authorized` / `failed` are still refused. Without this a refund would have permanently stranded paid-for inventory: cancel is illegal from `confirmed` and `mark_shipped` is the only path to `fulfilled`, so there is no operator route out. Both shipped writers (the fulfil JSON seam and the admin bulk action) are pinned by `PartiallyRefundedShipmentTests`.
- Suite: **1243 tests, OK (1239 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (4806 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected". `black --check` is clean on the three new files; `orders/{views,models,admin,state}.py` and `config/urls.py` were already not Black-formatted as files at HEAD (pre-existing drift, untouched here, as SPEC-22-04 recorded) - every line this commit adds is inside 88 columns. No network, no secret, no new env key, no new dependency, `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B01 = SPEC-1-05 (Section 1, [1.14]) - builder FIX CYCLE 1 (audit of 7b1ab40: 8 findings)

The audit confirmed the parts that are hard to get right - over-refund is genuinely impossible under concurrency, the rollback is genuine and mutation-verified, the money arithmetic reuses the one existing convention, idempotency works, the pre-existing `refunds.create` permission class was reused, and the tests assert behaviour. Eight findings were all real; all eight are fixed here.

- **[P1] a partially-refunded order could never ship again - the interaction I named instead of fixing.** `_require_captured_payment` accepted `captured` only, so the moment a refund moved the payment dimension to `partially_refunded` all three shipped writers (the fulfil JSON seam, the admin bulk `mark_shipped`, the admin change form) refused with "payment must be captured". There is no operator escape: cancel is illegal from `confirmed` and `mark_shipped` is the only path to `fulfilled`, so paid-for inventory was stranded permanently. The precondition's own docstring justified the strictness with "no refund flow yet (V-03)" - and this commit IS that refund flow, so the rationale was void. `_require_captured_payment` now accepts `partially_refunded` beside `captured` (still refusing `pending` / `authorized` / `failed`, none of which is captured money) and the false clause is gone. `PartiallyRefundedShipmentTests` pins it through **both** writers: the fulfil seam (200, status `shipped`, fulfilment `fulfilled`, and the refund row untouched - shipping reconciles against it rather than erasing it) and the admin bulk action, plus the three still-refused payment states.
- **[P2] `Refund.amount` was `max_digits=12`; `conventions.md:15` mandates `max_digits=10, decimal_places=2` for every money column.** The bible beats the task text, and "unreachable headroom" was not a defence - the column admitted a refund larger than any `Order.total_amount` could justify. The model and the unreleased migration `0015_refund.py` are corrected in place (the migration was introduced by the commit under audit, so editing it keeps the history linear), and the justifying comment is deleted.
- **[P2] the changelog row carried numbers I had not measured** ("1239 tests ... 4744 stmts"). Corrected to the real, re-measured figures, which also contradicted my own task handoff. The row's "two consequences" bullet is replaced by the truth: the shipping interaction is fixed (P1 above) rather than deferred.
- **[P3] four comments/docstrings asserted a refund flow that now exists**, and left the codebase lying in the files this commit already touched: `orders/state.py` ("no refund flow yet (V-03); reconciliation is manual by design" on the cancel rationale, and "No writer exists yet for authorized / partially_refunded / refunded"), `orders/models.py` (the `[R-8.16]` note saying no writer touches `refunded_at`), `orders/admin.py` (the cancel skip message, the change-form error, and two docstrings saying a refund "does not exist here"), and `orders/views.py` (the cancel docstring + its 409 body). All now state what is true: a paid order still cannot be **cancelled** (the machine declares no edge into `cancelled` from confirmed/shipped/delivered), and its money comes back through the refund seam, which records a Refund and moves the payment dimension without moving the order status. `authorized` is the only payment value still without a writer.
- **[P3] two comments stated a reason that was not the mechanism.** The write site claimed the payment dimension "moves only along declared edges (PAYMENT_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS)" while `payment_transition_allowed` was never called there; it now **is** called - the eligibility gate asks the machine whether the row's current payment declares an edge to either refund value instead of restating the eligible states as a tuple, and the write-site comment names the balance gate as what chooses between the two targets. The lock comment claimed the balance was "read from rows nothing else can be appending to"; the balance comes from `refundable_remaining`'s aggregate, and what makes the attempt safe is the **Order row lock** every refund writer takes first - the comment now says exactly that (the locked list serves the replay probe, and the queryset is materialized because that is what makes the lock take effect).
- **[P3] containment by construction**: the one refund test that had no Razorpay mock (`test_a_failed_payment_cannot_be_refunded`) now patches the client and asserts `refund.create` was never called, so no test can reach the gateway even if a gate is reordered.
- Suite after the fixes: **1243 tests, OK (1239 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (4806 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", `black --check` clean on the new files. No new model, no new env key, no network, `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B02 (Section 1, lines 29-151, [1.15]) - builder: Razorpay can tell the server what happened to a payment

- **`backend/orders/webhooks.py` (new) - `POST /api/v1/webhooks/razorpay/`.** The gap: the only statement this store had about a payment was the customer's browser (`orders.views.verify_payment`), so a dropped callback left a paid order pending forever, and a client-side verify was only as trustworthy as the machine that sent it. Spec section 1 puts "Payment gateway, refunds, webhooks" in the payments row, 11.2 makes "verify webhook signatures" / "handle duplicate webhook deliveries" payment requirements, and the threat table answers webhook spoofing with "signature verification and replay/idempotency controls".
- **Signature verification is the credential, and it is mandatory.** HMAC-SHA256 over the **raw** request body (`request.body`, read before anything parses it - DRF marks `request.data` access as consuming the stream, so a signature over a re-serialized payload is not a signature over what was sent), compared with `hmac.compare_digest` against the env-driven `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. A missing header, a wrong digest and a signature over different bytes all get one uniform 400 and write nothing. An **unconfigured** secret answers 503 for every delivery: a deployment that forgot the key cannot degrade into an unverified endpoint that moves money.
- **`PaymentEvent` (new model + migration `0016`), replay-proof.** `event_id` (the provider's per-delivery id) is **unique** and that constraint - not a check-then-write - is the replay authority: the insert runs **before** the handler inside one `transaction.atomic()`, so a second delivery of the same id collides, the whole block rolls back, and the endpoint acknowledges a replay without a second effect. The retry/IntegrityError shape is `create_payment`'s payment-intent pattern verbatim (conventions.md:17); an IntegrityError that is *not* the event collision is re-raised as a 500 (so the provider redelivers) instead of being acknowledged. `outcome` records what the handler decided - `applied` / `refused` / `recorded` - because "the provider says this happened and we did nothing" is exactly what a reconciliation has to read. `order` is nullable/SET_NULL, so an event naming an order this store does not know (or a deleted one) still survives.
- **The capture reconciler drives the EXISTING state machine; no status literal is written here.** Inside `transaction.atomic()` with `select_for_update()` on the Order, it asks `transition_allowed` and `payment_transition_allowed` before anything moves, and takes its target status from the machine itself via the new `orders.state.status_for_payment(PAYMENT_CAPTURED)` (the inverse of `payment_for_status`, added so a writer never names `"confirmed"` beside `"captured"`). Gates, in the order they explain themselves: no order bound to the claimed gateway order id -> an order already bound to a **different** gateway payment -> captured money that is not this order's `total_amount` -> a payment dimension that already means captured (`captured`/`partially_refunded`/`refunded`, so a duplicate *fact* under a new event id is recorded and refused instead of appending a second transition to the audit trail) -> a machine edge that does not declare the move. On success: both lifecycle dimensions, the gateway payment reference, `paid_at` (is-none guard), an `OrderStatusEvent` carrying the new `TRIGGER_PAYMENT_WEBHOOK`, the `PAYMENT_VERIFIED` + `ORDER_PAID` audit rows and the same customer notification `verify_payment` sends - all in that one transaction.
- **Inventory is not duplicated.** The stock decrement and its `StockMovement` rows are SPEC-12-02's confirm step under its own product locks; a second copy here would risk the double decrement SPEC-1-B01 just fixed for refunds. What the webhook does write is the order's active holds -> `CONVERTED` (one idempotent `update`, the same statement `verify_payment` writes), so a paid order can never have its holds swept away as expired. Finishing a webhook-confirmed order's inventory commit is the SPEC-12-03 reconciler's job - named here rather than faked.
- **A refund event is RECORDED, not re-issued (requirement 8, justified).** `payment.refunded` / `refund.processed` means money went back at the gateway - possibly the SPEC-1-05 seam's own refund echoing back, possibly an out-of-band refund at the provider. Writing a `Refund` row from it would double-spend the refundable balance against the row the refund writer already recorded, and would have to invent the operator `reason` and `actor` a financial trail requires (`Refund.reason` is NOT NULL by design). So the delivery is recorded (with the payment it names, and the order when provable), logged at WARNING for an operator, and moves nothing - pinned by test, including `Refund.objects.exists()` staying False.
- **Credential placement (requirement 7): `authentication_classes([])` + `AllowAny` + `throttle_scope("webhook")`.** The gateway holds no session cookie and no bearer token, so opting out of the credential chain is what places the endpoint past the SPEC-17-03 CSRF gate - and a test proves it with a session cookie and no CSRF token (the cookie alone must not turn a signed delivery into a 403). Exempt from CSRF is not exempt from authentication: the signature is the credential here, and one test pins that knowing an order id is not enough to mark it paid.
- **Config is env-driven (2 files outside the declared 5, stated): `config/settings.py` gains `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (no default - an unconfigured value is empty, never a guessable literal) and the `webhook` throttle rate, both documented in `backend/.env.example`.** An endpoint that verifies a signature cannot have that signature hardcoded, and a public mutating endpoint needs a throttle scope (conventions.md:24), so both keys follow from the same non-negotiables that scoped the task. Both files are otherwise untouched (settings.py is a pure insertion).
- **`config/tests.py`: the absence pin is replaced, not preserved.** `test_webhooks_prefix_reserved_until_webhook_endpoint_exists` asserted a `Resolver404` for `/api/v1/webhooks/razorpay/` - it pinned the absence of exactly what this task builds. It is now `test_webhooks_family_carries_the_payment_webhook_endpoint`, pinning the mount in both directions (`reverse("v1:webhooks:razorpay-webhook") == "/api/v1/webhooks/razorpay/"` and `resolve(...).func is razorpay_webhook`) so a moved mount fails in the suite rather than silently in Razorpay's dashboard; the now-unused `Resolver404` import and the stale docstring sentence are gone.
- **`orders/tests_webhooks.py` (new, 33 tests), behaviour not prose.** A valid signature reconciles the order; an invalid signature, a missing signature, a signature over different bytes, an unsigned forged payload and an unconfigured secret each move no money and write no row; a replayed event id leaves exactly one `PaymentEvent`, one `OrderStatusEvent`, one `PAYMENT_VERIFIED`/`ORDER_PAID` audit pair, one email and one converted hold; a second event for an already-captured order is recorded and refused with `paid_at` unmoved; an event for an unknown gateway order, a different payment id, a wrong amount, a cancelled order and three malformed capture entities are each recorded and refused; a negative amount and a string amount store no money; refund events (both spellings, with and without a payment reference) record without a refund row; an unhandled event type and a payload with no event type are recorded; signed-but-non-JSON, non-object and undecodable bodies are 400 with nothing recorded; GET is 405; and the endpoint's credential/throttle shape is pinned structurally. The signature in these tests is computed with the same HMAC the provider uses - that is the contract, not a mock - so nothing here touches the network.
- Suite before: **1243 tests, OK (expected failures=4)**, coverage **100.00%** (4806 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0. Suite after: **1276 tests, OK (expected failures=4 unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5310 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0; `black --check` clean on the three new files (`orders/webhooks.py`, `orders/tests_webhooks.py`, `config/tests.py`). The pre-existing non-Black drift in `orders/{views,models,state}.py` and `config/urls.py` is pre-existing (SPEC-22-04 recorded it); this commit does not add to it - every line it adds to those files is Black-formatted. No secret in code, no network, `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B02 FIX CYCLE 1 (Section 1, [1.15]) - builder: audit of af2109c, 4 bugs + 1 production-portability item

The auditor confirmed the hard parts and they are untouched here: raw-body HMAC verified in both directions (a re-serialized payload is correctly REJECTED), fail-closed on an unconfigured or whitespace secret, replay leaving exactly one event / one status event / one email across 5x redelivery, a non-replay IntegrityError surfacing as a 500 so the provider redelivers, the payload never trusted alone (a forged signature on a known order moves nothing; 1200.49 against an order of 1200.50 is refused), no other endpoint weakened, side effects idempotent with no double decrement, `payment.refunded` record-only correct, Decimal-only money, and all three guards mutation-proven non-vacuous. Four real bugs and one portability defect are fixed; no behaviour that passed was churned.

- **[P2] an attacker-reachable 500 on a public money endpoint (`orders/webhooks.py`).** `hmac.compare_digest(expected, provided.strip())` raises `TypeError: comparing strings with non-ASCII characters is not supported` when the signature header contains a non-ASCII character - reproduced directly (`compare_digest('a'*64, 'e-acute'*64)` -> `TypeError`), and reachable by an anonymous `POST` with `X-Razorpay-Signature: "e-acute"*64`. It fails closed (no state change), so it is not P1, but it breaks the uniform-response convention and a 500 tells Razorpay to retry. `_signature_matches` now normalizes the candidate first: an ASCII-encoding failure returns False and the length is checked against `WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_HEX_LENGTH` (derived as `hashlib.sha256().digest_size * 2`, so the expected width comes from the digest rather than a chosen constant) before any comparison. Pinned by `test_non_ascii_signature_header_is_refused_not_crashed` (400, no row, no state change) and `test_signature_of_the_wrong_length_is_refused`.
- **[ALSO-CONSIDERED, applied] `.strip()` is gone from the signature comparison.** It accepted a digest with a trailing newline, which Razorpay - which signs and compares literally - never sends. The candidate is compared verbatim now, pinned by `test_signature_with_surrounding_whitespace_is_refused`. The `event_id` header keeps its `.strip()`: that value is this store's own dedupe key rather than a value compared against the provider's bytes.
- **[P2 portability] provider-controlled strings were unbounded, so behaviour differed between the development database and production.** `event_id` / `event_type` / `gateway_payment_id` are `VARCHAR(100/50/100)`: SQLite ignores the width and accepts anything, Postgres enforces it and raises `DataError` on the same request - i.e. an anonymous caller could not produce a 500 locally and could produce one in production on the endpoint this task exists to harden. Every value is now checked against the column itself (`_fits` reads `PaymentEvent._meta.get_field(...).max_length`, so the bound cannot drift from the schema) rather than a hardcoded number: an oversized `event_id` or `event_type` is refused 400 before anything is recorded (a truncated replay key would dedupe two different deliveries onto one row), and an oversized payment reference inside a payload is recorded and refused with NO reference stored rather than a truncated one, which would read like a real payment id. Four tests: oversized event id, oversized event type, oversized capture reference, oversized refund reference - all refused cleanly, none a 500, no money moved.
- **[P3] two Black regressions this commit had introduced, and one false changelog claim.** `orders/state.py`'s `WEBHOOK_REFUND_EVENTS` spanned three lines where Black wants one (76 cols) and `config/urls.py` carried a stray double blank line after the new import; both are corrected, so `black --diff` now reports only the pre-existing hunks in those files and nothing inside this commit's lines. The changelog row's own Black gloss was wrong - it listed `config/tests.py` among the Black-dirty files when that file is clean - and is corrected in place above (the three files Black accepts are `orders/webhooks.py`, `orders/tests_webhooks.py`, `config/tests.py`).
- **[P3] a bare string literal contradicted the vocabulary comment six lines above it.** `WEBHOOK_REFUND_EVENTS` contained `"refund.processed"` inline, in a block whose comment says "no string literal can drift away from the vocabulary the rest of the file declares". It is now the named `WEBHOOK_EVENT_REFUND_DONE = "refund.processed"` beside the other event names (named for what it means, because only the provider gets to spell the literal), and the set is built from constants only.
- **[P3] `docs/architecture.md` was knowingly false - swept, not just line 62.** "No webhook listener - payment truth arrives only via the client callback" is untrue since af2109c; the neighbouring entries were stale for earlier tasks and are corrected in the same pass rather than left next to the fix: item 2 now says stock IS reserved between checkout and payment (`StockReservation` mint/convert/release/expire) while keeping the honest caveat that the checkout availability gate is advisory and `verify_payment`'s locked re-check is still the authoritative 409; item 4 now describes the two live URL families and the v1 prefixes instead of claiming no versioning prefix exists; item 5 says media is served by an explicit `django.views.static.serve` route against the env-driven `MEDIA_ROOT` (the `static()` helper returns an empty list when `DEBUG=False`, which was the 404-in-production bug) instead of repeating that helper's name; item 3 (no cart ownership) was checked against the model and stays true - `Cart` has no user FK.
- Suite after the fixes: **1283 tests, OK (1279 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5383 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0; `black --check` clean on `orders/webhooks.py` / `orders/tests_webhooks.py` / `config/tests.py` and reports nothing inside this commit's lines in `config/urls.py` or `orders/state.py`. No new model, no migration change, no new env key, no network, no secret, `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B02 FIX CYCLE 2 (Section 1, [1.15]) - builder: a doc-truthfulness fix that stated a path that does not exist

The cycle-1 sweep corrected a stale claim and introduced a false one in the same sentence. That is the failure mode the sweep was meant to remove: the same file already carried the correct path at line 35, so the new line contradicted its own document.

- **[P3] the client-callback path in `docs/architecture.md` was wrong.** Cycle 1 wrote `/api/orders/verify-payment/`; that route does not exist. Verified directly against the urlconf rather than taken on report: `reverse("verify-payment")` -> **`/api/orders/payment/verify/`**, `resolve("/api/orders/payment/verify/")` -> `orders.views.verify_payment`, `resolve("/api/v1/store/orders/payment/verify/")` -> the same view (the v1 alias), and `resolve("/api/orders/verify-payment/")` -> `Resolver404`. The literal is corrected to `/api/orders/payment/verify/`, which is the path line 35 of the same file already named.
- **Every other endpoint literal in the file was audited by resolution, not by reading.** A probe extracted every concrete path in the document (both the backticked literals and the bare ones in the fenced flow block) and resolved each against the live urlconf: `/admin/`, `/api/cart/`, `/api/orders/checkout/`, `/api/orders/payment/`, `/api/orders/payment/verify/`, `/api/v1/webhooks/razorpay/` - all six resolve. The two entries that do not resolve are the URL **families** written with `...` / `{store,account,admin,webhooks}` syntax, which are prefixes rather than routes; each was verified separately (the legacy family and all four v1 prefixes resolve). The reported literal was the only wrong endpoint path.
- **Three further false references found and fixed in the same commit** - pre-existing, not introduced by cycle 1, but knowingly left in a file whose one job in this section is being true, and all three confirmed wrong before being touched: `orders/views.py:53-74` (the "checkout requires the session key to locate the cart" reference - those lines are imports today; the session-key lookup is in `orders.views.create_order`), `orders/views.py:249-250` (the "comment at" reference for inventory/coupon/cart cleanup happening only after verification - those lines are `_next_order_sequence`; all three effects are inside `orders.views.verify_payment`), and `config/settings.py` "line 36-37" for the `SECRET_KEY` guard. Each now names the symbol instead of a line number, because a line number is false again after the next commit that inserts above it - which is how all three went stale.
- **The five cycle-1 entries were re-verified line by line and all five stand.** (1) the webhook listener exists (`/api/v1/webhooks/razorpay/` resolves to `orders.webhooks.razorpay_webhook`, `PaymentEvent` exists); (2) stock IS reserved between checkout and payment, and the advisory-gate caveat is accurate - the checkout gate really does compare `cart_item.quantity > product.stock` (on-hand, not net of other holds) and its own comment calls the check advisory, with the authoritative re-check in `verify_payment` returning 409 - and the expiry claim is backed by a real writer, `products/management/commands/expire_reservations.py`, which flips lapsed active holds to `EXPIRED` and lands `StockMovement` rows (SPEC-12-03); (3) `Cart` has no user FK, only `session_id` (re-read `cart/models.py`); (4) both URL families are live and re-use the same view objects; (5) media is served by `django.views.static.serve` against `MEDIA_ROOT`, which `config/settings.py` resolves from `DJANGO_MEDIA_ROOT`, with that route deliberately last in the urlconf.
- Suite after (doc-only change, re-measured anyway): **1283 tests, OK (1279 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5383 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0. No code touched, no migration, no new env key, `.gitleaks.toml` untouched (release-engineer's), `master` untouched.
## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B03 (Section 1, [1.33]/[1.34]) - builder: the superadmin tier exists, and the inventory/fulfilment operator has a packing queue without order visibility

Spec 1.1 ends its role table with two tiers rather than one, and adds the rule that makes the second one necessary: line 142-146 gives Superadmin "high-privilege settings, access and platform configuration", line 137 gives Admin "users, roles, settings and operational access", and line 150 says plainly that "admin is not one giant permission". The tier did not exist - `common/roles.py` declared six roles with `ROLE_ADMIN` on top, and the only escalation was Django's `is_superuser` flag bypass, which is a trust anchor, not a role. Line 110 was the second half of the task and was a live DEVIATE: the inventory/fulfilment operator manages "stock, packing, shipping and returns" and could not pack or ship at all.

- **The tier is a capability, not a label.** `ROLE_SUPERADMIN = "superadmin"` is added last in `STAFF_ROLES` (the tuple order IS the tier order), and the map grants the new tier exactly the three capabilities the spec's Superadmin row names - `platform.configure` (line 146, new, superadmin-only), `settings.manage` (line 146 "high-privilege settings") and `staff.manage` (line 146 "access", and line 137 for Admin) - and NO operational capability at all. That is the deliberate reading: the spec grants the tier no catalogue, fulfilment, refund, discount or reporting authority, so a superset-of-admin tier would have been powers the spec does not grant. Least privilege also keeps the top of the map from being an unlabelled superuser. `platform.configure` is the one capability Admin deliberately does not hold, which is what makes it a tier above Admin rather than a synonym.
- **Deny-by-default still holds for the new tier.** `platform.configure` resolves through `CAPABILITY_ROLES` like every other identifier, so `HasPlatformConfigure` (the new named class) denies an unknown identifier, an anonymous caller and a role-less customer; and because `get_user_roles` intersects group membership with `STAFF_ROLES`, a stray "superadmin"-named group outside the map grants nothing. The existing drift pins (one named class per map capability, no pinned class outside the map, the full role x capability matrix) cover the new entry without being edited.
- **The escalation guard extends to the tier instead of being replaced.** `ROLE_GRANT_CAPABILITY = {ROLE_SUPERADMIN: "platform.configure"}` plus `common.permissions.user_may_assign_role` answers the question `staff.manage` cannot: may this caller touch the tier ABOVE its own? On the User admin the roles field's queryset is narrowed to `_assignable_roles(request)`, so an Admin is never even offered `superadmin`, and `_refuse_restricted_roles` refuses the submission with a named 403 on the interstitial leg - in BOTH directions, because it runs against the whole diff (`added | removed`) before any confirmation page is rendered. On the COMMIT leg the two directions are refused by two different gates and neither is a hole: the UNMINT is refused by `_apply_staff_roles` with a named 403 (it also covers a directly constructed call), while the MINT is refused EARLIER and differently - the narrowed field queryset fails form validation, so the re-rendered page answers 200 with `granted=False` and the refused role is simply never applied, `_apply_staff_roles` never running for it. The commit-leg MINT refusal is therefore covered independently by the direct-sync pin rather than by the round-trip POST. The diff is checked in BOTH directions: minting the tier is an escalation and so is unminting it, so an Admin can neither promote anyone - including itself - above its own tier nor strip it from an account. The pre-existing flag guard (`PRIVILEGE_FLAGS`) is untouched and still refuses `is_staff` / `is_superuser` / `user_permissions` for every non-superuser editor, admin role included.
- **The Django superuser bypass is byte-for-byte unchanged and is pinned as such.** `is_privileged` still reads `user.is_superuser or user_has_capability(user, "staff.manage")`; the new tier needs no second privileged-population definition because it holds `staff.manage` (it manages access), so mandatory MFA (R-17.9) reaches it through the existing rule rather than a widened one. `RoleAwareModelAdmin._holds_capability` / `_map_grants` still answer on the flag before the map is consulted, including for `platform.configure` and for an identifier no role grants.
- **The line-110 DEVIATE is closed with the least-privilege grant.** `orders.fulfill` is granted to the inventory role beside support. That capability is precisely the order-status walk `pending -> confirmed -> shipped -> delivered` plus the three admin status actions - i.e. packing and shipping and nothing else - so no new capability was invented and no existing one was split. What the operator does NOT get is the point: `orders.read` (order visibility), `orders.cancel`, `refunds.create` and `customers.read` stay with the roles lines 92/124/128 name them for, because "packing, shipping and returns" is not "see every order, cancel one, or refund one". Support's fulfilment authority is unchanged (line 92, "permitted order issues"). Proved behaviourally: the operator walks a paid order to `delivered` through `POST /api/admin/orders/<id>/fulfill/`, is refused cancel and refund, is refused the `orders.read` order LIST (`GET /api/admin/orders/`), and holds exactly `{products.read, inventory.read, inventory.adjust, orders.fulfill}` - an exact-set assertion, so a future widening of this role fails loudly. **Corrected in fix cycle 3:** those refusals are the JSON seam's, and the JSON seam never had the defect; the row's original unqualified "is refused cancel and refund" was true for the API and FALSE for the admin surface, where the packing queue cycle below left the change form and the `list_editable` cell able to commit `pending -> cancelled` - the destructive authority [6.12.4] reserves a confirmation and a captured reason for - with neither. The admin write path is closed in cycle 3's row, named there. (The admin-side counterpart of that pairing is the fulfilment QUEUE added in the fix cycle below: the operator lists and packs the orders awaiting it without ever holding `orders.read`.)
- **Migration decision: a new data-only migration, because `0001` cannot be reused.** `0001_sync_staff_role_groups` reads the LIVE `STAFF_ROLES`, so it only ever created the roles that existed when it RAN - and it has already been applied on every deployed database, so adding a role to the map cannot reach an existing installation. `common/migrations/0006_sync_staff_role_groups_superadmin` re-runs the same idempotent `sync_role_groups()` (`get_or_create`, so no existing group or membership is touched) and creates the one group 0001 could not know about. Its reverse deletes ONLY the `superadmin` group: 0001's reverse drops all six of its own groups, and repeating that here would delete live role assignments that predate this migration. A future tier gets its own migration rather than widening that tuple. No model changed, so `makemigrations --check` stays clean.
- **Six pre-existing pins asserted the pre-tier world and were updated to the spec's world - none was weakened, skipped or deleted.** They sit in four files. `tests/test_rbac_foundation.py` (three pins): `staff.manage`/`settings.manage` are now pinned to the exact two-tier set plus a second assertion that no operational role is in it, `platform.configure` is pinned to the top tier alone, and the role-assignment loop admits both tiers; `tests/test_staff_roles_admin.py`: the roles field is pinned to "exactly the roles an admin may assign" (all but the tier) with a new sibling test pinning that the top tier is offered all of them; `tests/test_audit_log.py`: the audit-log route's non-grantees are now derived from `CAPABILITY_ROLES["staff.manage"]` with an explicit assertion that both tiers are not among them; `tests/test_admin_rbac.py`: the inventory role's action set moves from `set()` to the three fulfilment actions, which is the DEVIATE this task closed, not a widened expectation. Black also aligned four touched files it had been reformatting around (a pre-existing over-long `list_display` line in `ops/admin.py`, module-docstring blank lines).
- Suite before (measured, this worktree at 454b6d5): **1283 tests, OK (1279 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure)**, coverage **100.00%** (5383 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0. Suite after, re-measured at the tip of this task by the fix cycle below: **1340 tests, OK (1336 pass + 4 expectedFailure - unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5456 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0. `tests/test_superadmin_tier.py` holds the 37 new tests of this commit, plus 18 more from the fix cycle below; 2 were added alongside the updated pins in the other three files. `black --check` reports no change inside any line this commit or the fix cycle added - each of the four Python files they touch (`common/admin.py`, `orders/admin.py`, `orders/views.py`, `orders/state.py`) carries pre-existing Black hunks that predate both, proved by running Black over the pre-change copies read from git. No new model, no new env key, no network in tests, no secret, `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B03 FIX CYCLE 2 (Section 1, [1.33]/[1.34]) - builder: the operator who can pack now has a queue to pack from, and the fulfil seam is scoped to it

Cycle 1 closed the line-110 DEVIATE in the capability map and shipped a changelog row that claimed more than the code delivered. The audit found the claim false in a way that mattered, and this cycle makes both the code and the row true.

- **[P2] the operator's fulfilment authority was reachable only by guessing a pk.** `orders.fulfill` maps to `OrderAdmin.capability_map["change"]`, and Django gates its changelist on view OR change - so the packing operator's grid was ALREADY answering 200 with the money columns (`total_amount`, `discount_amount`, `currency`, `coupon`, `payment_ref`), the customer name, and a search box over email/phone/full_name, while `has_module_permission` (which follows the view capability) kept the Orders module off the admin index. So the surface was real, undiscoverable, and WIDER than the role's mandate: an operator could read totals and probe customer records by typing a URL, and still had no page listing the orders awaiting packing. The capability map is deliberately NOT widened - `orders.read` also carries `export_csv`, and spec 1.1 lines 92/124/128 name that capability for other roles - so the fix adds a narrow door instead.
- **The narrow door is a declared, capability-gated "scoped viewer" in the `RoleAwareModelAdmin` idiom** (`common/admin.py`): `scoped_view_capability = "orders.fulfill"` on `OrderAdmin`, plus `is_scoped_viewer(request)` - true only for a caller holding that capability and NOT `orders.read`, so a superuser and every `orders.read` holder keep the ordinary full surface. Deny-by-default: the attribute is `None` on every other admin, and an unknown capability identifier grants nothing. It opens exactly one extra thing, module visibility on the index; `has_view_permission` still reads the map, so the generic capability-matrix pins are untouched and the global search and the saved-filter write endpoints keep refusing this role.
- **The surface itself is a fulfilment QUEUE, and the queue is the machine.** `orders/state.py` gains `FULFILMENT_QUEUE_STATUSES = tuple(ADMIN_FULFILMENT_NEXT)` - the statuses the packing walk can still advance, derived from the step map so the listing can never drift from the transitions it offers - and `OrderAdmin.get_queryset` filters to it for a scoped viewer. Everything that reads rows through this admin inherits that narrowing, which is why the three fulfil actions cannot be pointed at an order outside it either: a hand-posted `_selected_action` pk is filtered out of the action's queryset (pinned). An out-of-queue order has no change page for this role at all, because Django's `get_object` reads the same narrowed queryset.
- **What the queue exposes, each narrowing deliberate and pinned by absence-assertions.** The grid shows `id`, `order_number`, `city`, `status`, `created_at` - no customer name, phone, email, total, discount, currency, coupon or payment reference. `get_search_fields` narrows to the order reference, so a name/phone/email term finds nothing: the map's decision to withhold `customers.read` is enforced rather than merely unexercised. The change form shows the shipping address, the status and the fulfilment timestamps, its item inline (`OrderItemPackingInline`) lists product/sku/variant/quantity with no `price`/`subtotal`, and `get_readonly_fields` adds `order_number` so the checkout-minted reference cannot be rewritten from that form. The three status actions and the `status` list-edit cell were left exactly as they were, and that is the defect cycle 3 closes: on a scoped viewer the cell offered and accepted `cancelled`, and the change form beside it wrote the same edge plus the delivery address, so the queue was narrower than the editor it handed out.
- **`admin_order_fulfill` is scoped to the same queue, so a fulfil-only role cannot advance an arbitrary order by pk.** `orders/views.py` gains `_may_fulfil(user, order)`: a caller holding `orders.read` is unscoped exactly as before, Django's `is_superuser` flag is preserved as the bypass every other surface keeps, and an `orders.fulfill`-only caller may advance only a queued order. An out-of-queue order answers with the SAME uniform 404 an unknown id gets - which also closes a small leak: the old 409 body named the order's current status and allowed set, i.e. a status oracle on orders this role was deliberately not given visibility of (pinned in both directions).
- **One template guard, because the narrowing would otherwise 500 its own grid.** The saved-view bar's write endpoints gate on `has_view_permission`, which this role does not hold, so `OrderAdmin.changelist_view` skips `SavedFilterMixin` for a scoped viewer; `templates/admin/orders/order/change_list.html` therefore includes the bar only when the mixin's own context is present. Without that guard the per-model template reversed the save URL with empty arguments - found by running the surface, not by reading it.
- **Found while fixing, and stated rather than absorbed:** the order item snapshot inline has never rendered on the full surface for ANY role - Django skips an inline whose view, change, add and delete are all false, and `OrderItemInline` denies all four - so its `price`/`subtotal` were never actually leaking that way. The packing twin declares `has_view_permission` so the operator can see WHAT is in the parcel, which is the one thing this surface exists for; the full surface is untouched.
- Suite after (measured this cycle, this worktree): **1340 tests, OK (1336 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged from the 1322/4 measured before this cycle's edits)**, coverage **100.00%** (5456 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0; `black --check` reports no change inside any line this cycle added - every Black hunk abutting the new block shows the new lines as unchanged context, and the four Python files this cycle touches carry pre-existing hunks, proved by running Black over their pre-change copies read from git (`tests/test_superadmin_tier.py` was Black-clean before and is Black-clean now). 18 new tests, all in `tests/test_superadmin_tier.py`. No new model, no migration, no new env key, no network in tests, no secret, `.gitleaks.toml` and `.github/**` untouched (release-engineer's), `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B03 FIX CYCLE 3 (Section 1, [1.33]/[1.34]) - builder: the packing door can pack and nothing else - both admin write paths closed

Cycle 2 narrowed the operator's admin surface for READING and left the write path wide, so the audit found the one thing this task exists to prevent: a role holding exactly `{products.read, inventory.read, inventory.adjust, orders.fulfill}` and NOT `orders.cancel` could cancel a pending order through the change form AND through the `list_editable` cell, and could rewrite the delivery address on the same form. The narrowing, the index discoverability and the uniform 404 are untouched - this cycle only closes the write path.

- **What is now refused, and where, stated plainly.** On the scoped fulfilment surface (the inventory/fulfilment operator, `/admin/orders/order/`) `status=cancelled` is refused on BOTH admin write paths, and `address` / `city` / `state` / `pincode` (plus the checkout-minted `order_number`) are refused on the change form. The status widget and the grid's status cell no longer OFFER a `cancelled` option to that role either. Refusal is a refusal, not a message: the persisted order is unchanged - same `status`, same `cancelled_at` (still NULL), no `OrderStatusEvent`, no `LogEntry` - and the change form answers 200 with the field error. The role still holds `orders.fulfill` and nothing else was granted to it: no `orders.cancel`, no `orders.read`, so no `export_csv`, and the exact capability-set assertion from cycle 1 still passes unchanged.
- **Both write paths are closed by ONE declaration, not two fixes.** `pending -> cancelled` is a legal machine edge, so `save_model`'s transition guard could never catch it: the gate has to be about WHO may write WHICH value, not about which edges exist. Two deny-by-default attributes in `common/admin.py` do it - `scoped_writable_fields` (which fields a scoped viewer may edit at all) and `scoped_value_capabilities` (`field -> {value: capability required}`) - and they are enforced at the layer that owns the input, in `RoleAwareModelAdmin.formfield_for_dbfield`: Django builds BOTH the change form and the `list_editable` formset through that callback (`get_form` and `get_changelist_form` each pass it as `formfield_callback`), so a refused value is neither offered nor accepted, and a hand-posted one dies on the field's own validation before `save_model` is ever reached. `OrderAdmin` therefore declares exactly two lines of intent: `scoped_writable_fields = frozenset({"status"})` and `scoped_value_capabilities = {"status": {"cancelled": "orders.cancel"}}` - the second pinned equal to `action_capabilities["cancel_pending"]`, so the change form, the list-edit cell and the confirmed bulk cancel are one authority rather than three that can drift.
- **The field half is Django's own mechanism, not a hand-rolled filter.** `get_readonly_fields` withholds every editable model field the declaration does not name, and `get_form` EXCLUDES readonly fields from the ModelForm - so a posted address is not a form field and is ignored outright rather than validated away. It reads the withheld list from the MODEL and deliberately never from `get_fieldsets()`: the default `get_fieldsets` reaches back through `get_form` into `get_readonly_fields`, so consulting it here would recurse on any surface that declares no fieldsets of its own (proved while building it - the probe blew the stack, the model-derived list does not).
- **No over-blocking, pinned in both directions.** A cancel holder (support: `orders.cancel` + `orders.read`, therefore not a scoped viewer) keeps the `cancelled` option offered and can still commit `pending -> cancelled` from the change form with `cancelled_at` stamped; the admin role and the Django superuser keep the full form, the editable address, the grid cell with its cancel option, and a working changelist commit. The packer's own surviving work is pinned on BOTH paths too: it advances `pending -> confirmed` from the change form (one `OrderStatusEvent`, its own actor) and from the list-edit cell, so the fix narrows the surface instead of closing it.
- **Deny-by-default is a property of the base, and there is a pin for it.** `RoleAwareModelAdmin` declares NOTHING writable and NO gated value, so a ModelAdmin that opens a door without declaring its writes offers a read, not an editor (probed directly on a bare admin), and a value can never be writable by omission of a future map entry. The drift pin derives every target the machine can reach, subtracts the fulfilment walk's own targets, and requires the remainder to be exactly the mapped set - so the next non-fulfilment edge anyone adds to `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` fails the suite until someone names the capability that owns it.
- **16 new tests, all in `tests/test_superadmin_tier.py`** (that file is the honest home: `FulfilmentQueueSurfaceTests` beside them is the suite that introduced and pinned this surface, and a split would put a write-path pin in a different module from the read-path pins it depends on). Each negative test asserts the PERSISTED row, not a status code - status AND `cancelled_at`, plus the absence of the audit and log rows a committed write would have left - because a refusal that still moved the row would be no refusal at all. Proven to bite by mutating the fix and observing the failure, in both directions: with `scoped_value_capabilities` emptied, the change-form cancel returns 302 instead of 200 and the row reads `'cancelled' != 'pending'`, and the list-edit cancel writes `'cancelled' != 'pending'` too (7 failures); with the address fields added to `scoped_writable_fields`, the row reads `'1 Attacker Lane' != '1 Queue Lane'` (6 failures). Both mutations reverted, suite green again.
- **Two inaccurate claims in this file are corrected in place rather than left standing.** (1) Cycle 1's row said the operator "is refused cancel and refund" without qualifying the surface: true for the JSON seam, false for the admin surface at the time, now scoped and pointed at this cycle. (2) Cycle 2's row said "The three status actions and the `status` list-edit cell work as they always did, both through `save_model`'s machine guard" - which is precisely the unscoped cancel it never mentioned; that sentence now says what was true (they were left exactly as they were) and names the defect it created. (3) Cycle 1's closing sentence "No new model, no new env key, no network in tests, no secret, `master` untouched." was duplicated verbatim in the same bullet; one copy is gone.
- Suite before (measured at 047a90b, this worktree): **1340 tests, OK (1336 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5456 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0. Suite after, measured at the tip of this cycle: **1356 tests, OK (1352 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5471 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0. `common/admin.py` and `orders/admin.py` both report 100.00% (`common/admin.py` 105 stmts, `orders/admin.py` 254). `black --check` reports no change inside any line this cycle added: the seven hunks Black wants in the two source files are byte-identical to the seven it wanted in their pre-change copies read from git at `047a90b` (proved by running Black over those copies), and `tests/test_superadmin_tier.py`, which was Black-clean before this cycle, is Black-clean now. No new model, no migration, no new env key, no network in tests, no secret, `.gitleaks.toml` and `.github/**` untouched (release-engineer's), `master` untouched.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B04 (Section 1, [R-1.13]) - builder: a guest can check out, and read their own order back, with no login

Spec line 74 gives the guest exactly one job - "browse products and optionally check out without an account" - and spec 21.2's first acceptance row is that a guest can "browse, search, add to cart and complete an eligible purchase". `POST /api/orders/checkout/` answered 401 to anyone without a JWT, so a guest could build a cart and then had nowhere to go: the row could not hold. Spec 9.3 asks for the checkout to be bound "to the correct customer or guest session", which is what the guest identity below is.

- **The login wall is replaced, not removed.** `create_order` moves from `IsAuthenticated` to `AllowAny` with the reason stated at the decorator, and `_resolve_checkout_owner` settles ONE owner per submission that every writer below then reuses: an authenticated caller owns their account (and a body-supplied `guest_email` is ignored outright, never stored), an anonymous caller owns the guest email they present, which is required input and is validated with `django.core.validators.validate_email` before a row exists. The gates that stood behind the login wall are untouched: no cart is still the documented 404, an empty cart still 400, a blank required field still 400 naming the field. The endpoint is now a public MUTATING endpoint, so it takes a throttle scope (conventions.md:24) - it joins the existing `cart` budget on purpose, because checkout is the terminal mutation of the same session-cart funnel those rates already govern (one budget for add-to-cart -> coupon -> submit, not two that can be spent independently).
- **The credential is a 256-bit `secrets` token, unique globally, and it is the whole authorization.** `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` - never `random`, never sequential - inside the SAME bounded IntegrityError-retry loop that already guards order-number minting, so the unique constraint is the authority for both values and a collision converges the way a number race does (conventions.md:17). Uniqueness is global, not per user, because the lookup is "the order whose token is this"; it is safe because account orders store NULL and NULLs stay distinct in a unique index on both SQLite and Postgres (the shape `razorpay_order_id` above already uses). The token rides an `X-Guest-Order-Token` header and never a URL, because a URL lands in access logs, history and Referer. **CORRECTED IN PLACE by FIX CYCLE 2 (below):** the original text here said the token was returned by "the checkout that minted it, including both dedup collapse paths" - that was the P1 defect, and the disclosure is now the minting 201 alone.
- **Possession is the guard, and every miss is one byte-identical 404.** `GET /api/orders/guest/` (the token alone, which is unambiguous because the token is unique) and `GET /api/orders/guest/<order_number>/` (the token plus the customer-facing number as an optional cross-check) share one view whose lookup filter IS the authorization: no token, a wrong token, an unknown number, a token paired with somebody else's number and an account order all answer `{"error": "Order not found"}` - never a 403, which would confirm the order is real. The isolation probe is pinned in `orders/tests_guest.py` by a second guest holding a VALID token for their own order and pointing it at the first guest's order number: 404, byte-identical to the no-token answer, while the same token still reads its own row.
- **The dedup guards were re-pointed, and that is where the isolation actually lives.** `_find_duplicate_pending_order` now takes an owner filter, and for a guest that filter is `user IS NULL AND guest_email = ...` - never a bare `user=None`, which is every guest order in the table and would let one guest collapse onto (and be handed the token of) another's order. A keyed guest replay gets a database authority the account path already had: `(guest_email, idempotency_key)` is UNIQUE **conditionally on `user IS NULL`**, so the account pair keeps answering for account rows and keyless rows never collide. Two submissions still collapse only when they agree on identity AND the whole shipping payload AND the cart contents AND fall inside the dedup window - four agreements, which is what "the same purchase attempt" means for a caller whose only identity is the address they typed.
- **Two owners, never a hybrid - as a database fact.** A `CheckConstraint` refuses a row with no owner at all (unreachable by any customer and by staff) and a row with both (two competing owners on one sale); both refusals are pinned. `Order.user` keeps `CASCADE`: that is still the policy for the rows it applies to, and a guest row simply has no account to cascade from, so deleting an account cannot take a guest sale with it.
- **Stock behaviour is unchanged, which needed one column outside `orders`.** The SPEC-12-02 hold is minted in checkout, so a guest checkout mints it too - otherwise guest sales would carry no reservation and the oversell guarantee this task must not regress would simply not apply to them. `StockReservation.owner` therefore becomes nullable (NULL = "the checkout that minted this hold belonged to a guest"; the order FK beside it is always present), and the reservation is pinned: quantity, product, ACTIVE status and a future `expires_at`, with no account attached. No money arithmetic moved - all of it Decimal, all of it server-side, and the coupon path is pinned on a guest order to prove it.
- **Guest orders are first-class for staff (requirement 5), and three surfaces had to learn about the NULL user.** `Order.customer_name` and `Order.recipient` resolve the label and the address in one place, which is what `__str__`, the admin CSV export and the order-confirmation notification now read: the export walks the selected queryset and would have raised `AttributeError` mid-stream on a guest row, and the notification reads the recipient so the guest gets the confirmation instead of an exception swallowed into the log. `guest_email` joins the admin grid's search terms and the Customer fieldset, so a guest order is findable and readable the way a customer order is; the admin JSON seam and the B03 packing queue need no change (their querysets were never owner-scoped) and are pinned to prove it. Guest orders deliberately do NOT join `scoped_search_fields`: the packing operator still finds work by reference, never by probing customer records.
- **Nothing that dereferences `order.user` raises on a guest row (requirement 6), each path pinned with a real guest order.** The signed payment webhook confirms one (transition, trail, hold conversion, `actor=None`), the refund seam refunds one to `refunded` with the money unchanged, an admin cancel releases the hold, the order-confirmation mail arrives at the guest address, and `create_payment`/`verify_payment` answer the same uniform 404 an unknown id gets - they stay account-scoped, so a guest order is outside every account's scope. The authenticated path is unchanged: an account submission's history and detail still exclude guest orders, and its own labels stay the account's.
- **Stated boundary, not hidden:** a guest order can be placed and read, but it cannot yet be PAID through the customer payment seams - those remain authenticated and user-scoped, and a guest order is in no user's scope. Staff can still advance or cancel it from the admin. Token-authorizing `create_payment`/`verify_payment` is a separate money-path change with its own audit and is proposed as the follow-up task, not smuggled in here. The pre-existing observation that the packing queue's action-checkbox `aria-label` renders `str(order)` (an account order's username, and now a guest order's email) is also left alone as out of scope.
- **Two pins asserted the wall this task had to remove, and both were rewritten to the NEW contract rather than deleted or weakened.** `orders/tests.py::test_checkout_requires_authentication_and_cart` now pins the cart gate and the guest-email gate; `tests/test_e2e_purchase_flow.py::test_unverified_user_cannot_login_and_cannot_checkout` still pins the property it was written for - an unverified account can produce NO order attributable to itself - and now proves it the new way (400 for a nameless anonymous submission, and a guest submission that names an address lands with no user, never as `ghostbuyer`'s). A third pin in `orders/test_idempotency.py` moved from 401 to the guest-email 400 for the same reason.
- **44 new tests, all in `orders/tests_guest.py`** (its own module, beside `tests_refunds.py` / `tests_webhooks.py`, so the guest contract reads in one place): creation, token minting (proved by patching `orders.views.secrets.token_urlsafe`, so the CSPRNG source itself is pinned rather than assumed), both collapse paths and their isolation, every lookup miss shape, the staff surfaces, and the four null-user paths. The file is Black-clean; every other file this commit touches was already Black-dirty in the house one-argument-per-line style BEFORE the change. **CORRECTED IN PLACE by FIX CYCLE 2 (below):** the original claim here - "the hunks Black wants in them are the same pre-existing ones ... no line this commit adds is reformatted by Black" - was FALSE as a statement about the added lines. Comparing Black's own hunks against the added-line set (not the pre-change file state, which only proved the FILES were already dirty) found added lines Black would rewrite in `orders/models.py`, `orders/serializers.py`, `orders/urls.py`, `orders/views.py` and `orders/tests.py`; they are now conformant. Django-generated migration files are excluded, as they are for every migration in the repo.
- **Scope finding, declared rather than absorbed:** the ledger declared 5 files for this task, and four more were mechanically forced by making `Order.user` nullable. `products/models.py` + its migration (`StockReservation.owner`, without which a guest order cannot reserve stock at all), `orders/admin.py` (the export crash and the grid/search/fieldset), `common/notifications.py` (the recipient, without which the guest confirmation is lost) and this changelog. No new model, no new env key, no new throttle rate, no network in tests, no secret, `.gitleaks.toml` and `.github/**` untouched (release-engineer's), `master` untouched.
- Suite before (measured this worktree at `917f11d`): **1356 tests, OK (1352 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure)**, coverage **100.00%** (5471 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0. Suite after, measured at the tip of this commit: **1400 tests, OK (1396 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged)**, coverage **100.00%** (5948 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected" exit 0. `orders/views.py` 537 stmts, `orders/models.py` 161, `orders/tests_guest.py` 422 and `orders/admin.py` 254 all report 100.00%.

## 2026-10-01 - SPEC-1-B04 FIX CYCLE 2 (Section 1, [R-1.13]) - builder: audit of 0d9c438, 4 bugs (P1 keyed-replay credential disclosure, P2 varchar width trap, P2 case-sensitive guest identity, P3 Black)

- **BUG-1 (P1), what is now refused, and where.** `POST /api/orders/checkout/`, anonymous branch. Before: the KEYED replay guard matched on `(guest_email, idempotency_key)` alone, so a caller who knew only a victim's email and a low-entropy `Idempotency-Key` - from a FRESH session, with a DIFFERENT cart and their own address - got HTTP 200, the victim's `id`, the victim's `guest_token` (a permanent read credential for that order) and the victim's address/phone/full_name.
