The other two shapes (an absent `order_number`, and a reason code outside the vocabulary) are 400s refused BEFORE any lookup runs, so they are identical whatever the order number would have turned out to be and cannot disclose an order either. A malformed request is not hidden behind the 404, because that would only hide a real input error from the customer who made it; `_return_request_miss`'s docstring says so rather than claiming a uniformity that does not exist.

- **No credential that cannot fail.** Every ownership and stranger probe mints a REAL JWT by POSTing to `/api/accounts/login/` and presents it as a bearer (`login_as`, the `shipping/tests_tracking.py` pattern); `force_authenticate` is not used anywhere in the module. A forged bearer is refused by the auth stack itself (401) before the view runs, and a non-staff customer is redirected to the admin login rather than being told the queue exists.

- **Duplicates are prevented ATOMICALLY, and the database is proved to be the authority rather than the probe.** The customer seam takes `select_for_update()` on the ORDER row, probes for an open request under that lock, and answers a racing insert's `IntegrityError` with the same 409 from inside a savepoint. Underneath, a partial unique index (`orders_returnrequest_open_uidx`, `fields=["order"], condition=Q(status__in=RETURN_OPEN_STATUSES))`) makes "one live request per order" a database fact: `test_the_database_refuses_a_second_open_row_with_the_probe_skipped` neutralises the probe with a mock so the INSERT runs for real, and `test_a_direct_orm_insert_of_a_second_open_row_raises` writes two rows with no view involved. A rejected or closed request releases the order, so a customer whose request was refused may file again.

- **An illegal transition is REFUSED, never silently applied - on two layers, and the second one exists because of a measured finding.** `ReturnRequestAdminForm.clean_status` reads the stored status under `select_for_update()` inside the change-form transaction and raises a field error naming the allowed set; `ReturnRequest.save` re-checks the edge against the stored status behind it. The validation layer rather than an aborted `save_model` is deliberate and was chosen from Django's own source: `_changeform_view` calls `self.log_change(...)` unconditionally AFTER `save_model` returns, so a `save_model` refusal would leave a "Changed status" entry on a row whose status never moved. `test_an_illegal_transition_writes_no_change_log_entry` pins that, and `test_a_terminal_status_says_nothing_is_legal` pins the wording.

- **The queryset paths that bypass `save()` are refused, before the block that would poison the caller.** `ReturnRequestQuerySet.update` refuses `status` in kwargs and `bulk_update` refuses it in the field list, raising BEFORE Django's `atomic(savepoint=False)` wrapper opens (the reason `ShipmentQuerySet` records: a refusal raised inside it marks the caller's transaction as needing a rollback). `test_a_bulk_status_write_is_refused_before_the_block_opens` runs the refusal inside a real transaction and then proves the transaction is still usable. **What is NOT claimed, and is stated in the queryset docstring and in a test rather than left for a reader to assume:** `bulk_create` is NOT refused, so a row can still be inserted directly with whatever status it is given - a creation is not a move, and `test_a_row_can_still_be_created_bulk_and_deleted_in_bulk` says exactly that; `delete()` is NOT refused at the model layer (the ADMIN surface refuses it for every role including the superuser, pinned by `test_nobody_may_add_or_delete_a_request_through_the_admin`); and the `save()` edge guard reads the stored status without locking it, so it is a backstop behind the form's locked check rather than a substitute for it.

- **The SQLite/Postgres width trap, closed on every write boundary.** `_reject_oversized_value` reads the bound off the field (never a literal) and is applied by `ReturnRequest.save`, by `ReturnRequestQuerySet.update` and by `bulk_update` - a per-app copy of `shipping.models._reject_oversized`, declared as such: that name is private to the shipping app and reaching into it (or refactoring it into `common/`, outside this task's scope) would couple two model layers for one function. It measures `str` only, because Django routes `bulk_update`'s values through the same `update()` as a `Case` expression. `WidthGateTests` (6) and `WidthHelperTests` (3) cover both bounded columns, the unbounded one, and the non-string case.

- **Staff authority: `returns.read` / `returns.write`, support + admin, and a RECORDED DEVIATION.** `common/roles.py` gains exactly those two identifiers; `common/permissions.py` gains `HasReturnsRead` / `HasReturnsWrite`, because `tests/test_rbac_foundation.py` derives a class name per capability and asserts set equality in both directions. The admin map is `view: returns.read`, `change: returns.write`, `add: None` (a request is the customer's ask, never hand-written), `delete: None` (deleting one erases the review trail spec 6.8 line 1983 requires - and the superuser bypass does not reach the form either, which is pinned). **DEVIATION from spec 1.1 line 110** ("Manage stock, packing, shipping and returns"), which names returns for the inventory/fulfilment operator: that capability is NOT granted here, because SPEC-1-B03 pinned that role's exact set in `tests/test_superadmin_tier.py` and widening it would undo a shipped, audited contract. The consequence, stated rather than hidden: the physical half of a return (receiving and inspecting the parcel back) is not reachable by that role today. `test_the_packing_operators_capability_set_is_unchanged_by_this_task` IMPORTS `INVENTORY_CAPABILITIES` from that module rather than restating it, and asserts the consequence (403 on the returns changelist) as a test, so the deviation is a measured fact and not a comment. No `scoped_view_capability` on this admin, and the pin test asserts its absence.

- **One admin write path, and the `.first()` trap answered explicitly.** No `list_editable` cell and no bulk action on this surface (`test_the_admin_map_gates_exactly_the_capabilities_the_roles_map_grants` asserts both), so the change form is the only admin route that can move a status - which is what makes the queryset refusal a statement about the whole surface instead of one of two doors. On the read side, SPEC-1-B06's trap (a listing that reported only the newest row, so an older parcel vanished) is answered by a test of its own: `test_the_queue_lists_every_request_and_not_only_the_newest` files three requests on three orders and asserts one listing carries all three, each with its own reason.

- **The view returns a bare `Response` on purpose, so SPEC-1-B07b keeps a real job.** `_return_request_payload` builds five keys inline (id, order_number, status, reason_code, created_at) with explicit keys and no `'__all__'`, and a test asserts the rendered body carries no `total_amount`, `payment_status`, `guest_token` or address. A second, narrower representation here would be a second body to keep in step with the returns serializer B07b owns for the customer list/detail surface. There is NO throttle scope: the seam is `IsAuthenticated`, not `AllowAny`, and conventions.md requires one on public MUTATING endpoints - a new rate would mean editing `config/settings.py`, which is outside this task's scope. **Eligibility is one gate and no more:** a cancelled order is refused (the machine declares the cancel edge only from `pending`, so nothing was fulfilled). Spec 4's "where eligible" and spec 6.8's "Eligibility validation" describe a WINDOW the spec never defines, so no window and no magic number was invented here; that decision was handed to SPEC-1-B07b, and it was later OVERTURNED by the product owner - the window is now SPEC-1-B07d's configurable `ops.SiteSettings` row, so read "that policy is B07b's" as "B07b decided it, and the decision did not survive".

- **Named gaps, so they are not mistaken for oversights.** `reason_code`/`reason_note`/`order` are read-only on the admin, so this surface has nowhere for an operator's own words: spec 6.8's "Manual exception workflow", its decision note and its "Return/refund audit trail" (line 1983) belong with B07b's serializer work. Spec 6.8's remaining unbuilt features - authorization number, requested quantities, disposition, partial refund - are named there too. Spec 10.2's `closed` is terminal by the table above, which is a modelling choice this task declares rather than a spec sentence.

- **Mutation evidence, measured in THIS session on the final 69-test module, each mutation applied and reverted.** Removing the `save()` edge guard -> **FAILED (failures=1)** in 69; removing the queryset `update()` status refusal -> **FAILED (failures=1)** in 69; dropping `user=request.user` from the lookup -> **FAILED (failures=8)** in 69. The fourth is the one worth recording as a finding: removing `condition=Q(status__in=RETURN_OPEN_STATUSES)` from `Meta.constraints` produced **OK - zero failures in 69** - **the mutation never reached the schema**, because the test database is built from MIGRATIONS, not from the models. Re-measured against the migration itself (partial index -> plain unique on `order`) it is **FAILED (failures=2, errors=1)** in 69, naming both probes (`test_a_terminal_request_releases_the_order_for_a_new_one`, `test_a_terminal_status_does_not_hold_the_index`). A schema mutation that "passes" is a measurement mistake, not a green suite.

- **Suite: floor was 1617 tests, OK (1613 pass + 4 xf), 100.00% (7155 stmts), exit 0 - measured on this worktree before a line was written.** After this task: **1686 tests, OK (1682 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged), coverage 100.00% (7822 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0**; delta **+69 tests, +667 stmts**, all of it from the new module. `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", exit 0. `manage.py test orders.tests_returns` is **69 tests**, enumerated by the Django test loader rather than counted by eye: request shape 7, miss shapes 8, guest order 4, duplicates 7, money untouched 3, transitions 14, capabilities 9, admin form unit 4, queryset guards 4, width gate 6, width helper 3 = 69. `black --check orders/tests_returns.py` reports 1 file unchanged; the pre-existing files were NOT reformatted, because they are legacy-styled throughout (`@api_view(['POST'])` and friends) and a repo-wide Black pass would be an unrelated diff.

- **Scope for this task:** `orders/models.py`, `orders/migrations/0020_returnrequest.py`, `orders/admin.py`, `orders/views.py`, `orders/urls.py`, `orders/tests_returns.py`, `common/roles.py`, `common/permissions.py`, `backend/docs/changes.md`. **Beyond the ledger's core 5, declared up front and justified:** `common/roles.py` + `common/permissions.py` (a capability without its named class fails `tests/test_rbac_foundation.py` in both directions - B05 and B06 hit this), and `config/urls.py` was NOT needed at all: `orders/urls.py` is already mounted under both `/api/v1/store/orders/` and `/api/orders/`, so the customer seam needed no new registration. **Deliberately untouched:** `orders/state.py`, `config/settings.py`, every other app, `backend/docs/spec-run-state.md` and `backend/docs/run/`.

## 2026-10-02 - SPEC-1-B07a cycle 2 - builder: audit cycle 2 of 3. Four cycle-1 bugs fixed, none of the accepted surface disturbed

Cycle 1's structural guarantees are all still in place and were re-verified this cycle, not assumed: the partial unique index and its `IntegrityError` -> 409 race handling, the 36 `(from,to)` transition pairs, the structural guest exclusion (`user=request.user`, which a NULL-user row can never satisfy - still no input-driven bypass beside it), and the money-untouched guarantee (a text scan of the seam body finds no `Refund` import, no gateway call and no write to `payment_status` / `total_amount` / `refunded_at` / `refundable_remaining`; `payment_status` is now READ by the eligibility gate, never written).

- **BUG-1 (P2, the one that mattered): a 500 on a public endpoint. Every body field is now type-gated before any string operation.** Cycle 1 read the three fields as `(request.data.get(field) or "").strip()`, so a non-`str` reached `.strip()` and raised `AttributeError` - HTTP 500, reachable with any JSON client. The auditor drove five shapes to 500: `order_number: ["x"]`, `order_number: 1`, `reason_code: 5`, `reason_code: ["damaged"]`, `reason_note: {"a": 1}`. The new `_return_body` reads all three fields listed in `_RETURN_BODY_FIELDS` and applies one uniform rule: **absent or `null` -> the empty string** (which keeps `reason_note` optional and keeps a null `order_number` on the "required" 400 it has always got - both answers unchanged from cycle 1), **a `str` -> stripped** (padding still tolerated), **anything else -> a 400 naming the field, never coerced** (coercing would mean inventing an order number out of `["x"]` and guessing a note out of `{"a": 1}`). Shapes now refused with 400 across `order_number` (`int`, `float`, `bool`, `list`, `dict`), `reason_code` (`int`, `bool`, `list`, `dict`) and `reason_note` (`int`, `bool`, `list`, `dict`). A body that is not a JSON object at all - a bare list, string or int - parses fine and has no `.get` to call, so it is refused on the same path with `"A JSON object body is required"` rather than crashing on the lookup that follows. The docstring claim that a malformed body "is refused with a 400 BEFORE any lookup runs" was FALSE in cycle 1 (it covered only missing/bad-vocabulary strings); the code now makes it true and the sentence names every shape it covers.

- **BUG-1's oracle property re-verified AFTER the fix, which is the part that mattered.** The auditor had confirmed the 500s were byte-identical for an existing and a non-existent order, so they were not an existence oracle - but a fix that moved validation could easily have introduced one, so it is now asserted rather than assumed: `test_malformed_input_is_still_not_an_order_existence_oracle` compares `response.content` (not just the status code) for seven refused bodies that name an order, sent once against the caller's real order and once against an order number that does not exist; and for two bodies whose `order_number` is itself malformed it sends the same body as two different accounts holding different orders, proving the answer is built from the request alone. All byte-identical. One honest correction to the test as first written: my first draft compared a WELL-FORMED body and so asserted 201 == 404, which is not a leak but the feature working - the property is about REFUSALS, and the draft also re-logged-in per request and tripped the login throttle (429). Both were wrong tests, not wrong code; the module logs in once per test in `setUp`.

- **BUG-2 (P2): the eligibility gate no longer contradicts its own rationale. It is DERIVED, and the derivation is what the auditor asked for.** Cycle 1 refused `cancelled` with the rationale "the machine declares the cancel edge only from `pending`, so nothing was fulfilled" and then accepted `pending` - and it HAD to: `LEGACY_STATUS_DIMENSIONS` (orders/state.py) is the machine's own map of every status onto the two dimensions spec 10.2 declares, and it records `pending` and `cancelled` as the same row twice, `("pending", "unfulfilled")` both. Any rule that refuses a cancelled order for "never fulfilled, nothing to send back" therefore refuses a pending one for exactly the same reason; the measured `pending 201 | confirmed 201 | shipped 201 | cancelled 409 | delivered 201` was the contradiction showing. **The rule now implemented, read off the row and off the machine's own vocabulary:** an order is ineligible only while NEITHER dimension says anything happened - `order.payment_status != PAYMENT_CAPTURED` AND `order.fulfilment_status == "unfulfilled"`. `PAYMENT_CAPTURED` is state.py's declared capture point; `unfulfilled` is the declared default of `Order.fulfilment_status`. **Accepted set produced: confirmed, shipped, delivered. Refused: pending, cancelled.** `cancelled` keeps its 409 with a message that no longer names a rationale the code does not implement. The rule is a disjunction of the two dimensions, not `payment == captured` alone, on purpose: the machine WAIVES the capture precondition for COD orders and puts their capture point at delivery (`orders/models.py` 571-574), so a COD order that has SHIPPED must stay returnable while its money is still uncaptured - `test_a_cod_order_that_shipped_is_still_returnable_uncaptured` drives exactly that row.

- **The `payment_status=failed` question, decided explicitly rather than left open.** A return against an order the store never took money for is refused, and it is refused BY THE FIRST HALF OF THE SAME PREDICATE, not by a bolted-on special case: the machine reaches `failed` only from `pending` and declares no capture there (`PAYMENT_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS`), so "never paid for" and "money not captured" are one test. `test_a_never_paid_order_is_refused_on_the_payment_dimension_alone` drives the auditor's own probe - zero items, zero amount, `payment_status=failed` - and gets 409, where cycle 1 answered 201 with zero items in it. **No eligibility window was invented:** a whole-file sweep found no window number in the spec (one unrelated `day` hit at line 276), and the window POLICY - what the window is and whether it is tunable - was handed to SPEC-1-B07b. *(Superseded: B07b's own no-window outcome was later overturned by the product owner, and the window is SPEC-1-B07d's configurable `ops.SiteSettings` row. The sweep evidence here still stands; only the deferral's outcome changed.)*

- **BUG-2 is PINNED so the implemented set and the derivation cannot drift apart again.** `EligibilityDerivationTests` computes the refused set at run time from `LEGACY_STATUS_DIMENSIONS` and `PAYMENT_CAPTURED` - never restated as a literal list - then drives the endpoint for every status in the machine's map and asserts the status code equals what that derivation predicts, and that the row-level predicate agrees. `test_the_machine_says_pending_and_cancelled_are_the_same_order` pins the machine FACT the whole rule leans on (the two statuses share a dimension pair), so a future migration that separates them says so before the rationale quietly becomes false again.

- **BUG-3 (P3): the inventory-operator 403 assertion is DELETED, and the omission is now a ledgered gap rather than asserted behaviour.** Cycle 1 asserted `403` on the returns changelist for the inventory/fulfilment operator, which hardened a known spec gap into a contract: spec 1.1 line 110 reads `Inventory/fulfilment operator -> Manage stock, packing, shipping and returns`, so returns SHOULD be reachable for that role and today is not. The cycle-2 audit ruled that a MISSING requirement rather than a defect and ledgered it as **SPEC-1-B07c**, which widens `returns.read` / `returns.write` to the inventory role AND widens the `INVENTORY_CAPABILITIES` pin in `tests/test_superadmin_tier.py` in the same commit. The assertion is replaced by a comment naming B07c and quoting line 110. **The set-equality assertion that IMPORTS `INVENTORY_CAPABILITIES` is kept** - that is B03's shipped contract. No capability was widened in this cycle, and `common/roles.py` and `common/permissions.py` were not touched at all.

- **BUG-4 (P3): the queryset docstring no longer outruns the code, and the three named Black lines are fixed.** `ReturnRequestQuerySet`'s docstring said the paths that bypass `save()` are covered; `bulk_create` is NOT width-gated and NOT refused (the auditor measured `BULKCREATE-WIDE NOT REFUSED`). The claim is narrowed to name exactly `update()` and `bulk_update()` as the gated paths, and states the `bulk_create` gap, its reachability (ORM and admin shell only, never HTTP - the change form saves one row through `save()`) and why it is documented rather than gated. Narrowing was chosen over adding the gate: it changes no behaviour and adds no untested branch. **`orders/models.py` was the one file touched outside the declared scope, and only for that sentence** - the claim lives there and both options the auditor offered require editing it.

- **Black: measured, not asserted, and by the auditor's method.** Added lines intersected with the new-side line numbers of the hunks Black would apply to the final file (`git diff -U0 HEAD` added lines vs `black --diff` hunks that REMOVE something, so context lines and pre-existing debt are excluded): `views.py` 141 added / **0 dirty** [figure CORRECTED in cycle 3: this line said 142; `git show --numstat 5e0a4db` measures **141 added / 19 removed**. Every figure in this section is now git-measured rather than counted by hand.], `tests_returns.py` 296 added / **0 dirty**, `admin.py` 5 added / **0 dirty**, `urls.py` 0 added / **0 dirty**, `models.py` 14 added / **0 dirty**. **TOTAL: 0.** The three lines named by the audit are fixed (`orders/admin.py` `select_for_update()` chain parenthesised, `orders/views.py:2549` `@api_view(["POST"])`, `orders/urls.py` stray blank line before `]`), and the measurement caught a fourth I had introduced - a 15-line dict literal in the new oracle test that Black wanted exploded - which is fixed too. No whole-file reformat; these files carry pre-existing Black debt (views.py alone has 1183 lines Black would touch) that is not this task's. Migration `0020` untouched.

- **Mutation evidence, measured in THIS session, each mutation applied and reverted.** Restoring cycle 1's three `(request.data.get(f) or "").strip()` reads -> `manage.py test orders.tests_returns.MalformedBodyTests`: **FAILED (errors=22)** across 7 tests, every error an `AttributeError` (`'int'|'list'|'dict'|'bool'|'float' object has no attribute 'strip'`, and `'list'|'str'|'int' object has no attribute 'get'` for the non-object bodies). Restoring cycle 1's `if order.status == "cancelled"` gate -> `manage.py test orders.tests_returns.EligibilityDerivationTests`: **FAILED (failures=3)** in 5 - `test_a_pending_order_is_refused_like_a_cancelled_one`, `test_a_never_paid_order_is_refused_on_the_payment_dimension_alone`, and `test_the_accepted_set_matches_the_derivation_for_every_status` breaking on `status='pending'`. Two earlier mutation attempts were CONTAMINATED and are recorded because a contaminated number is worse than no number: an `if False: body = {}` scaffold left `body` unbound and produced 28 errors that were partly `NameError`, not `AttributeError`.

- **Suite, both ends measured in THIS session on this worktree.** Before: **1686 tests, OK (1682 pass + 4 xf), 100.00% (7822 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0** - the cycle-1 floor. After: **1698 tests, OK (1694 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged), coverage 100.00% (7950 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0**; delta **+12 tests, +128 stmts**. `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", exit 0 - BUG-2 changes no stored data, the gate only reads columns that already existed. `manage.py test orders.tests_returns` is **81 tests** (69 + 12: malformed body 7, eligibility derivation 5).

- **Scope for this cycle:** `orders/views.py`, `orders/tests_returns.py`, `orders/admin.py`, `orders/urls.py`, `backend/docs/changes.md`, plus **`orders/models.py` for the one BUG-4 docstring sentence** (declared in the BUG-4 bullet above). **Deliberately untouched:** `common/roles.py`, `common/permissions.py` (B07c's job), `orders/state.py` (read, never edited - every derivation reads its constants from there), `config/urls.py`, every migration, `backend/docs/spec-run-state.md` and `backend/docs/run/`.
## 2026-10-02 - SPEC-1-B07a cycle 3 - builder: audit cycle 3 of 3. The one open P2 closed: the payment axis now tracks every captured-money value the machine admits, not half of them

Cycle 1's BUG-1, BUG-3, BUG-4 and cycle 2's BUG-1..BUG-4 were re-read and deliberately **not touched**; no line of the accepted surface moved in this cycle except the eligibility gate's money half and its rationale, which is the whole of BUG-5.

- **BUG-5 (P2) CLOSED. Decision: (a) - widen the money half to the machine's FULL captured-money set, derived in `orders/state.py` rather than listed.** The finding was that `_return_eligible` read `order.payment_status == PAYMENT_CAPTURED` while its own docstring said "an order is ineligible only while NEITHER dimension says anything happened - the money has not been captured AND nothing has shipped". `PAYMENT_CAPTURED` is a single value, and the machine admits a second post-capture one, so a customer who had partly refunded an order was refused 409 on a state this repo's own shipped refund writer produces. Three independent pieces of machine evidence say `partially_refunded` IS captured money, and I took (a) on all three: (1) `PAYMENT_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` (`orders/state.py` 151-158) places `partially_refunded` STRICTLY AFTER `captured`; (2) `orders/models.py` 557-573, this repo's own `[R-1.14]` ship precondition, admits it ALONGSIDE `captured` - "partially_refunded is real money too - a capture minus a recorded refund"; (3) the refund gate at `views.py` 2358 already admits it as a refund source. (b) was the other coherent option and was rejected because the evidence is one-sided: it would mean recording as policy a refusal the machine contradicts on three separate surfaces.

- **The derivation, and why it lives in `state.py` (the one in-scope file beyond the four).** Spec clause invoked: `[R-10.1]`/`[R-10.4]` - "the order machine's ... transition table, allowed-from sets and helpers ... are consolidated here so a machine edge can only ever be changed in one place" (`state.py` module docstring), which is the same reason `PAYMENT_CAPTURED` is already a named constant there ("Named here because the machine is the single source for what a capture MEANS"). The new value is `CAPTURED_MONEY_PAYMENT_STATUSES`, computed as the CLOSURE of `PAYMENT_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` from `PAYMENT_CAPTURED`, less the values with no declared outgoing edge, via a new private helper `_reachable_payment_values`. The closure is what admits `partially_refunded`; the no-outgoing-edge filter is what excludes `refunded`. It belongs in the machine rather than in the view because both halves are facts about the transition table, and a view that restates them is precisely how cycle 2's "derived" gate came to track half the machine. **Measured value: `{'captured', 'partially_refunded'}`, 2 elements.** The view now reads `order.payment_status in CAPTURED_MONEY_PAYMENT_STATUSES` and contains NO literal for the money half.

- **THE FULL ENUMERATION of every value the machine admits, and the gate's answer for each - the generalisation of the bug, done by hand across all 6 payment values x 3 fulfilment values (18 rows, all driven through the endpoint).** `payment_status`: `pending/unfulfilled` **409** (never paid; the machine reaches it only from checkout), `authorized/unfulfilled` **409** (the gateway's step BEFORE capture - the store holds no funds on it), `captured/unfulfilled` **201** (money held), `failed/unfulfilled` **409** (never paid for; reached only from `pending`, no capture there), `partially_refunded/unfulfilled` **201** (CHANGED from 409 - this is BUG-5), `refunded/unfulfilled` **409** (unchanged, and now stated as a policy rather than left implied). `fulfilment_status`: any of `unfulfilled`, `partially_fulfilled`, `fulfilled` is ACCEPTED for **all six** payment values, because the goods-moved half of the disjunction does not consult the money at all - which is what keeps a COD order returnable while its money is still `pending`. **No further value is mishandled:** the other five payment values and both non-default fulfilment values all answer what the rationale claims, and the reason is structural rather than lucky - with the money half now the machine's own closure, no payment value can be silently un-enumerated, and the fulfilment half compares against the single declared default `unfulfilled`.

- **THE MISSING TEST THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT THIS, which is the substantive deliverable. `100.00%` line coverage could not see it because coverage measures lines EXECUTED, not states REASONED ABOUT - the gate executed on every probe and was still wrong about a value nothing drove it with.** Two reasons the cycle-2 pin was structurally unable to catch it, both now fixed: (i) `EligibilityDerivationTests` iterated `LEGACY_STATUS_DIMENSIONS`, five rows, none of which is a refund value (the legacy single status cannot express one - `status_for_payment` returns `None` for both refund values by design), so `partially_refunded` was not in the tested population at all; (ii) its expected set was RECOMPUTED from `PAYMENT_CAPTURED`, the same constant the gate used, so a wrong constant looked right from both sides. The new oracle in `MACHINE_ADMITS` is **hand-written**, not computed, and `test_the_oracle_covers_every_value_the_machine_admits` fails if a new payment or fulfilment value ever appears in the machine's own choice tuples without being enumerated here. The cycle-2 legacy-status pin is kept unchanged beside it.

- **`RefundSeamIntegrationTests` - the two features are now joined by a test, which is what let them drift apart.** Every row in it is produced by the real SPEC-1-05 refund endpoint (mocked gateway, no network), never by an ORM write standing in for it, because that seam writes `payment_status` and NEVER `fulfilment_status` and so manufactures exactly the two rows the returns path must have an opinion on. `test_a_partly_refunded_order_is_still_returnable` refunds 300.00 of a 1200.00 captured order through the real seam, asserts the row lands on `partially_refunded / unfulfilled`, then files the return and asserts **201**, and asserts the return itself still moves no money (`payment_status` unchanged, `refundable_remaining` still 900.00, `Refund.objects.count()` still 1). `test_a_fully_refunded_order_is_refused_and_says_why_it_is_a_policy` refunds the full 1200.00 and pins the sibling **409** as a deliberate policy, so the day anyone widens it, a test says the policy CHANGED rather than the behaviour drifting. `test_a_refund_leaves_a_shipped_order_returnable_whatever_it_did` and `test_the_refund_seam_refuses_the_payment_values_the_gate_also_refuses` (pending / authorized / failed, both endpoints 409) cover the other three answers on real seam output.

- **Mutation evidence, measured in THIS session, each mutation applied and reverted.** (A) Restoring the single-literal money half (`order.payment_status == "captured"`) -> `manage.py test orders.tests_returns`: **FAILED (failures=2) of 89**, and the two are exactly the new probes: `EligibilityDerivationTests.test_every_machine_value_gets_the_answer_this_rationale_claims` breaking on `(payment='partially_refunded', fulfilment='unfulfilled')` and `RefundSeamIntegrationTests.test_a_partly_refunded_order_is_still_returnable`. (B) Removing the no-outgoing-edge filter from the derivation in `state.py` (which widens the set to include `refunded`) -> **FAILED (failures=4) of 89**: the set assertion, the derivation-vs-oracle pin, the all-18 probe on `(refunded, unfulfilled)`, and `test_a_fully_refunded_order_is_refused_and_says_why_it_is_a_policy`. Both halves of the derivation are pinned, and (B) is also the proof that the hand-written oracle is genuinely independent of the gate's constant. Reverted; suite green again.

- **Black, measured by the cycle-2 method, on this cycle's added lines.** `orders/views.py` 49 added / **0 dirty**, `orders/tests_returns.py` 272 added / **0 dirty**, `orders/state.py` 46 added / **0 dirty**. TOTAL **0**. Two of my own lines were Black-dirty on the first pass (a call I had split across three lines that fits in 88, and an `update()` I had split that does not) and are fixed. One honest note on the method: `views.py` carries 49 hunks of PRE-EXISTING Black debt (a missing blank line after the import block at line 31 among them), whose new-side span covers my import line 37; Black does not change my line, and under the cruder "added lines inside a dirty hunk" intersection it would count as 1. I left that pre-existing debt alone rather than reformatting a line this task did not touch, and report the precise figure (0) with the caveat stated, as cycle 2 did.

- **Changelog figure corrected: `views.py` 141 added, not 142.** The cycle-2 line above said 142; `git show --numstat 5e0a4db` measures **141 added / 19 removed**. This is the third figure in this ledger that needed re-measurement (B05 BUG-7, B06 BUG-7, now this), so the correction is recorded rather than silently edited, and the corrected line now says how it was measured. - **Suite, both ends measured in THIS session on this worktree.** Before: **1698 tests, OK (1694 pass + 4 expectedFailure), 100.00% (7950 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0** - the cycle-2 floor, re-run rather than assumed. After: **1706 tests, OK (1702 pass + 4 pre-existing expectedFailure - unchanged, not grown), coverage 100.00% (8059 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0**; delta **+8 tests, +109 stmts**. `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", exit 0 - BUG-5 adds no stored data, the gate only reads columns that already existed. `manage.py test orders.tests_returns` is **89 tests** (81 + 8: eligibility enumeration 4, refund-seam integration 4).

- **MUST-NOT-REGRESS, re-verified in this cycle rather than assumed.** The guest exclusion is still STRUCTURAL (`user=request.user` cannot match a NULL-user row) and the type gate beside it is unchanged - no input-driven bypass was introduced, and `GuestOrderTests` passes untouched. No return path writes `payment_status`, `total_amount`, `refunded_at` or `refundable_remaining`; the widened eligibility gate still only READS, and `MoneyIsUntouchedTests` plus the new integration test both assert it. The partial unique index and its `IntegrityError` -> 409 race handling are untouched. Real JWTs only - `force_authenticate` was NOT used, including for the new finance treasurer, who logs in through `/api/accounts/login/` on a second client. **No capability was widened:** `common/roles.py` and `common/permissions.py` were not opened in this cycle, and the inventory operator still has no returns access - that remains SPEC-1-B07c's job.

- **Scope for this cycle:** `orders/views.py`, `orders/tests_returns.py`, `orders/state.py`, `backend/docs/changes.md`. `orders/state.py` is the only file beyond the four named, and it is in scope: it is the same machine file SPEC-1-B03 scoped and the auditor reads, and BUG-5 could not be fixed without a new machine-derived named value there (clause quoted in the first bullet above). **Deliberately untouched:** `common/roles.py`, `common/permissions.py`, `orders/models.py`, `orders/admin.py`, `orders/urls.py`, every migration, `backend/docs/spec-run-state.md` and `backend/docs/run/`.
## 2026-10-02 - SPEC-1-B07b (Section 1, [R-1.16]) - builder: a customer can list and read their own returns through a real API, and the eligibility window ships unset rather than invented, with SPEC-1-B07d owning the configurable one

Spec 4 line 1045 gives the account a `/account/returns` page and line 1083 puts "Return/refund request where eligible" on it; spec 19.1 line 4687 names the "Return request confirmation" event; spec 17 line 4285 asks for IDOR tests "especially in order, address, return and customer APIs"; spec 6.8 line 1959's "Eligibility validation" is the row that described a window the spec never defines. The requirement: the customer can list and view their returns through a real API, and eligibility is a stated, derived policy rather than a hand-list.

- **THE ELIGIBILITY-WINDOW DECISION (SUPERSEDED - see below; the no-window outcome did not stand).** Options were (a) no window at all and (b) an env-driven `RETURN_WINDOW_DAYS`. B07b chose (a). Evidence, which still stands as the reason for THIS task's action: a whole-file sweep of the 5,734-line spec for `\bday(s)?\b` returns exactly ONE hit, and it is line 276 - "You do not need to deploy every service on day one", about deployment cadence, not returns. Every other eligibility reference (line 1083 "where eligible", 1959 "Eligibility validation", 1899 "Whether the customer is eligible", 1875 "Customer eligibility") describes a PREDICATE over state and names no duration. So any value for `RETURN_WINDOW_DAYS` would have been invented in this task rather than derived from the specification. **SUPERSEDED BY THE PRODUCT OWNER, who requires a configurable site-wide return window; that is ledgered as SPEC-1-B07d and is NOT built here.** Two corrections this cycle makes to how that is recorded: (i) the no-window outcome was a BUILD-ORDER state (nothing to apply yet), not a settled policy, and the four places that read as settled policy - `_return_eligible`'s docstring, config/settings.py's note, .env.example's block, and this bullet - now say so and name B07d; (ii) **the window is a merchant-facing SETTING, not deployment config**, so B07d's number belongs in `ops.SiteSettings`, NOT in env. The reasoning behind that split is that the two are different kinds of value: a return window is per-store policy a merchant chooses, while the page-size keys this task added are a property of the deployment and do not vary per store - which is why those stay env-driven and correct as they are.

- **The no-window STATE is pinned at its boundary, which for an ABSENCE means proving the absence three ways - and these pins describe what ships now, not a policy that will outlive this task.** `ReturnEligibilityWindowTests` drives the real endpoint across five ages - 0s, 1d, 30d, 365d, 3650d - and asserts the SAME answer at every age on BOTH sides: a delivered order from five years ago is still a 201, and a pending order from five years ago is still a 409 (the second half is what makes the first mean something; if age were an input, some of those would flip). `test_the_gate_reads_no_clock_at_all` reads the function's own source and asserts it contains no `timezone`, `timedelta`, `settings` or `now(`, so a window clause fails there the moment it is added rather than after an auditor notices. `test_no_return_window_setting_is_declared` walks `dir(settings)` so the config half cannot drift in quietly. The trade is named in the test: this rejects a docstring that merely MENTIONS a clock, which costs a docstring reword, in exchange for a missed window being loud instead of silent. **Read these as B07d's starting point, not as opposition to it:** they pin what this task's predicate does today, and B07d is expected to change it. `test_no_return_window_setting_is_declared` will keep passing under B07d as designed, because B07d's number is an `ops.SiteSettings` row rather than a config/settings.py key - the two are different kinds of value (per-store policy vs per-deployment shape), which is exactly why they do not share a home.

- **ONE serializer, and the omissions are the contract** (`ReturnSerializerContractTests`, B06's `ShipmentTrackingSerializer` as the pattern). Explicit fields, no `'__all__'`: `id, order_number, status, reason_code, reason_note, created_at, updated_at`. The `order` FOREIGN KEY is NOT exposed - ownership is read off the order and the pk stays a server-side routing key, with `order_number` (spec 8.3's customer reference, `OrderSerializer`'s [R-8.5] identifier strategy) as the handle. NO money rides it: no `total_amount`, `payment_status`, `refundable_remaining`, `currency`, and specifically the serializer never REACHES THROUGH the relation to pull one off the order - `test_no_field_reaches_through_the_order_but_for_its_number` reads every declared `source` and requires each to be a column of the return row itself, except the single `order.order_number` traversal. `internal_note` (B06's leak class) is asserted absent from BOTH the model and the projection. **The leak is prevented structurally, and this task's earlier claim about HOW was wrong and is corrected here.** What was written: the projection test exists "so that if the model grows a customer-visible column, this fails rather than the column silently riding a `fields` list nobody re-read." **Measured repro of that sentence, and it is false:** adding `staff_hint = models.CharField(...)` to `ReturnRequest` and running `makemigrations orders` leaves `orders.tests_returns` at **125 tests, OK, zero failures**. The test iterates `serializer.fields`, and because `Meta.fields` is an explicit allowlist a new model column never reaches it. The true statement: **explicit `Meta.fields` mean a new column CANNOT leak unless somebody edits that list, and the exact-set oracle catches that edit** - `test_the_projection_names_no_field_outside_the_permitted_set` (RENAMED this cycle from `test_the_projection_reveals_nothing_the_allowlist_does_not_name`, a name that oversold it) rejects an unpermitted name the moment it appears in the projection, and `test_the_projection_is_exactly_the_hand_written_field_set` rejects the field set changing at all. Both fire only when the column is added to the projection TOO, which was measured: with the field declared AND added to `Meta.fields`, **failures=3**. So the two-layer guarantee is real; only the claim that the model column alone trips the test was not, and no column-probe test was added to paper over it - a probe that cannot fail is worse than an honest description of the mechanism. Every field is `read_only`, which is what makes "a client can never write a status or a reason" structural rather than a per-view promise.

- **The list is a LIST: `.first()` and unbounded pagination are the two traps this endpoint lives in.** `.first()` would answer "the returns this customer has" with an arbitrary one of them and drop the rest - the exact shape of B06's split-shipment defect. The queryset is `filter(order__user=request.user).select_related("order").order_by("-created_at", "-id")`; the `-id` tiebreaker makes the sort TOTAL so a paginated partition cannot repeat or skip a row. `test_the_pages_partition_the_whole_list_with_nothing_lost_or_repeated` files 7 returns over 7 orders, walks the listing page by page at `page_size=2`, and asserts the union is all 7 ids exactly once - completeness across the partition, which is where an unbounded or mis-sorted listing loses rows. `test_the_listing_carries_every_return_the_caller_owns` is the direct two-row assertion.

- **Pagination follows the existing convention and invents nothing.** The house page-number envelope (`count/total_pages/current_page/next_page/previous_page/results`) and the `Paginator` + `get_page` shape are `order_list`'s (SPEC-9-04 [R-9.2.14]); `get_page` never raises, so a page past the end lands on the last page rather than 404-ing. conventions.md forbids a hardcoded page size, so `RETURNS_HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE` / `RETURNS_HISTORY_MAX_PAGE_SIZE` live in config/settings.py as `_env_int` keys and a `?page_size` caller is clamped to the cap, with non-numeric/non-positive values falling back to the configured default - the same contract `_history_page_size` already implements, deliberately NOT shared: the store may want a denser returns page than order page, and threading two setting names through one helper would read as though the two listings share a knob. `test_the_page_size_is_configuration_and_not_a_literal_in_the_view` overrides the setting and asserts the listing follows it.

- **THE DETAIL ENDPOINT IS THE IDOR SURFACE, and it is probed rather than asserted-in-prose** (`ReturnReadIdorTests`, 9 probes). Ownership is part of the LOOKUP (`get(pk=..., order__user=request.user)`), not a check after the fetch, so a stranger's row is never in the result set and no branch can answer differently for "yours" and "theirs". `test_a_stranger_probing_another_customers_pk_gets_the_baseline_miss` compares `response.content` - BYTES, not status - against the answer for a nonexistent pk, because a body that differs is as good an oracle as a code that differs. 404 and never 403: "forbidden" would confirm the pk is real and is a stranger's. `test_a_stranger_s_own_return_reads_fine_so_the_refusal_is_about_ownership` is the control that stops the probe passing for the wrong reason (a route that refused everyone would satisfy the first test). Anonymous caller 401, forged bearer 401, the B04 guest token opens no door alone and changes nothing when riding a valid account bearer, and a non-integer pk is refused by the router rather than crashing. The list is probed the same way, and a guest order's return row - written directly past the create seam - is asserted unreachable, structurally rather than by a check that could be forgotten: `user=request.user` cannot match a NULL-user row.

- **A declared deviation, and it is the honest one: ONE shipped B07a assertion was updated.** B07a's `test_the_confirmation_carries_nothing_but_the_request_s_own_facts` pinned the create confirmation to an exact 5-key set. B07a built that body inline and left the real serializer to B07b "so there would not be a second body to keep in step" - and this task delivers one representation, so the create view now renders through `ReturnRequestSerializer` and the set is 7 keys (`reason_note` and `updated_at` added; neither is a leak). The assertion was widened to match, which makes it STRICTER (it now pins the list and detail key set too, not just create), and the anti-drift pin for the whole decision is `test_create_detail_and_list_agree_byte_for_byte_on_one_row`. The URL route changed shape for the same reason and it is worth stating plainly: `returns/` is now ONE view dispatching on method, because Django resolves the FIRST matching pattern, so a second `path("returns/", ...)` would have left the listing permanently unreachable behind the create route - a listing that answers 405 to every GET. B07a's POST body, miss contract, type gate, eligibility rule and atomic duplicate guard are otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged; the url name moves from `return-request-create` to `return-requests` (nothing in the repository reversed the old name - verified by grep before renaming).

- **No staff endpoint was added, and that is a decision with its evidence.** Spec 6.8's returns routes are `/admin/returns` and `/admin/returns/:id` - Django admin pages, which B07a already serves behind `returns.read` / `returns.write` ({support, admin}) - and the spec names no staff JSON API for returns anywhere. Inventing one would have widened the API surface past what the spec asks for. `ReturnsSurfaceNotWidenedTests` asserts it rather than promising it: the returns family is EXACTLY the two customer routes, no returns route is mounted in an admin family, and the capability map still reads `CAPABILITY_ROLES` (the source of truth, imported rather than restated) for `{support, admin}`. The inventory operator's access remains SPEC-1-B07c's, exactly as B07a recorded it.

- **No throttle scope on the create seam, and the deferral B07a left is now resolved rather than passed along. The DECISION stands; the REASONING is corrected, because "my test would break" is not a foundation.** The decision is authorization-based, and holds on its own: conventions.md requires a scope on public MUTATING endpoints, this seam is `IsAuthenticated` and never `AllowAny`, so it is not that class of endpoint; and a throttle would buy little specifically, because an anonymous caller cannot reach the seam at all and a caller looping over their OWN orders cannot amplify rows - the partial unique index allows at most one OPEN request per order, so the write is bounded by the number of orders the account owns rather than by the number of requests it sends. **What this task previously recorded, and what was wrong:** the declining reason was that any rate low enough to be worth having "would 429 those probes", which reads as though no rate is worth having. That is false, and it is now recorded as what it actually is - a COST a future scope would carry, not the reason to skip one. The real obstacle to a throttle here is test-shaped: the enumeration probe that covers EVERY payment x fulfilment pair issues ~18 requests inside a single test and `ScopedRateThrottle` keys on user pk, so any rate worth having (the repo's existing ones run 5-120/min) would 429 it. Restructuring that probe - distinct users per cell, or a cache reset between cells - is exactly the work a throttle on this seam would require, and it is tractable. Recorded so the next task reads a decision resting on authorization rather than on a test. (The auditor also noted the repo is already inconsistent here - `create_order` is throttled, `verify_payment` is not - and declined to charge that to this task; it is a separate reconciliation, not a B07b defect.)

- **Mutation evidence, every figure measured in THIS session on the final 125-test module, each mutation applied and reverted.** Dropping `order__user=request.user` from the list -> **FAILED, failures=3** (stranger's-returns, empty-listing, guest-unreachable). Dropping it from the DETAIL lookup -> **FAILED, failures=1** (the byte-identity IDOR probe). Replacing the list count with a one-row answer (the `.first()` trap) -> **FAILED, failures=6**. Dropping the detail miss for a 403 -> **FAILED, failures=1** (same IDOR probe). Setting `read_only_fields = []` -> **FAILED, failures=3** *(CORRECTED in cycle 3: the cycle-2 draft of this bullet recorded failures=1, which did not reproduce when re-measured with this patch verbatim against the final tree; the corrected figure is 3, and cycle 3's re-measurement table carries the command)* (`test_no_field_is_writable...`). Declaring a money field on the serializer -> **FAILED, failures=45**; adding it to the exposed list alone -> **FAILED, failures=1, errors=45** (an invalid field set, so the cascade is a construction error rather than a clean assertion); and the two TOGETHER, which is the only configuration that actually leaks -> **FAILED, failures=4, errors=0** in the serializer class, all four clean failures being `test_no_field_reaches_through_the_order_but_for_its_number`, `test_the_body_carries_no_money_and_no_customer_record`, `test_the_projection_is_exactly_the_hand_written_field_set` and `test_the_projection_names_no_field_outside_the_permitted_set` (the last renamed this cycle; at the time of that measurement it was `..._reveals_nothing_the_allowlist_does_not_name`). Adding a 30-day window to `_return_eligible` -> **FAILED, failures=7**. Hardcoding the page size in the view -> **FAILED, failures=1**. Widening the list filter to admit NULL-user rows -> **FAILED, failures=3**. Baseline after reverting all ten: **125 tests, OK, zero failures.**

- **Suite: floor was 1706 tests OK (1702 pass + 4 xf), coverage 100.00% (8059 stmts), `makemigrations --check` clean - measured before any code was written.** After this task: **1742 tests, OK (1738 pass + 4 expectedFailure - unchanged), coverage 100.00% (8370 stmts, 0 miss), exit 0**; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` "No changes detected", exit 0. **NO MIGRATION: this task adds no model field, no constraint and no index**, which is also why a future return window could be added without one. `orders.tests_returns` is 125 methods (B07a's 89 + 36 new), and the five new classes were measured individually so the mutation figures above have honest denominators: serializer contract 8, list 11, IDOR 9, eligibility window 5, surface-not-widened 3.

- **Formatting, and this bullet's central claim was WRONG and is corrected here.** The original text asserted that intersecting Black's old-side REMOVED line numbers with my diff's added lines "yields an EMPTY set in every file". **It does not, and the measurement is reproducible.** Re-measured against base `6021138` on the final worktree, intersecting at LINE granularity (hunk granularity over-counts, as the auditor noted): `orders/tests_returns.py` 680 added lines, Black-dirty 0 at base and 0 now, **0 dirty added lines**; `orders/views.py` 239 added, dirty 718 → 718 (**unchanged**), **0 dirty added**; `config/settings.py` 24 added, dirty 384 → 388, **2 dirty added lines - 578 and 579, the two new `_env_int` keys**; `orders/serializers.py` 59 added, dirty 173 → 174, **0 dirty added**; `orders/urls.py` 20 added, dirty 45 → 45, **0 dirty added**. So the true statement is: **0 dirty added lines in 4 of the 5 files; `config/settings.py` has 2.** Both are single-quoted where Black would use double quotes, and both deliberately MATCH the adjacent pre-existing `ORDER_HISTORY_*` pair they were modelled on - so the dirt is inherited, not introduced in style. (The serializers.py +1 is on Black's ADDED side only; its removed side is 89 at base and 89 now, and `git diff 6577ce9 -- orders/serializers.py` is empty, so no line of mine sits on either side of that change.) **Chose to RESTATE rather than make the two lines Black-clean, and why:** making them clean means double-quoting them, which would leave them as the only double-quoted pair in that block - trading consistency with the very lines they mirror for a cosmetic claim - and it would not buy `black --check` anyway, since 388 dirty lines remain in that file, all pre-existing. Restating is true, is inside this cycle's declared scope (comment/rationale only for this file), and changes no byte of code. **1,022 added lines across the five files, 0 of them over 88 columns.**

- **Scope for this task, with every file beyond the core three named and justified.** Core, as expected: `orders/serializers.py`, `orders/views.py`, `orders/urls.py`, `orders/tests_returns.py`, `backend/docs/changes.md`. **Beyond the core set, and declared up front as the handoff required:** `config/settings.py` - the returns listing needs a page size and conventions.md forbids a hardcoded one, so `RETURNS_HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE` / `RETURNS_HISTORY_MAX_PAGE_SIZE` are added as `_env_int` keys beside the existing `ORDER_HISTORY_*` pair, and the comment there records why there is deliberately NO return-window key. `.env.example` - documents those two keys in the same block that already documents `ORDER_HISTORY_*`, and records the no-window decision where an operator configuring a deployment will read it. Both were named as "almost certainly required" in the handoff and neither turned out to need anything beyond the two keys plus comments. **Deliberately untouched:** `orders/models.py` (no new field, so no migration), `orders/state.py`, `orders/admin.py`, `common/roles.py`, `common/permissions.py`, every migration, `backend/docs/spec-run-state.md` and `backend/docs/run/`.

- **Named gaps, so they are not mistaken for oversights.** The projection deliberately omits `updated_at`-adjacent staff fields and every money field; `is_open` is NOT exposed even though it is a model property, because it is derived from `status` and a client deriving it against a stale copy of the open-status tuple could disagree with the server. No staff JSON API, for the reason above. The inventory operator still cannot reach returns (SPEC-1-B07c). Spec 6.8's remaining unbuilt returns features - requested quantities, authorization number, disposition, partial refund, manual exception workflow, customer notifications - remain named and unbuilt, exactly as B07a left them; none is a customer read or write surface, so none belongs to this task.


## 2026-10-02 - SPEC-1-B07b - builder, audit cycle 2 of 3: five findings closed, all test-side or prose-side; the product code was correct on every axis the auditor attacked

The auditor's cycle-1 finding was that B07b's **product** code was correct on every axis attacked - IDOR (byte-identical miss plus a control proving the refusal is about ownership rather than a dead route), no 403-vs-404 oracle anywhere, uniform 401s for anonymous and forged callers, the guest row structurally excluded from every listing, 10 `page` and 9 `page_size` abuse shapes clean, projection bytes free of money / `internal_note` / other customers' data, and pagination clamped server-side with no literal in the view. All four deviations were ruled in this task's favour: the one-route method dispatch, the strengthened shipped assertion, no staff JSON API, and the throttle DECISION. **Every finding below was therefore test-side or prose-side, and none of them changed product behaviour.** No file outside the five declared in scope was touched; `git diff` over `orders/models.py`, `orders/serializers.py`, `orders/state.py`, `orders/admin.py`, `common/roles.py`, `common/permissions.py`, every migration and `config/urls.py` is empty.

- **BUG 1 (P2) - a named security control with a VACUOUS pin: the page_size clamp. Fixed, and the fix is proven by the auditor's own inversion.** `test_a_page_size_caller_is_clamped_and_never_unbounded` overrode `RETURNS_HISTORY_MAX_PAGE_SIZE=3` against the two-row `ReturnListTests` fixture, so clamping and NOT clamping returned the identical two rows and the pin could not see the difference it named. Measured repro of the vacuity: changing `views.py:2753` from `return min(value, settings.RETURNS_HISTORY_MAX_PAGE_SIZE)` to `return value` gave **1742 tests, OK, exit 0 - nothing noticed.** Fix: the test now files 3 further returns over 3 further orders so the fixture holds **5 rows against a cap of 2**, and asserts the truncation is observable - `count == 5` (there genuinely are more rows than one page may carry), `len(results) == 2` (the caller's `page_size=10000` was clamped to the cap), `total_pages == 3`, `next_page is True`. Under the same one-token inversion: **125-test module, FAILED (failures=1), exit 1, the pin itself by name** - and after reverting, 125 tests OK again. The generalisable lesson, and the reason this was filed as P2: **a pin whose expected value is recomputed from the constant under test agrees with a wrong value from BOTH sides.** Before writing any mutation evidence, the question to ask is "what would this test do if the guard were absent?" - when the answer is "the same thing", the fixture, not the guard, is the defect.

- **BUG 2 (P3) - the same fixture defect, a different test: the past-the-end page.** `test_a_page_past_the_end_lands_on_the_last_page_rather_than_404ing` ran `?page=99` at the shipped default page size, where the two-row fixture is a SINGLE page - so "landed on the last page" and "silently fell back to page 1" are both `current_page == 1` and the pin was blind to which one happened. The behaviour was always correct; the pin could not see it. Fix: size the page down to one row against the same two rows so the listing is genuinely multi-page, then assert `total_pages == 2`, `current_page == 2`, `previous_page is True`, one row returned. Measured under the fallback inversion (`paginator.get_page(request.query_params.get("page", 1))` to `paginator.get_page(1)`): **FAILED, `AssertionError: 1 != 2`, exit 1.** One honest caveat on that inversion: run across the WHOLE module it does not merely fail but HANGS, because `test_the_pages_partition_the_whole_list_with_nothing_lost_or_repeated` walks pages until `next_page` is false and a resolver that always returns page 1 never terminates it. The evidence above is therefore the single-test run, which is the measurement that isolates the property being pinned.

- **BUG 3 (P3) - a changelog claim that did not reproduce. The claim is corrected; no column-probe test was added to paper over it.** The text claimed the projection test exists "so that if the model grows a customer-visible column, this fails rather than the column silently riding a `fields` list nobody re-read". **Measured, and it is false.** Added `staff_hint = models.CharField(...)` to `ReturnRequest`, ran `makemigrations orders`, then `manage.py test orders.tests_returns`: **125 tests, OK, zero failures, exit 0** - the test iterates `serializer.fields`, and because `Meta.fields` is an explicit allowlist the new column never reaches it. The corrected claim, now what the changelog says: **explicit `Meta.fields` mean a new column CANNOT leak unless somebody edits that list, and the exact-set oracle catches that edit.** Both halves measured in this session: the column alone leaves the module green (above), and the column added to `Meta.fields` TOO gives **FAILED (failures=3), all clean assertion failures and zero errors** - `test_the_projection_is_exactly_the_hand_written_field_set`, `test_the_projection_names_no_field_outside_the_permitted_set (field='staff_hint')`, and `test_the_confirmation_carries_nothing_but_the_request_s_own_facts`. So the two-layer guarantee is real and only the description of it was wrong. The test was RENAMED from `test_the_projection_reveals_nothing_the_allowlist_does_not_name` to `test_the_projection_names_no_field_outside_the_permitted_set`, because the old name sold a model-column detection the test does not perform; its comment now states what it does and does not pin, and names the measurement above. Deliberately NOT done: adding a model-column probe. A probe that cannot fail is worse than an honest description of the mechanism, and the leak here is prevented structurally rather than by a test.

- **BUG 4 (P3) - a Black claim that did not reproduce. CHOSE TO RESTATE rather than make the two lines Black-clean, and here is why.** The Formatting bullet asserted an EMPTY intersection between Black's old-side REMOVED line numbers and the diff's added lines "in every file". Re-measured at LINE granularity (hunk granularity over-counts) against base `6021138` on the final worktree: `orders/tests_returns.py` 680 added / Black-dirty 0 at base and 0 now / **0 dirty added**; `orders/views.py` 239 added / dirty 718 -> 718, **unchanged** / **0 dirty added**; `config/settings.py` 24 added / dirty 384 -> 388 / **2 dirty added, lines 578 and 579 - the two new `_env_int` keys**; `orders/serializers.py` 59 added / dirty 173 -> 174 / **0 dirty added**; `orders/urls.py` 20 added / dirty 45 -> 45 / **0 dirty added**. So the true statement is **0 dirty added lines in 4 of the 5 files; `config/settings.py` has 2** - and **1,022 added lines across the five files, 0 over 88 columns.** Both settings lines are single-quoted where Black wants double quotes, and both deliberately MATCH the adjacent pre-existing `ORDER_HISTORY_*` pair they were modelled on, so the dirt is inherited in style rather than introduced. (The serializers.py +1 is on Black's ADDED side only - its removed side is 89 at base and 89 now, and `git diff 6577ce9 -- orders/serializers.py` is empty - so no line of this task sits on either side of it.) **Why restate rather than fix:** making the two lines Black-clean means double-quoting them, which would leave them the only double-quoted pair in that block - trading consistency with the very lines they mirror for a cosmetic claim - and it would not buy `black --check` anyway, since 388 dirty lines remain in that file and every one is pre-existing. Restating is true, stays inside this cycle's declared scope (comment/rationale only for that file), and changes no byte of code.

- **BUG 5 (P3) - a rationale that generalised falsely. The DECISION is unchanged; the REASONING is rebuilt on authorization.** `_create_return_request`'s docstring declined a throttle on the ground that any rate low enough to be worth having "would 429 those probes", which leaves the reader to conclude that no rate is worth having - and "my test would break" is not a foundation. The recorded reason is now the authorization argument: conventions.md:24 requires a scope on **public** mutating endpoints and this seam is `IsAuthenticated`, never `AllowAny`; an anonymous caller cannot reach it at all; and a caller looping over their OWN orders cannot amplify rows, because the partial unique index permits at most one OPEN request per order, so the write is bounded by the number of orders the account owns rather than by the number of requests it sends. The probe cost is retained but reclassified as what it actually is - **a cost a future scope would carry, not the reason to skip one** - and the fix is named: the ~18-request enumeration probe (all payment x fulfilment pairs, one test, `ScopedRateThrottle` keyed on user pk) would need distinct users per cell or a cache reset between cells. That is tractable, so the next task reads a decision resting on authorization rather than on a test. The auditor separately noted the repo is already inconsistent here (`create_order` throttled, `verify_payment` not) and declined to charge it to this task; recorded, not charged.

- **THE FOUR "NO WINDOW" STATEMENTS ARE CORRECTED, because the product owner has overridden the outcome. They now point at SPEC-1-B07d instead of asserting settled policy.** SPEC-1-B07d owns a configurable site-wide return window and this cycle does not build it - no `ops.SiteSettings` touch, no return-window logic, no migration. What changed is that the no-window outcome is no longer described as a policy that will outlive the task; it is a build-order state, and all four places say so and name B07d: `_return_eligible`'s docstring in `orders/views.py`, the NOTE in `config/settings.py`, the block in `.env.example`, and the changelog's window decision (plus its heading, and the "pinned at its boundary" bullet, which now says these pins describe what ships now and are B07d's starting point). Each carries the honest reason in the same two parts: **the spec states no number** (the whole-file day-count sweep's single hit is about deployment cadence, not returns - that evidence stands, it only justified B07b's action, not a settled policy), and **the product owner requires a configurable window.** Each also records the home that window will live in, which is the part that matters for B07d: **it is a merchant-facing SETTING, so its number belongs in `ops.SiteSettings`, NOT in env** - while the page-size keys added here stay env-driven because they are a different KIND of value (a page density is a property of the deployment and does not vary per store; a return window is per-store policy a merchant chooses). That kind-difference is why they do not share a home, and it is why the existing keys are correct as they stand and were not touched.

- **A failure this cycle caused and caught, recorded because it is the kind that hides.** The first draft of the corrected `_return_eligible` docstring wrote "the page-size keys B07b DID add to config/settings.py", and `test_the_gate_reads_no_clock_at_all` - which reads the function's SOURCE and refuses the tokens `timezone`, `timedelta`, `settings`, `now(` - failed on `token='settings'`. That probe is deliberately narrow and its trade is named in its own comment (a false alarm costs a docstring reword; a missed window is fabricated policy shipping silently), so the fix belonged in the prose, not the test: the docstring now says "beside the deployment config" and names `ops.SiteSettings` (capital S, which the token check does not match). Verified by scanning the function source directly for all four tokens: 0 occurrences of each. Worth recording because the corrected text was *more* accurate in every respect and still broke a test - prose changes inside a probed source string are not free.

Rewritten to parse the function's AST and walk only its executable body (docstrings dropped wherever they are genuinely docstrings), `ast.unparse` that, and apply the token scan to the result. **Both directions are proven by measurement, not asserted:**

  - **CYCLE 3's VERIFICATION PASS: the cycle-3 helper was right about prose and wrong about BEHAVIOUR - it could not see two thirds of the body it was meant to police. CORRECTED AND RE-VERIFIED IN CYCLE 4, including the part of the original claim that was false.** `_executable_source` did `tree.body[0].body[1:]`: it dropped the first statement of the def **unconditionally** instead of dropping the docstring **conditionally**. That is only the same operation when a docstring is present, and where it is not, the scan goes blind rather than strict. Probed by running the helper over **eight** fixtures outside the tree: **(a)** a function with no docstring - `body[1:]` was empty, so a lone `return timezone.now()` scanned as `''` and the clock read was **invisible**; **(b)** a nested function - the nested `def` IS the first statement when there is no docstring, so `body[1:]` removed the nested def **entirely**, and `timezone.now()` inside a nested helper scanned as `''`, **invisible**; both are precisely the shapes a window clause could be hidden in. (c) a docstring-less first statement, (d) a decorator-only def, (e) a comment, (f) a multi-line docstring and (g) a nested def **with** a docstring all behaved correctly. **(h) A THIRD defect showed in the same probe**: a bare string that is **not** first - a SQL literal, a data blob - was scanned as though it were code. **WHAT THE FIX ACTUALLY IS, because cycle 3's version of this bullet overstated it: `_without_docstrings` walks every node and strips a first-position docstring from `body`, `orelse` and `finalbody`, which is what closes (a) and (b) - the two BLIND spots - and nothing else.** It does NOT remove the first-position-only LIMIT, and no test claims it does: `_is_docstring` is a shape test (`ast.Expr` over a string `ast.Constant`) applied to `statements[0]` alone, so a bare string in any later position survives the strip - which is correct, because Python does not treat such a string as a docstring either. **The limit therefore REMAINS, and cycle 4 measured which way it errs rather than arguing it:** a fixture whose first statement is `x = 1` and whose second is a bare string naming all four tokens scans as three lines - `x = 1`, `'settings now( timezone timedelta'`, `return x` - and trips **all four** tokens, while the same four tokens in a **first-position** docstring trip **none** (both run through the real `_executable_source` and `CLOCK_TOKENS`). So what survives is a **FALSE ALARM** - the LOUD direction, which is the one this tripwire is built to afford, since a false alarm costs a prose reword and never lets a window through - and **not** the blind direction. The honest summary is: the docstring-less-function and nested-`def` blindness is fixed; the first-position-only rule stays and is a false-positive cost, not a hole a window clause can hide in. `test_the_clock_scan_reads_the_body_and_not_the_prose` now carries **seven** fixture functions - both directions - so the tripwire's contract is pinned rather than assumed.
  - **BOTH PROOFS RE-RUN ON THE FIXED HELPER, in the tree, not in a probe.** (i) A reachable 30-day clause `window_open = order.created_at >= timezone.now() - timedelta(days=30)` added to `_return_eligible` -> **`FAILED (failures=3)`, exit 1, tripped `timezone`, `timedelta`, `now(`**; `git diff` over `views.py` EMPTY after the revert. (ii) A prose line naming all four tokens inside that same docstring -> **`OK`, exit 0**. Proof (i) is run against a mutation that changes the answer, so it fails the way a real window would; proof (ii) is run against the property B07d actually needs. The scan's own contract is pinned by a new test, `test_the_clock_scan_reads_the_body_and_not_the_prose`, so it cannot silently regress into the text scan: it runs the scan over a prose-only function (must find nothing) and over a body with a real `timezone.now()` (must find something), in both directions, and **calls every fixture as well as parsing it** - a body that never runs is a shape, not a function, and leaving them uncalled put 2 uncovered lines in `tests_returns.py` and dropped the suite to 99.98%. Caught by `coverage report --show-missing` and fixed by executing them, which is why the final figure is 100.00% again.

## 2026-10-02 - SPEC-1-B07d (Section 1, [R-1.16]) - builder: a merchant sets the return window in the admin, and the eligibility gate honours it

One sentence of requirement: a merchant can set, site-wide and without a redeploy, how many days a customer has to request a return, and the gate refuses a request outside that window. **This OVERTURNS B07b's no-window outcome** at the product owner's direction (USER-DIRECTED 2026-10-02); it does not complement it. Nothing else about the seam changed: the machine's derivation is B07a's, untouched, and the window is a second independent refusal bolted after it.

- **Where the number lives: `ops.SiteSettings.return_window_days`, NULL-means-default.** No new model, no new admin, no new route, no env key - `ops/models.py`, `ops/admin.py` and `GET /api/settings/` already existed and are the whole surface. `settings.manage` stays `{admin, superadmin}`; `returns.read`/`returns.write` stay `{support, admin}`; `common/roles.py`, `common/permissions.py` and `orders/state.py`'s transition tables are untouched. `test_only_settings_manage_roles_may_read_or_write_the_window` drives a support operator's POST and gets 403 with the stored policy unchanged, which is the assertion that the new field did not widen anything.

- **DECISION (a) - the default is 30 days, `ops.models.DEFAULT_RETURN_WINDOW_DAYS`, defended twice.** (1) The ecommerce convention: 30 is the window general retail publishes and customers arrive expecting; the FTC's three-day online cooling-off rule is a statutory FLOOR, not the market norm. (2) What this repo's own order lifecycle implies: `orders.state`'s `LIFECYCLE_SEQUENCE` runs pending -> confirmed -> shipped -> delivered, and the gate ADMITS a `captured / unfulfilled` order - paid, nothing dispatched - so any order-date window shorter than the store's packing-plus-courier latency would expire a paying customer's right to ask BEFORE the parcel existed. Pinned twice, from two independent sides: `assertEqual(DEFAULT_RETURN_WINDOW_DAYS, 30)` and a boundary written with the literal `timedelta(days=30)`. Mutation M6 (14 instead of 30) -> **FAILED (failures=12)**.

- **DECISION (b) - the anchor is `order.delivered_at or order.created_at`: delivery where the goods arrived, the order's own date otherwise.** The fallback is not an edge case, it is the PRODUCTION case: `orders/models.py` says of `delivered_at`/`shipped_at`/`fulfilled_at` that "no writer touches them yet", the admin surface's mark_delivered does not stamp them, and `admin_order_fulfill` declines to invent a richer record than the admin surface for the same transition. Every real delivered row in this repo is NULL on `delivered_at`, so a pure delivery-anchored window would refuse every order that has actually been delivered.

- **THE DIVERGENCE, found and executed rather than argued.** The two readings disagree exactly where fulfilment is slower than the window. Order placed day 0, dispatched day 3, delivered day 20; today is day 50. **Order-date reading: refused** - `created_at + 30d < now`, so it ran out on day 30 with 10 days of the customer's ownership unspent. **Delivery reading with the fallback: admitted today**, on the last day of its window. `test_the_window_runs_from_delivery_when_the_goods_arrived` asserts BOTH halves: the chosen answer (201, and `_return_window_anchor(order) == order.delivered_at`) and the rejected one (a hand-written `assertLess(slow.created_at + timedelta(days=30), NOW)`). The chosen form is the GENEROUS of the two wherever they disagree, because `delivered_at` is never earlier than `created_at`. **The unshipped-but-admitted half of the divergence**: `confirmed` is `captured / unfulfilled`, so the gate admits it on the MONEY half alone with no delivery to anchor to, and BOTH readings fall back to the order date there - which is exactly why a 7-day default would have been wrong (`test_an_order_that_never_arrived_is_measured_from_its_own_date`).

- **The retired tripwire, INVERTED not deleted.** `test_the_gate_reads_no_clock_at_all` asserted the ABSENCE of a clock read in `_return_eligible`'s executable body. A window needs a clock, so the pin asserted the opposite of the required behaviour; deleting it would have left "the gate ignores elapsed time" as an untested claim, and rewriting it to assert the tokens are absent-by-name would have bought nothing. It is replaced by `test_the_retired_no_clock_invariant_now_names_what_the_gate_reads`, which asserts the INVERSE - the gate's executable body DOES read `timezone`, the number comes from `SiteSettings`, and neither read comes from `django.conf`. It is deliberately the smaller half of the replacement: the boundary, fork and per-call pins are what prove the window is honoured, because they fail if the tokens are present and the window is not read. `test_the_gate_reads_the_window_on_every_call_and_not_from_a_cache` is the behavioural counterpart - it walks one order through the seam twice and watches the answer change under a patched window, which no source token can do. **(Name corrected in cycle 2: this bullet quoted `...not_from_a_cached_one`, which does not exist in the repo; the shipped name is the one above, at `orders/tests_returns.py:2551`.)** **The `_executable_source` machinery is dismantled by nothing**: the helper, `CLOCK_TOKENS`, `_without_docstrings`, `_is_docstring` and `test_the_clock_scan_reads_the_body_and_not_the_prose` (its 7-fixture contract, both directions) are all retained byte-for-byte and still run.

- **Boundaries written with HAND-WRITTEN literals, never recomputed from the number under test.** Every probe subtracts a spelled-out `timedelta(days=N)` from a fixed aware `NOW = datetime(2026, 10, 2, 12, 0, 0, tzinfo=UTC)` and freezes `orders.views`' clock with `patch("orders.views.timezone.now", ...)`, so "exactly 30 days admitted, 31 refused" is a fact and not a scheduling race. `test_the_window_admits_exactly_thirty_days_and_refuses_the_next` asserts 30 -> 201 and 31 -> 409 over both the HTTP face and `_return_eligible`; `test_an_unset_window_admits_thirty_days_and_refuses_thirty_one` does the same end-to-end with the column untouched; `test_a_configured_window_is_the_one_that_is_enforced` sets 7 and pins 7 -> 201, 8 -> 409 and 11 -> 409, which a build reading the constant directly cannot pass. Boundary is INCLUSIVE (`<=`), and `_within_return_window`'s docstring says so once, next to the operator.

- **Probe (1), the security surface this task adds: a customer cannot steer the window.** `test_a_client_cannot_steer_the_window_through_the_create_body` posts six spellings of the attempt - `return_window_days`, `return_window`, `window_days`, `days`, a nested `site_settings` object, and the baseline with none - against a 10-day-old order with the store's window at 1 day. All six answer 409 with **byte-identical `response.content`** to the attempt that carried no steering parameter at all, and `SiteSettings.load().return_window_days` is still 1 afterwards. The create body reads exactly `_RETURN_BODY_FIELDS`; a parameter that was read would have to change the bytes.

- **Probe (2), no false universals.** `test_the_window_comes_from_the_singleton_and_from_no_other_row`: `load()` is a `get_or_create` on pk=1, so three further reads leave `SiteSettings.objects.count() == 1` and the window at 45. The singleton's `save()` pk=1 guard does NOT cover queryset paths, so the probe then creates exactly the row that guard was meant to prevent, by the one route that skips `save()` (`bulk_create`), confirms the count really is 2, and pins that the window is **still** 45 - the guarantee is "the window comes from the singleton", not "the table can only ever hold one row", and the difference is asserted rather than assumed.

- **Probe (3), no test that cannot fail:** every customer probe authenticates with a real JWT from the login endpoint via `login_as`; `force_authenticate` is used nowhere in this task. The admin probes use `client.force_login`, which is the credential the Django admin session actually uses - the ban is on DRF's `force_authenticate`, which assigns `request.user` directly.
